L1642026-10-12work

The site's builder parses on Python 3.10 again (Quarto's pre-render failed)

After the sessions restarted, the shell's python3 was Anaconda's 3.10, and Quarto's pre-render (python3 ../tools/build.py) stopped: one f-string in build.py held a backslash and a quote inside its expression, which only Python 3.12 accepts. The parameter page's correlation rows build their links in a function of their own (param_links), so the file parses on 3.10. The rendered rows are unchanged: each correlated parameter a link to its entry. An interrupted render had also left site_libs/ and eleven .html files in the site's sources; they were moved to the Bin.

ecosystem/tools/build.py (param_links) · ecosystem/site/_quarto.yml (pre-render) · Python 3.10.9
L1632026-10-12work

Explore stream: replay, the cycles played in order with every view following (R31)

The Explore stream's step 1 of L160 (commit 99a473d, merged); R31's replay (explore/DESIGN.md §2.6).

  • A play/pause button and a speed choice (½, 1, 2 or 4 cycles a second) beside the cycle slider.
  • One control sets the cycle, and the slider, the replay's timer and the sidebar's cycle list all drive it. The traces, the pair, the \(\chi^2\) map, the peak shape, the structure (its scene asked of the core again at each cycle) and the directions follow the same cycle.
  • A replay starts from the start when the end is shown and stops at the end. Nothing animates by itself.
  • Explore ▸ Play / Pause the Cycles, in the menu and ⌘K; the manual says it.
  • The demo page takes &explore=<view>&cycle=k&play=1, for headless checks on a recorded run.

Tested: explore_replay.test.mjs. At the merge, the app's 49 node tests pass (the nophysics guard among them) and the window bundles. Shown headless from a perturbed \(\mathrm{Y_{2}O_{3}}\) start (10 cycles, \(\chi^2\) \(28.0 \to 1.198\)); not yet run in the Mac app.

Next, step 2, two paths compared: what the core must add is filed as E11 (two paths aligned by parameter, scaled by one end's esd, their difference per cycle) before the window draws them.

L1622026-10-12work

App stream, 0.13.0: Start a Refinement from the data and the structure (W2, W3, TU4) on core 0.26.0's drs new

The app stream's step 1 of L160 (commit e6794e9, merged).

Data ▸ Start a Refinement… now offers three starts:

  • a previous control file (W3, as before);
  • the structure, as one CIF per phase;
  • a formula, a space group and a cell (a Le Bail start). The wavelength comes from the data file when it states one, typed (λ₁, λ₂, ratio), or an anode's Kα lines from the core's table; neutrons too.

Who does what:

  • The app's Rust side (modules/start.rs) only arranges the call: it reads the CIFs' text, turns the dialog's values into the core's Known and Instrument, asks start::start, and writes what it gives with Start::write. Nothing is overwritten, nothing is written into a reference folder.
  • ui/start.js parses the dialog's text into numbers and names every missing or wrong value.
  • Before the file opens, a dialog says what the core made: the phases, the free parameters, the background file, the data's range, the peaks and their median FWHM, and every default with its source. Open, or Discard (both files removed).

Tested:

  • a_refinement_started_from_data_and_a_cif, a_refinement_started_from_a_formula_a_group_and_a_cell and nothing_is_started_inside_a_reference_folder (Rust);
  • start.test.mjs (node); the nophysics guard still passes.
  • The built app checked by itself on a copy of Y2O3 (formula, I a -3, 10.60, Cu's lines): the core wrote 1 phase, 101 free background points and 24 peaks (median FWHM 0.1314°, L155's numbers), with 16 defaults listed. The file opened in Refine and the Editor.
  • At the merge: 46 node and 49 Rust tests pass on main.

Found: npm run build needs three.js installed (Explore's dependency), so npm install must come first.

Tutorial stream: the script as data with its checker, the data made by the core, the subtitles; a bug found in drs simulate

The tutorial stream's second step (commit 34eb208, merged), on L160's working answers.

  • tutorial/script.yml and SCRIPT.md: the storyboard as data, in steps of five kinds (run, field, action, wait, mac). Its checks are expressions over a player API that the tour player (TU1) is to provide.

  • Scene 2.1 has its variant in which the app writes the .drc from the structure.
  • tools/make_data.py: the tutorial's data made through the core only, never from manuals/.
  • The published \(\mathrm{Y_{2}O_{3}}\) and \(\mathrm{LaB_{6}}\) structures, through drs simulate (seeds 1–3) and drs new. The same seeds make the same files to the byte.
  • The output goes to tutorial/data/, which git does not keep, with a report. --install copies the folder to ~/Documents/diff-ref-struct.

  • The structures' digits are marked to be checked against the paper before the film.
  • tools/make_srt.py: the subtitles, 112 cues, to be retimed from a take's report.
  • A correction: the storyboard has 26 scenes, not 31 (the stream's miscount, now corrected in L158).
  • Found in the core (TU6): drs simulate never ends when the model's pattern holds NaN. G₁ = 0 gives NaN at every reflection, and the Poisson sampler's rejection loop for λ \(\ge 30\) never accepts a NaN. Reproduced twice; drs calc prints the NaN. Asked of the core: refuse a pattern with NaN, and a Poisson draw of 0 for a non-finite mean. Passed to the core stream with L153's step 2. No film: it waits for the app's tour player (TU1).

L1552026-10-12work

L153, step 1: a refinement started from a data file and a structure (drs new), checked on Y2O3 against FullProf's run

(Logged by the core stream as L154; renumbered L155 at the merge, L154 being the Plots decision.) The core writes a control file from a data file and what is known of each phase, with no previous control file (W2, W3, the tutorial's start). Module start, command drs new.

What the file holds:

  • the data file relative to the control file, the registry's reader, its range;
  • the wavelength the data file states, or one given (--wavelength), or an anode's Kα1 and Kα2 from the core's table at the ratio 0.5 (--anode); without any, drs new refuses rather than guess;
  • one phase per CIF (Rietveld), or per formula, space group and cell (no atoms: Le Bail, every reflection the group allows to 5° past the data, its scale fixed). The cell is given by its free values in the crystal system's order (cubic a; tetragonal a, c; …);
  • the background: arPLS's points (GSAS-II's default smoothness, a point every N/100 of the scan, 101 for Y2O3), in {stem} background.drb, as the file dictionary names it;
  • the widths from the data: W = H\(^{2}\), H the median FWHM of the peaks the data show (24 for Y2O3, H = 0.131°);
  • each Rietveld phase's scale by linear least squares against the data less that background;
  • free: the scale of each phase with atoms and every background point; the rest fixed;
  • every value not given and not read from the data listed with its source (Start::stated) and written as a comment at the head of the file: the polarisation, the peak window and asymmetry limit (the medians of FullProf's files), the profile (TCH, a new phase's), the geometry, the conventions (the papers'), the cycles. Nothing is overwritten.

Tested on Y2O3 (test start). The data with a CIF holding the atoms of FullProf's run (that run does not refine them) and the cell rounded to 10.60 Å; the instrument as the run states it. The file is refined: its own refinement, then zero and cell, then U, V, W, η0, X, then the asymmetry, each to FullProf's Eps 0.2.

  • With FullProf's own 62 background points in its place, it reaches FullProf's minimum: \(\chi^2\) 1.1988 against 1.199, every refined parameter within 1.04 FullProf esd (the bound is 1.2: one esd and the 0.2 esd at which both stop).
  • With its own 101 automatic points, all refined, \(\chi^2\) is 1.1673 (−2.64 %), and five parameters are more than one esd from FullProf's: the scale (+8.39 esd), η0 (+7.48), X (−3.19), W (−2.20), the zero (+1.50). The more flexible background takes up part of the peaks' Lorentzian tails and of their intensity. Frozen in the test (L037), for Josu to accept: the difference is the background's, as the run with FullProf's points shows.
  • The checks (drs check) find no error in the file; Le Bail from a formula, "I a -3" and 10.60 refines (20 cycles, \(\chi^2\) \(44.2 \to 1.27\)); drs new writes byte for byte what start::start gives.

A finding on the polarisation. FullProf's manual writes Lp = (1 − K + K·Cthm·cos\(^{2}\) 2\(\theta\))/(2 sin\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) cos \(\theta\)) with Cthm = cos\(^{2}\) 2\(\theta\)_M (eq. 3.26, p. 53). Without a monochromator 2\(\theta\)_M = 0, so Cthm = 1, the polarisation of an unpolarised beam. The parameter dictionary said "0 without monochromator"; 0 leaves out the angle dependence. drs new takes 1 for X-rays without a monochromator, and the dictionary's text is corrected.

Decided and set aside: the file holds no plan. A plan step frees only parameters the file lists, so a plan would have meant listing every parameter and leaving free more than the scale and background. The order used in the test (zero and cell, the widths, the asymmetry) is the user's to write.

core/src/start.rs, core/src/main.rs (drs new), core/tests/start.rs, core/tests/data/start/y2o3.cif · ecosystem/data/parameters.yml (monochromator_polarisation) · manuals/fullprof/FullProf_Manual.pdf p. 53 · manuals/fullprof/runs/Y2O3 · L153
L1542026-10-12decision

R32: a module for data as graphics, the counterpart of WinPLOTR; a Plots stream of its own; the streams in one table

Josu asked for the counterpart of WinPLOTR, better and modern, with more features (R32, added to the brief). It opens every file WinPLOTR opens and CSV, and several files at once: from different sources, experiments or temperatures. Each file is dimmed at will and displaced to separate it, to build the stacked views and projections of a series that his publications show. WinPLOTR's features are to be studied and improved, not copied.

Decided:

  • A stream of its own, so as not to rub against the others: folder plots/, branch plots, worktree ../20261009_diff-ref-str-plots, requests in plots/REQUESTS.md (PL1 …). It owns app/ui/modules/plots/ once its design is accepted.
  • The name proposed: Plots (Josu decides). It is the broadest, and WinPLOTR's heir.
  • Set aside: Compare (its main use, but it undersells one file), Series (too narrow), Overlay and Stack (each one display mode).
  • Boundaries (plots/README.md):
  • Data (R21) stays the one-file workbench;
  • Refine keeps the Rietveld plot;
  • a figure for a paper composed in Plots goes out through Publish, in LaTeX (L103);
  • every offset, normalisation, sum, conversion or map is the core's (L139).
  • The first deliverables before any code:
  • STUDY.md: WinPLOTR's features with their sources, from the FullProf School deck of December 2015 (46 pages), the \(\mathrm{Tb_{2}Pd_{2}Sn}\) temperature series with WinPLOTR's buffer files, FullProfAPP's pages, and WinPLOTR's manual and paper (Roisnel and Rodríguez-Carvajal 2001);
  • DESIGN.md;
  • mock-ups on the \(\mathrm{Tb_{2}Pd_{2}Sn}\) series.
  • The streams in one table in CLAUDE.md (core, app, Explore, Publish, tutorial, Plots): what each owns, its branch, worktree and request prefix, and what the merging session does to keep them in step.

The core's next work for the cloud sessions: a refinement from data and a structure, the open requests, then version 2's physics

Josu wants the core taken further on the cloud credit (until 5 November 2026). The roadmap's phases are all marked done; the proposal's version-1 features are met in the core. In order:

1. A refinement started from a data file and a structure, with no previous control file (the case of the tutorial, L152, and of W2/W3: a data file opened, something known of the material). The core writes a .drc from:

  • the data file (its format, its range, its wavelength or one given);
  • one or more structures: a CIF each, or a formula, space group and cell;
  • a starting refinement: scale and background free, the rest fixed, the background automatic (arPLS) kept in its file;

  • app 13 (R24 checks beyond the text), 31 (the file dictionary's role in describe()), 32 (FullProf's exports under Exports/FullProf/), and 12, 15, 16 checked against what core 0.2x already does;
  • Explore E7 (the sign convention of mode amplitudes);
  • Publish P15 (U_eq), P17 (the parent cell of a phase refined by coordinates), P18 (bond-valence sums and mean distances in the results file), P9 if open. 3. Version 2's physics, one at a time, each with its FullProf or GSAS-II reference run and the gate rule (L037): P25 anisotropic size and strain, P36 Pawley, P06 joint multi-pattern refinement, P54 sequential and parametric refinement, P55 guided refinement, P05 time of flight, P41 rigid bodies, P30 texture, P23 further background terms. Every step in the core keeps L139: what the app shows is computed here.

L1512026-10-12decision

Josu: a Modules menu in the menu bar, every module with its own actions

Josu proposed a menu-bar entry for the independent modules, Explore first: it follows a refinement, and its actions were in the menu bar only while Explore itself was the active module.

Decided and done (APP_CONVENTIONS §3):

  • A Modules menu, always there, between View and the active module's menu. Each module (Data, Refine, Editor, Explore) is a submenu: Show the module (⌘1 …), then all its actions.
  • Each copy runs its command and is enabled and disabled as the command is. The menu bar keeps the copies in their own list, refreshed with the rest.
  • The active module keeps its own menu beside it, as Xcode keeps its context menus. The toolbar already showed the active module's actions. Set aside: replacing the active module's menu by the Modules menu. That puts every action one level deeper for the module in use.

Checked in the running app (0.12.4, the self-check): the Modules menu holds 84 commands of 4 modules, 12 of them Explore's. With Refine active, "Show the Path of this Refinement" is enabled there. menus.test.mjs guards it. The app's 42 node tests pass.

L1502026-10-12finding

The Mac slowed down with the app open: measured, and two loads cut (the \(\chi^2\) map's processors, the 3D view's GPU)

Josu: with the app open the Mac seems stuck. Measured on his MacBook Pro (Intel i9-9880H, 8 cores and 16 logical processors, 16 GB, Intel UHD 630 and AMD Radeon Pro 5500M with automatic switching).

Measured, and not the cause:

  • idle with a Y2O3 document open: the app's process 70 MB, its window (WebKit) 101 MB, 0 % CPU after start-up;
  • during and after a refinement through the self-check: 86 MB and 127 MB; the CPU back to 0 when the run ends;
  • the window has no animation loop or timer: it redraws only on change and on resize.

Two loads found in the code:

  • Explore's \(\chi^2\) map computed as many patterns at once as the Mac has logical processors (16), at normal priority, so for the whole map nothing else ran well.
  • The 3D structure view created a WebGL context with the default power preference, which on a two-GPU Mac switches to the discrete GPU (heat, fans, pauses while it switches). It kept the context, and an extra copy of each frame (preserveDrawingBuffer), until the tab was closed, even with another view shown.

Changed:

  • The core's parallel work takes half the logical processors (lsq::worker_threads: 8 here).
  • The app runs the map in a thread of its own at macOS's "utility" QoS. The workers inherit it, so the system serves the user first.
  • Measured with a_grids_load_when_asked (\(41 \times 41\) points of Y2O3): 740–800 % CPU (8 processors) and about 11 MB, 1681 points in 24.6 s.
  • The 3D view asks for the low-power GPU and keeps no copy of the frame. It lets its context go on leaving the Structure view (made again on return, from the scene already in hand), and is made once however many redraws ask for it.
  • The rule is in APP_CONVENTIONS §9 and app/ui/tests/load.test.mjs.

Not found here: the Mac had swapped before the app ran (529 151 pageouts since start-up), and these sessions' own builds, tests and renders (cargo on all cores, headless Chrome, Quarto) also load the same Mac. Which of the app's views Josu had open when it stalled is to be told by him: if it was neither the \(\chi^2\) map nor the structure, the next step is to measure that view.

L1392026-10-12decision

Josu: every calculation is the core's, a must; the app only shows. Explore's path in esd units moved to the core

L138 kept the traces' (q − q_end)/\(\sigma\)_end in the window as "a unit of the drawing". Josu: "I want it in the core; I want any calculation in the core, the app only shows, this should be a must!!"

The rule is written where every session reads it:

  • APP_CONVENTIONS.md §0: every number the app shows that is derived from data, a model or a result is computed by the core, tested there, and sent ready to show. The window only turns numbers into pixels and text. A calculation found in the window is a fault.
  • CLAUDE.md's description of app/. Set aside: any reading of a computation as "just display"; L138's decision is withdrawn.

Moved to the core now:

  • lsq::Trajectory::in_esd (PathInEsd): for the start and each cycle, each parameter's (value − end)/\(\sigma\)_end and the cycle's shift/esd; each parameter's reach along the path; the end's values and esd's.
  • lsq::pair_span: the \(\chi^2\) map's span for "the path".
  • lsq::Cycle::largest_shift: the cycles' table's largest |shift|/esd. The app's Rust side had recomputed it. The app's Rust side sends the path so far with every cycle event, and the whole path with the run's final esd's with the result. Each parameter's tuple carries the core's shift/esd. The window looks these up for Explore's traces, its table's "shift / esd" and "(value − end)/esd_end" columns, the inspector's path, the map's span (explore_pair_span), and Refine's path caption and steering header.

A fault found on the way: the window took a run's start from the document's values. After the run those are the refined values, so every trace started at 0. The core's start is the model the run started from: in the recorded Y2O3 run, cell a starts 407.6 \(\sigma\) from its end.

Tested:

  • the_path_in_units_of_the_esd_at_its_end (core, three cycles of case A): every distance against the cycles' own numbers, the end at 0, the given esd's, pair_span, largest_shift;
  • the app's refine test: the events' paths and the result's;
  • nophysics.test.mjs: no (q − q_end)/\(\sigma\), shift/esd or span computed in Explore or Refine. The recorded run's cell a at cycle 1 is −462.003 \(\sigma\) from the core, the same as the old formula. The core's 185, the app's 43 Rust and 32 node tests pass; the app bundles.

L1382026-10-12work

Item 6 of L125's second group: Data's d-spacings and Explore's covariance ellipse come from the core; the traces' scaling stays a unit of the drawing

L125 found physics computed in the window, which the app must not do.

Data's d-spacings. The inspector computed each peak's d by Bragg's law in JavaScript. It also ignored a wavelength taken from a control file (W7), so such a file showed no d-spacings.

  • The core gains data_ops::to_axis(from, to, x, λ): one value converted between 2\(\theta\), d and Q. The scan conversion convert now uses it, so there is one rule.
  • The app's peak search returns each peak's d from it, with the scan's λ or the one taken from a control file. Taking another λ searches again.
  • Tested: one_value_converted_by_braggs_law (core): Cu Kα₁ at 2\(\theta\) = 28.60° is d = 3.1186 Å, Q and back, no λ is an error, sin \(\theta\) > 1 has no image. In peaks_and_background_come_from_the_core (app), Y2O3's 222 peak has no d without λ, and with λ = 1.5406 Å its d is λ/(2 sin \(\theta\)) = a/√12 within 0.005 Å.

Explore's covariance ellipse. The pair view and the \(\chi^2\) map found the ellipse's axes and angle in JavaScript, from the \(2 \times 2\) covariance.

  • The core gains lsq::covariance_ellipse(σa, σb, r): the eigenvalues λ₁ \(\ge\) λ₂ of C = [[σa\(^{2}\), rσaσb], [rσaσb, σb\(^{2}\)]] and the angle of the first axis, the same closed form.
  • Explore asks for it through explore_ellipse, once per run and pair, and draws what comes back. The figure export draws it too.
  • In a browser, without the app, no ellipse is drawn.
  • Tested: the_covariance_ellipse_of_a_pair (core: no correlation; r = \(\pm\)0.9 at \(\pm\)45°, the angle taken modulo π; λ₁λ₂ = det C) and the_pairs_ellipse_is_the_cores (app, the recorded run's Caglioti V and W, r = −0.871). The pair view drawn with that ellipse shows the 1\(\sigma\) and 2\(\sigma\) contours leaning with the negative correlation; given no ellipse, it draws none.

app/ui/tests/nophysics.test.mjs keeps both out of the window: no Bragg's law in Data, no eigen-decomposition in Explore. It fails on the previous files. The core's 183 tests (184 with the new one), the app's 43 Rust and 31 node tests pass; the app bundles.

Decision: the traces' (q − q_end)/\(\sigma\)_end stays in the window. It divides the core's own numbers (a value, its value at the end, its esd at the end, all from the cycle events) to put every parameter on one axis. It makes no quantity of its own, and is of a kind with the plot's √ and log axes, which the window also draws.

  • Set aside: a core function sending each trace already scaled with every cycle. It doubles what every cycle event carries, for one subtraction and one division.
  • Josu can overrule this: the move is small.

  • in a 1280 px window: A pair 543 px, \(\chi^2\) map 710 px, Structure 259 px, Peak shape 170 px.

Now the tabs keep their line, and the controls have a bar of their own under them, which wraps onto further lines when the view is narrow:

  • a selector is at most 200 px wide, with a long name cut short in it and whole in its list;
  • the title takes what room is left and ends in "…". Measured again at 1500, 1280 and 1024 px, in all six views: nothing past the edge (at 1024 px the \(\chi^2\) map's bar takes four lines).

Also fixed in Explore's list of runs: the cycle count ran into the control file's title ("…PCR file)8 cycles"). It is now on its own line.

L1362026-10-12work

Item 4 of L125's second group: Refine names parameters by the dictionary, not by their address

The core names each parameter by its address in the model ("phase1.cell_a", "pattern1.background_points.12", "phase1.Y.atom_position.x"). Refine's bottom panel printed those addresses as they came.

Now each address has a name for the window, built by the app's Rust side from the core's own data:

  • field_of finds the value the address names, dictionary_id gives its dictionary entry, and the dictionary gives the name;
  • then come the component and the place: "Cell a", "Atom position x (Y)", "Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry P2", "Background at 15.48°" (a background point by its 2\(\theta\), not its index);
  • the phase is added when the model has several;
  • a name that is no address (a variable of the user's) is kept. The names go with every cycle, every result and the steering view (labels).

The window shows them everywhere a parameter appeared by its address:

  • the Parameters list;
  • the correlations and the correlation map's pointer;
  • the cycles' largest shift;
  • the parameter paths;
  • the combination a singular matrix cannot fix;
  • the Free parameters tab;
  • the steering inspector, its links and variables. The core's sentences (steering notes, why a run stopped) are put in the same words, the longest address first. The address stays the parameter's identity (fix, free, the control file) and shows on hover. In the panel the names are in the text font, on one line, and a long name gives way to the value(esd), which is never cut.

Tested:

  • refine_accept_discard_and_stop: the names of a Y2O3 run (Cell a, Zero shift, P2, a background point, the atoms Y:2 and O), no address left in any of them, and the same names on the cycles;
  • the_cores_sentences_are_put_in_words;
  • import_writes_the_control_file_and_opens_it: no outline label is an address;
  • app/ui/tests/labels.test.mjs: labelOf, and that refine.js and outline.js print an address only on hover (the check finds 7 bare addresses in the previous refine.js). A recorded run, rendered in headless Chrome, lists "Zero shift 0.0028(3)" and "Background at 15.48° 19.1(4)", and its notes read "Pseudo-Voigt η₀: the step shortened to Pseudo-Voigt η₀'s maximum shift". The app's 42 Rust and 27 node tests pass, and it bundles.

Item 3 of L125's second group: an unnamed phase is proposed as \(\mathrm{Y_{2}O_{3}}\), the core's formula, not "YO"

When a .pcr leaves its phase unnamed (FullProf's "phase 1:"), the app proposes a name (W7). It joined the element symbols in the file's order ("YO"). L1342026-10-12work

Item 2 of L125's second group: File ▸ Export ▸ Plot writes the plot in view as PDF, SVG or PNG

APP_CONVENTIONS §8 asks that every plot can be exported as PDF, SVG and PNG. Until now only the clipboard copied a PNG of the screen.

Now File ▸ Export ▸ Plot… works in Data, Refine (its plot view) and Explore (every view):

  • The plot draws itself once more, with the same code that draws it on screen, into an SVG recorder (canvas2svg 1.0.16, MIT, vendored by tools/vendor.js). Its legend goes at the top. A module offers this through figure(), and the command is enabled only where a module has one.
  • The figure is always drawn in the light palette, on white, whatever the window's appearance; the window redraws in its own appearance straight after.
  • The app's Rust side (src-tauri/src/figure.rs) writes the SVG as it is, or converts it: to PDF with svg2pdf 0.13, to PNG at 300 dpi with resvg 0.45. Text uses the Mac's system fonts. The save dialog proposes <project>/Exports/<name>.pdf.
  • Every write passes the app's one guard: nothing goes into a reference folder.
  • Explore's figures:
  • traces and pair: with their legend;
  • \(\chi^2\) map;
  • peak shape: its two panels side by side;
  • directions;
  • the 3D structure: as an image.

Four faults found while checking the exports, each fixed:

  • canvas2svg has no dashed lines: they are recorded as stroke-dasharray.
  • Its clip() wrote an empty outline, so Refine's difference strip vanished. The outline is now written first.
  • Its cleared areas were always white: they take the plot's background.
  • A series kept the colour it was created with. In a dark window Ycal was white and did not show on paper. A series now names its colour ("--calc"), and the plot looks it up each time it draws. All 19 colours in Data and Refine were changed.

Checked:

  • Data, Refine, Explore's traces and pair were exported from a window in dark mode (headless Chrome), then turned into PDF and PNG by the app's own code and looked at: every curve, the difference strip and the legend show on white.
  • figure.rs tests: SVG written as is; a %PDF file; a \(400 \times 300\) figure at 300 dpi is a \(1250 \times 938\) PNG; .jpg refused; nothing written into a reference folder.
  • The app's 41 Rust and 24 node tests pass, and it bundles.

Correction to L125. L125 says tools/vendor.js exits 0 when a package is missing. It exits 1. The failure was hidden by the command I ran the build through (its output piped into grep, which reported the pipe's status, not the build's). The rule stays: after every build, the bundle's date and version are checked.

Item 1 of L125's second group: one tick rule for every plot; √ and log axes with round labels; Explore's cycle axis thinned

Three faults with one cause, two tick rules:

  • the √ and log axes placed their ticks in transformed units and printed the back-transformed values with three decimals ("2500.000");
  • Explore had its own copy of the tick function, which had drifted from the shared plot's;
  • Explore's cycle axis labelled every cycle.

Now there is one rule, app/ui/ticks.js, used by the shared plot (Data, Refine) and by Explore's views (traces, pair, \(\chi^2\) map, peak shape):

  • axisTicks: the linear axis, with the common factor and offset in the axis label and ticks thinned by measured width;
  • sqrtTicks: round counts (0, 2000, 4000…) placed on the √ scale, thinned where they crowd;
  • logTicks: decades, with 2× and 5× when fewer than three show, and 10ⁿ for long numbers;
  • labelStride: Explore's cycle axis labelled every k-th cycle, always including the last. Tested in app/ui/tests/ticks.test.mjs (round √ labels at least 26 px apart, decades, factor and offset, no touching labels, the cycle stride). Rendered: Data's plot on its √ axis reads 0 … 12000 and on its log axis 10 … 10000. The app's 24 node tests pass and it bundles.

L1292026-10-12work

Fix 4 of L125: the control file's Cycles decide a run; Settings' value is only a default for a file that states none

Every run sent Settings ▸ Cycles to the core when it was filled, and the core took it before the control file's [refine] cycles. So a value typed once in Settings silently overrode the Cycles field in the toolbar and in the file. APP_CONVENTIONS §4 makes Settings' refinement values defaults, not overrides.

Now the decision is one pure function, app/ui/cycles.js (runCycles), used by Refine:

  • the control file's Cycles decide;
  • Settings' value (now labelled "Default cycles", with a tooltip saying when it applies) is sent only when the control file states none;
  • a plan's step brings its own;
  • "cycle k of N" shows the same number. Tested in app/ui/tests/cycles.test.mjs: the file's 15 wins over Settings' 5; Settings' 5 applies to a file with none; with neither, 10; a plan's step ignores Settings. The app's 12 node tests pass and the module bundles.

L1172026-10-11work

App 0.11.0: the refinement paused and resumed with the engine's state, every stop the core's reason; W12's steering, restraints and plan in the window

Main merged (app 0.10.0, the Explore design, L112). App 0.11.0 is built on core 0.25.0 (its lsq::Session, steering and plan are core 0.24.0's); the app's requests 23–30 are closed.

  • A run is the core's session (W11, requests 23, 24). The window's run takes the lsq::Session out of the document and runs it without the documents' lock, then puts it back. A paused session stays in the document.
  • Pause (⌥⌘.) pauses after the cycle in progress; Resume (⌃⌘R, or Run) continues with the engine's state. Stepped 2 cycles and resumed, Y2O3 ends where the uninterrupted 6-cycle run ends, to the bit.
  • Step n continues a paused run. Stop ends a run in progress after its cycle, or a paused one where it is.
  • Found and fixed: until 0.10.0 a run held the documents' lock for its whole length, and Stop took the lock with try_lock, so it could not reach a run in progress. The stop and pause flags now live outside that lock.
  • Every stop is said from the core's code in plain words: converged, a minimum, the cycles out, diverging, singular (with the combination, why and what to do), stopped by you, paused.
  • Each cycle shows its λ, the core's time, the refused steps and the steering's notes.
  • During a pause what is free and how it is steered can change; the session takes it up on Resume with its damping restarted, as the core notes. A value cannot be set until Accept or Discard.
  • W12 in the inspector (requests 25–28). Every number is the core's (ParameterSet::describe, the control's refinement); every change is written to the control file by the core and is one undo step.
  • Steering: λ's start and Marquardt; each class's damping; each free parameter's last value, esd, shift and shift/esd, damping, and per value its bounds, behaviour at a bound, maximum shift and link.
  • Links and new variables, added, edited and removed.
  • Restraints: target, value, \(\Delta\)/\(\sigma\), weight, share of \(\chi^2\), and their part (Sr-ox: 8 restraints, 0.028 of \(\chi^2\) 2.09).
  • The plan: steps resolved to the parameters they free, run one at a time. Each accepted step becomes a version that names the step, and the next step becomes the one to run.
  • Request 29: the background file of a data file alone is the file dictionary's "{stem} background.drb", named by the core (L116). Request 30: the forbidden reflections are the core's diagnostics::forbidden_in.
  • Tests. The app's tests now build and run on Linux, so a cloud session can run them: WebKitGTK's development packages, and the title-bar style set on macOS only. 32 pass, five of them new:
  • pause and resume against the uninterrupted run;
  • a change during a pause;
  • the steering kept in the control file and read back;
  • the plan run step by step;
  • the restraints' shares.
  • The window, checked in Chromium. It was opened from the test's demo files (a run stepped two cycles and paused, with a plan, a damping and a link) and drawn without a page error. For Josu to try on the Mac before the next step.

L1152026-10-11work

The Explore stream's requests E1–E7 met: a refinement's trajectory kept and persisted, \(\chi^2\) on a grid, a state's bundle, the 3D scene, the eigen-spectrum, an irrep's displacement, the sign convention of mode amplitudes

explore/REQUESTS.md E1–E7, on case A (test explore_requests).

  • E1, the trajectory. Each cycle now carries:
  • its covariance (the correlations at every cycle, lsq::correlations_of);
  • the spectrum of the scaled normal matrix;
  • its time. lsq::Trajectory (a session's trajectory()) answers state_at(k), the model after k cycles rebuilt from the start and each cycle's applied shifts. Over three cycles of case A it is the engine's model to the bit. The results file writes every cycle (\(\chi^2\), R's, λ, the singular directions, the time; each parameter's value, shift, esd and computed shift) to the last bit and reads it back. Trajectory::from_end, given the control file written there and those cycles, rebuilds the states within 3.5·\(10^{-18}\). drs refine --trace <file.json> writes the record (results::trace_json).
  • E2, \(\chi^2\) on a grid. lsq::chi2_grid computes the points from coarse to fine (the centre and the corners first: coarse_to_fine), on every thread in batches, with a progress callback that can stop it. drs refine --map a b [--span 3esd] [--grid n] drives it. On \(5 \times 5\) of \(\Gamma\)₄⁺ and X₃⁺ about the converged case A (\(\pm\) 1 esd), the minimum is at the centre and the rest is the normal matrix's quadratic form within 2.3 %. drs refine on case A with its cycles and a \(5 \times 5\) map ran in 0.23 s here.
  • E3, a state's bundle. state::summary (and drs state, at any --cycle k) returns in one call:
  • the cell, the volume and the strain against a reference cell;
  • FWHM, η, the unclamped H_G\(^{2}\) (negative where U, V, W make it so) and H_L over a 2\(\theta\) range;
  • the reflections, and a reflection's own profile with the background apart (reflection_profile);
  • the contacts by atom, operator and translation with su's, and the valence sums;
  • the modes and irreps with su's, the breadths and the restraints. state::plausible gives the bond-valence single-bond distance d₀ − B₀ ln(V/N) for chosen N and the sum of ionic radii. Sb\(^{5}\)⁺–O (N = 6) is 2.0095 Å against case A's 1.9788; Sc\(^{3}\)⁺–O is 2.1055 against 2.0746.
  • E4, the scene. geometry::scene (and drs structure --cartesian) returns:
  • the images in a box, in fractions and Cartesian Å, each named by atom, operator and translation;
  • the bonds by rule (ionic radii, a distance, or element pairs);
  • the polyhedra as faces of the convex hull with volume and Baur's distortion;
  • the cell's edges;
  • each irrep's displacement on each image, with the reference positions. Case A's Sb1 octahedron has 8 faces and 10.3305 Å³, against 10.3314 for the regular one of its mean bond.
  • E5. check::directions returns the whole spectrum of the scaled normal matrix with its vectors, sorted. It is the first cycle's spectrum at the start.
  • E6. Modes::displacement_of(amplitudes) and irrep_displacement(irrep). The irreps' displacements add up to the whole.
  • E7, the signs. Modes::equivalent_amplitudes applies every operation of the parent group, with every translation of its lattice, in the phase's basis. It compares the moved structure with the reference and projects it on the modes. Case A has 24 equivalent sets, one per domain. The Explore stream's mirror solution (\(\Gamma\)₄⁺ +0.8286, the X₅⁺ pair reversed) is the same structure by the parent's −x,−y,z. \(\Gamma\)₁⁺'s sign is never reversed. same_structure says whether two sets are one structure; nearest_to picks the set nearest a reference, the convention. AMPLIMODES's own FullProf block for case A is in its P2₁/c setting, whose X2+ sign and GM5+/X5+ bases differ from this phase's, so a reference must be in the phase's basis, as the analysis the control file's modes come from is.

L1142026-10-11work

The Publish stream's requests P2–P11 met: the results file and the CIF complete, the positions and bonds of the refined state with their su's, charges, one su rule, a phase's name, the parent cell

publish/REQUESTS.md P2–P11, each against FullProf where it prints the number (test publish_requests).

  • P2, the results file. R_F per phase; Rp, Rwp and Rexp without the background, as FullProf's "conventional" R's (manual p. 44: yᵢ − bᵢ for yᵢ in the denominators). The net counts are summed with their signs, as the manual writes and FullProf sums them. LaB6 gives 26.67/33.84 against FullProf's 26.7/33.7; with |yᵢ − bᵢ| it would be 23.9. Y2O3 gives 10.91/14.86 against 10.9/14.9. Also written: the mode amplitudes and the irreps' with su's, the reference structure, the cell reduced to the parent's, _refine_ls_shift/su_max, and _refine_special_details (how the su's were obtained, \(\chi^2\) included). The reflections' 2\(\theta\) with the zero and the displacement were already there since 0.23.0 (_drs_refln_2theta).
  • P3, a mode phase's positions and distances. The positions are reduced to [0, 1) and propagated through the amplitudes' covariance (module geometry, one Propagator for every su). Case A's Sb1–O11 is 1.9777(16); FullProf's Bond_Str prints 1.9778(17). Bond_Str reads the CFL FullProf writes, whose positions are rounded to their esd's digit and whose esd's are rounded too (O11's x, 0.000257, printed "(3)"). From that CFL the core gives 1.97777(170), FullProf's print to the digit. The core's own value at the unrounded positions differs from it by 5.7·\(10^{-5}\) Å, less than the 4.4·\(10^{-4}\) Å the CFL's rounding can move it.
  • Bond_Str on case A. The test bond_str now also reads case A's two .dis files. All 132 distances agree. 40 of their standard deviations stand above Bond_Str's own formula applied to the CFL's esd's, by up to 0.32 of a last digit beyond the print's half unit (Sb1–O11_2 0.0016 where the formula gives 0.00152). Neither the unrounded esd's nor one factor reproduces them: unexplained, counted and frozen, listed for Josu.
  • P4, bonds from the refined state. drs bonds on a .pcr or control file (--cycles n) gives the same numbers as the CIF.
  • P5, charges. charge = q on an atom in the control file (an integer from −8 to 8, not 0), or drs bonds --charges Y=3,O=-2. The species keeps its scattering. Y2O3's sums: Y 2.94, 2.95; O 1.96.
  • P6, one su rule (Josu's decision, L116). cif_write::with_su rounds the su up (IUCr, Rec. 12), keeps the 2–19 range, and rounds the value half away from zero: 2.2865 \(\pm\) 0.0032 is 2.287(4), case A's b 5.68003(11). It is now used everywhere a su is printed: the CIF, the results file, drs bonds on a CFL (FullProf's print only under --fullprof) and the weight fractions. The observed counts' \(\sigma\) in the results file keep four figures, so that the weights read back. That is a datum, not a result's su.
  • P7, nothing on fixed values. A value the symmetry fixes has no gradient, so it is written without su: Y2O3's O–Y1–O at 180°. On a CFL the angle had been 180.0000(9), the arc cosine's rounding; it is now taken by atan2 in Cartesian axes and is exact at 0° and 180°. Each bond is listed once, with _geom_*_publ_flag.
  • P8, the CIF's items. Profile, background and preferred-orientation functions, constraints, _refine_ls_R_Fsqd_factor, weighting, _refine_ls_shift/su_max, _pd_block_id with the diffractogram's, _computing_*. Labels are unique (Y1, Y2).
  • P9. drs modes --amplimodes prints AMPLIMODES's summary per irrep.
  • P10, a phase's name. A one-word name of its own is kept; otherwise the formula with the elements by increasing electronegativity (IUPAC Red Book 2005, IR-4.4.2): Y2O3, Sr2ScSbO6. The phase's CIF is "a_modes Sr2ScSbO6.cif".
  • P11, the parent cell. With the parent known (AMPLIMODES's analysis or the control file), case A reduces to 8.04299(11), 8.04299(11), 8.02621(15) Å, 89.964(3), 90.036(3), 90.1442(15)° (at the file's values). The committed results file of case A and GSAS-II's reading of it are regenerated. GSAS-II reads the same cell and atoms; their coordinates now sum differently, being reduced to the cell.

L1132026-10-11finding

FullProf's half step on the cell traced: a factor of FullProf's own, ½ with the pseudo-Voigt and ¼ with TCH, reproduced cycle by cycle; L109's freeze re-read without the cell

L111's run (shift_control/n3: Y2O3 converged, a, b and c moved by 3 esd, one cycle) traced in the derivatives.

  • Not the reciprocal metric. A Gauss–Newton step is the same to first order whether it is taken in a or in A = 1/a\(^{2}\): the core's step in a, 2.95 esd back, is what a step in A gives. The cell's codeword multiplier is 1. So neither explains FullProf's 1.44 esd.
  • A fraction of FullProf's own. Replayed in the core as a damping of the cell's parameters alone, the fraction that gives FullProf's cell is 0.488 in n3: with ½ the core's a is 0.034 esd from FullProf's, and every other parameter moves as the core's within 0.088 esd. Far from the minimum (m10, a 130 esd off) the core's half step gives a = 10.601563 Å, FullProf 10.601567.
  • ¼ with Thompson–Cox–Hastings. LaB6 over its 9 cycles and rutile–anatase over its 12 follow FullProf's cell at every cycle with the cell's step at ¼, within 2.7·\(10^{-6}\) and 2.6·\(10^{-5}\) Å (the whole step: 7.7·\(10^{-5}\) and 5.3·\(10^{-4}\)). There FullProf also prints the cell's esd at half of √(C_kk \(\chi^2\)) (L053): it behaves as if the cell's derivative were doubled, which halves the step once more.
  • Egypt's five TCH phases refined together are not reproduced by one fraction: 10 of its cell values follow FullProf better with ½, 5 with ¼. Counted. The factor is not in FullProf's manual. The core keeps the whole Gauss–Newton step as its default (it converges in fewer cycles to the same minimum, fullprof_refinements); a user who wants FullProf's path damps the cell (damping in [refine], W12). Counted and frozen in the test fullprof_cell_step, listed for Josu.

L109's freeze re-read. Its factor 2.00 on a was this half step. The cell is now left out of the first cycle's comparison in steering: 5 of the 6 other parameters differ by more than 10 % from the far start, by up to 4.29 (U 0.975, V 1.919, W 4.286, X 1.577, Zero 2.033, η 0.291), and near the minimum (n3) the same parameters move as FullProf's within 0.09 esd. So what remains of L109 belongs to the far start's non-linearity, not to the step.

L1102026-10-11work

Core 0.24.0: the refinement run as a session (W11) and steered from the control file (W12), with a plan of steps

W11, the engine (lsq::Session).

  • step(n) runs n cycles and pauses; run goes on to the end; between calls the session keeps the model, λ, the cycle count, the history and the count of rising cycles.
  • set_value and set_parameters during a pause restart λ, and the notes say so.
  • to_text and resume save and restore the state for scripts: every value moved, the variables, λ, the count and the history.
  • Every stop is data (Stop, with a stable code and a text): converged; minimum; cycles; diverging by \(\chi^2\) rising, a jump of many esd's or an impossible value (Divergence); singular, with the combination, its eigenvalue and the dictionary's why and tip (check::null_direction, shared with drs check); the user.
  • Each cycle gives, for every free parameter, its value, esd, shift, shift/esd and computed shift; and λ, the steps refused with their λ and \(\chi^2\), the restraints with their weight (c/\(\sigma\)²) and contribution to \(\chi^2\), and notes.
  • refine and refine_until are sessions run to the end, as before.

W12, the steering, all in the control file and checked as it is read (module steering):

  • in [refine]: lambda, marquardt, damping by class, divergence (rising, jump, jump_from, singular), steering (damping of a parameter, maximum shift of a value), and behaviour on limits;
  • the tables [[refine.link]] (value = coefficient × follows + offset), [[refine.variable]] (name, value, terms) and [[refine.step]] (the plan: name, free, damping, cycles, convergence);
  • the reader's checks: a damping not above 0 is an error and above 1 a warning (over-relaxation); a periodic bound needs two bounds; a link to itself, or of a refined value, is refused; a variable moving a refined value or a mode phase's coordinate is refused; a step that frees nothing is an error with its name; unknown classes and guards get the nearest name;
  • ParameterSet::describe gives each free parameter's class, damping, and for each value its coefficient, bounds, behaviour, maximum shift and link, for the app. The .pcr import reads R_at … R_gl and the multipliers by their meaning (L107); the export writes them back, and notes what FullProf has no place for.

The command line. drs refine runs a session.

  • --session <file> resumes from the file if it exists and saves the state after; with --cycles n it is Step n.
  • --plan runs the control file's steps in order, each from the values the previous step reached, and stops the plan at a divergence, a singular matrix or Ctrl-C; --step k runs one step.
  • --parameters prints each cycle's parameters; each cycle's notes are printed; the restraints are printed with their weight and contribution.

Tests (steering, 13):

  • shift_control against FullProf (L109);
  • the import of multipliers;
  • damping never changes an esd: damping 0.3 reaches damping 1's minimum within 0.007 esd, with esd's within 1.1·\(10^{-4}\), in 28 cycles instead of 7;
  • a maximum shift binds and the dictionary's defaults do not;
  • stop, reflect and periodic, to \(10^{-12}\);
  • the occupancy's bounds on Y2O3's three sites;
  • links giving Co-YBCO's shared codewords exactly (the same set; the same refinement to \(10^{-12}\), Cu1 + Co1 kept);
  • one value across phases;
  • case A's 5 refined amplitudes as variables moving the coordinates: the refinement of the amplitudes to 7·\(10^{-14}\) esd;
  • each guard;
  • a session stepped 2, saved, resumed and run 2 more, equal to 4 straight cycles to the last digit;
  • a plan of three steps reaching the single run's minimum within 0.003 esd, written and read back;
  • the restraints' weights and contributions;
  • the reader's checks;
  • the command line. The other suites pass, with the dictionary's maximum shifts, the occupancy bounds, the singular guard and the damping now imported.

The app's requests (branch app, filed while this was built):

  • 23–28, W11 and W12: answered by the above. Session::paths gives each parameter's path over the cycles (25); lsq::restraints_part gives the restraints' total part of \(\chi^2\) (27).
  • 30: diagnostics::forbidden_in(model, x) gives the forbidden reflections over the pattern's range, Zero added; drs reflections --forbidden uses it, and reflection_diagnostics checks it.
  • 29 is left for Josu. A name {data} background.drb could not be told from {control} background.drb by its name. Proposed: one kind, whose {control} is the data file's stem until a control file exists; the control file created from that data file (W4) then takes the same stem.

L1092026-10-11finding

Against FullProf's shift_control runs: the steering agrees parameter by parameter; the first cycle from that start does not, explained in part and frozen (for Josu)

Test steering::shift_control_against_fullprof, one cycle of each of FullProf 8.20's three runs (L097) from the same start.

What agrees.

  • For every one of the 72 parameters, FullProf's shift in m05 and in r05 over its shift in m10 is the core's. m05 halves the cell and nothing else; r05 halves the 9 profile-class parameters and none of the 63 others. FullProf's ratios hold to its print, the core's exactly.
  • The computed (undamped) shifts are the same in the three runs.
  • The core's esd is not changed by the damping: \(\sigma\)/√\(\chi^2\)_ν is the same in the three runs to \(10^{-9}\).
  • FullProf's esd's follow its own rules exactly. The ratio m05/m10 is 0.49989 = ½ (10.600783/10.601567)³, and r05/m10 is 0.99978 = (10.600783/10.601567)³. FullProf derives \(\sigma\)(a) from the reciprocal metric A = 1/a\(^{2}\) at the new a: it refines A (manual p. 19, the magnetic cell's multiplier 0.25).
  • The core's \(\sigma\)(a) at the start's \(\chi^2\) (7.544, the \(\chi^2\) FullProf uses after a cycle) is 9.095·\(10^{-5}\), against FullProf's 9.050·\(10^{-5}\): 0.5 %, FullProf's \(\chi^2\) being over the points with Bragg contributions (esd_profile). The core reports \(\sigma\) at the \(\chi^2\) of the values reached (1.86 here), hence its 4.52·\(10^{-5}\) after this cycle.

What does not agree: the first step itself. The core moves a by +0.003126 Å, FullProf by +0.001567. Over the parameters translated as they are, the core's shift over FullProf's is:

  • U 0.975, V 1.92, W 4.29, X 1.58, zero 2.03, a 2.00, η 0.29;
  • 6 of the 7 are beyond 10 %. The background points move less than their esd in both programs, and are left out.

Explained in part. FullProf's own Y2O3 run starts at its minimum (its cycle 1 moves nothing by more than \(10^{-6}\)). shift_control moves a by 0.005 Å, about 130 esd: at 2\(\theta\) = 140° the peaks are misplaced by about 0.15°, more than their width, far outside the range where a Gauss–Newton step is linear. There every detail of the derivatives changes the step: FullProf's cell parameter A = 1/a\(^{2}\), its hard peak window (with which the core's analytic derivative of U differs from finite differences by cos 0.964; with the smooth window, exactly), and others not identified. Both reach the same minimum (fullprof_refinements).

In the linear range the core's step is the Gauss–Newton step. From the minimum with a, b and c moved by 3 esd, one cycle brings a back to −0.04 esd.

Frozen in the test (it fails if more than 6 parameters, or a factor above 4.29, differ), and listed for Josu: one FullProf run would separate it, Y2O3's converged file with a moved by 3 esd (10.604939 − 0.00011, b and c tied), one cycle. If FullProf's step then returns a as the core's does, the difference belongs to the far start alone.

L1082026-10-11finding

How the core treated multipliers until 0.23.0, which compared runs carry relaxation factors, the scale's class measured, and the largest shifts per cycle measured

A lone multiplier until 0.23.0. It was taken as a change of variable f = a·q. Since the derivative carries a, the value's shift and its esd were the same as with a = 1: no damping, and the field's own esd, not FullProf's a·\(\sigma\).

No esd compared so far carries FullProf's scaling. No .pcr of the comparisons (fullprof_refinements, restraints, Le Bail, QPA, modes) has a lone multiplier other than \(\pm\)1. The one in the runs, YVO3/YVO3a_calc.pcr (Y \(\times 0.5\)), is used only for its zero. The \(\times 2\) and \(\times 0.5\) factors in parameter sets are the import's translations (X-ray scale, axial pair), not multipliers. Shared multipliers of \(\pm\)4 on occupancies occur in FP_tests/monte.pcr and prnio.pcr, which are not compared.

Relaxation factors were not read until 0.24.0. In 27 of the 456 .pcr files of manuals/fullprof/ they are not 1 (0.8 in 9, 0.85 in 5, R_at alone 0.08–0.5 in 6, …). Among the compared runs, mode case A (0.8, a_modes, a_coords) and LaMnO3 3T2 (0.85) were refined undamped, where FullProf damps them. Their tests pass with or without, since they compare converged values. The results file of case A after one cycle is regenerated (test results_file, its GSAS-II reading too): the cell moves by \(10^{-5}\) Å, a tenth of its su.

The classes, measured. In shift_control/r05 (R_pr = 0.5) FullProf halves the shifts of the scale, U, V, W, η, X, both asymmetry parameters and the cell (9), and leaves the zero and the 62 background points whole (63). The scale's class is not in the manual's list; it is the profile class, measured. Every other class is the manual's. Two classes are believed, not measured: the wavelength ratio (global) and the mode amplitudes (atomic, being coordinates). The dictionary has each entry's class and its source.

The largest shift per cycle, measured on the core's runs of the nine FullProf comparisons and mode case A (both parametrisations), from FullProf's starting files with no limit:

  • zero 0.22°, displacement 0.20°, transparency 0.06°;
  • U 0.034 deg\(^{2}\), V 7.8·\(10^{-4}\), W 8.1·\(10^{-4}\);
  • X 0.030, Y 0.010;
  • the cell 0.004 Å, β 0.0018°;
  • positions 3.8·\(10^{-4}\), B 0.15 Å², overall B 0.23 Å², occupancy 2.3·\(10^{-4}\), β_ij 4.5·\(10^{-4}\);
  • modes 2.8·\(10^{-4}\) Å, η₀ 2.5·\(10^{-4}\), the asymmetry 5·\(10^{-3}\), S/L 6·\(10^{-4}\), the ratio 1.6·\(10^{-3}\). The dictionary's defaults are physical guardrails, each at least 4 times the largest measured (in its unit):
  • zero, displacement and transparency 1°; U, V, W 0.5 deg\(^{2}\); the size and strain terms 0.5;
  • cell lengths 0.1 Å, angles 1°;
  • positions 0.05, B 1 Å², overall B 1 Å², occupancy 0.2, β_ij 0.01;
  • mode amplitudes 0.2 Å; η₀ 0.2, η slope 0.005/°; the asymmetry 0.5; S/L and D/L 0.02; G1 0.5, G2 0.2;
  • the ratio 0.05. The scale and the background (linear, in arbitrary units and counts) have none. No comparison changes with them.

A fault found and fixed. A value that the import's translations give a row, but that had no codeword of its own, was left out of its parameter. This was D/L when D_L < 0.0002 and S_L is refined (Co-YBCO's Cocus-asym: FullProf's S_L moves both S/L and H/L of the default model), and P1 when only P2 is coded. Found by the export's round trip; such a value now stays in its parameter. With it, Co-YBCO's frozen difference between the predicted correlations and those after refining (check_diagnostics::predicted_correlations, L076) grows from 0.03169 to 0.03323, the same pair (a – c) and the same single pair crossing 0.9. With the fix disabled it is 0.031683, so the growth is the corrected set's. Re-frozen at 0.03324, for Josu to accept.

L1072026-10-11decision

W11 accepted; W12 built as asked: a lone multiplier is damping, shared ones are a link's coefficients, and no esd is ever scaled

Josu accepted W11 (L095) and asked for W12 (L097) to be built in the core, with the import of a .pcr's multipliers by their meaning. The choices made in building it, each with what was set aside:

  • A lone multiplier is damping. A codeword whose parameter number is carried by one value alone (the values the symmetry ties to it, such as a cubic cell's b and c, do not count) is that value refined with its shift multiplied by |a|, as the manual says (p. 13, "the factor by which the computed shift … will be multiplied"). The coefficient keeps a's sign. Shared multipliers stay the coefficients of a link (Co-YBCO's Cu1 and Co1 occupancies, 71.00 and −71.00).
  • The relaxation factors R_at, R_an, R_pr, R_gl (LINE 9, p. 77) are the damping of the four classes, and the damping of a parameter is its own times its class's.
  • No esd is changed by damping. \(\sigma\) = √((A\(^{-1}\))_jj \(\chi^2\)_ν) whatever the step. FullProf prints a·\(\sigma\) for a parameter with multiplier a (its eq. 3.7, p. 43; measured, L097). That reading is kept in the FullProf-compatibility layer, lsq::fullprof_esd, for comparisons only (L043). Set aside: printing FullProf's esd, which would make an uncertainty depend on how cautiously the value was approached.
  • Convergence is judged on the computed shift, before damping. A damped run converges to the same minimum and does not stop early because its applied steps are small.
  • The guards and their defaults. \(\chi^2\) rising 3 cycles in a row. A computed shift above 100 esd's, from cycle 3 (the first cycles from rough values legitimately move far). An impossible value (non-finite, a cell length or angle out of range, a negative Gaussian H\(^{2}\) at a reflection). A singular matrix, named as check names it (R24). Each threshold is in the control file, and 0 turns a guard off. FullProf's Dum = 2 stops at the first rise of \(\chi^2\) (manual p. 68); set aside as the default because a Le Bail extraction or maximum-likelihood weights can raise \(\chi^2\) once without diverging.
  • The step. λ's start defaults to 0, a Gauss–Newton step first, as before; GSAS-II starts at \(10^{-3}\). With marquardt = false every step is applied, as FullProf applies them, and the guards stop a divergence.
  • Bounds. Stop (held, P39), reflect, or periodic, the last two as FullProf's IBOUND 0/1 (LINE 47). The physical bounds come from the dictionary, now with the occupancy's upper bound, a full site (Occ \(\le\) m/M); the user's bounds go on top.
  • Maximum shifts are absolute, in each value's unit, as asked; a relative maximum was set aside. The defaults are in the dictionary (L108).
  • Links x = c·p + d are applied after the symmetry's ties, and their values are set from the followed value at the start of a run.
  • New variables are columns of their own, through the symmetry like any parameter. They may share a value with each other (two modes moving one coordinate), never with a parameter.
  • A plan is a list of steps, each with selectors (addresses, prefixes ending in *, variables, class words, "all"), its damping over the steering's, its cycles and its convergence. "One cell for two patterns" waits for multi-pattern refinement (one pattern today).

L1042026-10-11decision

R31: the Explore module, a fifth stream that shows the path of a refinement in configuration space and in physical subspaces

Josu proposed showing the refinement's path: the point in the space of the freed parameters as it moves, and the physical things the parameters build (the cell, the peak shape, the structure, the mode amplitudes) as they change, so that physical evolutions can be seen and judged. Audited and extended (R31):

  • the trajectory projected sensibly, since 72 free parameters cannot be drawn: traces scaled by esd, pairs with the covariance ellipse, and the directions the data cannot fix, from the core's eigen-decomposition (R24);
  • a \(\chi^2\) map on a grid around the current point for a chosen pair, with the path drawn on it;
  • the physical subspaces: the cell box, strain and ticks; a reflection's profile and FWHM(2\(\theta\)), marked where U, V, W become impossible; the structure with distances, angles and bond valences against plausible ranges; the mode amplitudes, with the structure morphed from the parent mode by mode; size and strain;
  • replay, a past cycle opened and refined again from there, two paths compared, W11's stops marked;
  • later, a point dragged by hand in a two-parameter subspace with the pattern and \(\chi^2\) following. Every number comes from the core; the module is a viewer.

Organisation, as for Publish (L094): a fifth stream in its own worktree (../20261009_diff-ref-str-explore, branch explore). It owns explore/ (design, prototypes, requests) and, later, app/ui/modules/explore/, and asks the core and the app's shell for anything else in explore/REQUESTS.md. It starts with a design for Josu: what FullProf and GSAS-II offer (checked), the projections, the 3D library bundled with the app, and mock-ups on Y2O3 and Sr2ScSbO6 by modes. Set aside:

  • drawing the full configuration space as such (not drawable);
  • computing the derived quantities in the app (against "no physics in the app");
  • adding the module to the app stream's list (it would wait behind W11, W12 and the fixes).

L1032026-10-11decision

Publish: everything in LaTeX, in Latin Modern, in every figure and table and every preset

Josu reviewed the Publish stream's design and mock-ups (branch publish, commit afe2b05): "not bad", but everything must be LaTeX, in the figures, the labels, everything. The mock-ups used two typefaces in each figure:

  • the journal preset set words and digits in Helvetica, with only the symbols (Y_obs, 2\(\theta\)) in Latin Modern (pdffonts: Helvetica + LatinModernMath);

  • axis labels, tick labels (in math mode, with the true minus), legends, insets and annotations;
  • units through siunitx;
  • chemical formulas through a chemistry package (mhchem or chemformula);
  • irrep labels and symbols in math mode. A raster (TIFF, PNG) or EPS for a journal is made from that LaTeX-typeset PDF. The design's §9 points 4 (the thesis presets' fonts) and 5 (the digits) are answered by this rule. Set aside: Helvetica for journals; the body font of a template (EHU Sans) in figures; any text drawn outside LaTeX.

L1012026-10-11work

The results file written in pdCIF, read back and read by GSAS-II; one structure CIF per phase; the file dictionary updated

results::write writes the results file of a refinement as Josu chose (L092, L099): "{control} {time} results.cif" in Results/. It is a powder CIF of three kinds of data blocks, linked by _pd_block_id.

  • data_overall: Rp, Rwp, Rexp and the goodness of fit (_pd_proc_ls_prof_*, _refine_ls_goodness_of_fit_all), the numbers of points, parameters and restraints, the phases' blocks, every refined parameter with its value and standard uncertainty, and the control file as a text field.
  • One block per structure phase, the deposition CIF's (L083): cell, symmetry and atoms with their uncertainties, R_Bragg and the geometry, with _pd_phase_name.
  • data_pattern: the wavelengths, the excluded regions, the profile (_pd_meas_2theta_scan, _pd_meas_intensity_total with \(\sigma\) as su, _pd_proc_ls_weight, _pd_proc_intensity_bkg_calc, _pd_calc_intensity_total, each phase's curve), and the reflections (_pd_refln_phase_id, _refln_index_*, _refln_symmetry_multiplicity, _refln_d_spacing, _refln_F_squared_calc, _refln_intensity_calc, _refln_intensity_meas partitioned by the model, _refln_intensity_sigma). DDL1 names are used wherever the core or powder dictionary has one, and the local prefix _drs_ for the rest: the phases' curves, the reflections' 2\(\theta\) and FWHM, the parameters, \(\chi^2\), the control file.

Beside the results file, one structure CIF per phase ("{control} {phase}.cif"). On demand, the CSV exports of the reflections and of the profile (results::reflections_csv, profile_csv).

Read back and by GSAS-II.

  • results::read reads the file back. The CIF reader now keeps text fields and gives a value's su (cif::value_su).
  • GSAS-II (305a159) takes \(\sigma\) from the intensity's su and not from _pd_proc_ls_weight: its line that would read the weight is commented out (G2pwd_CIF.py). So Yobs is written with its \(\sigma\) to four significant digits.
  • tests/data/make_gsas2_results.py runs GSAS-II's own powder and phase importers on the committed file tests/data/results/a_modes_results.cif (case A, one cycle) and on its phase's CIF. The phase importer needs GSAS-II's compiled pyspg, built here with gfortran and f2py from its sources.
  • Test results_file:
  • the profile, the 519 reflections, the agreement factors and the 17 parameters with their uncertainties are the core's, to the seven figures written;
  • the control file read back writes the same text, and the phase's CIF gives the cell within half its su;
  • the committed file is what the core writes today;
  • GSAS-II reads every point with the core's Σx, Σy and Σw, the first and last points and the wavelength, and from both CIFs the cell, P2₁/n and the 6 atoms.

The dictionary (files.yml): the results kind decided (pdCIF; extension cif). New kinds: reflection_notes, export_reflections, export_profile. Every other kind decided (L099). The {results} placeholder removed. 21 kinds. Command: drs results <control> [--cycles n] [--project folder] [--time t] [--exports] writes them where the dictionary says.

core/src/results.rs, cif.rs, formats/files.rs, main.rs (drs results) · core/tests/results_file.rs, data/make_gsas2_results.py, data/gsas2_results.txt, data/results/ · GSAS-II 305a159 imports/G2pwd_CIF.py (l. 276–292), G2phase_CIF.py · COMCIFS cif_pd.dic, cif_core.dic (DDL1) · L083, L092, L099
L1002026-10-11work

Readers for FullProf's .prf and .hkl (app request 17); the core's side of W7–W8: per-reflection widths and partitioned intensities, forbidden reflections, a mode phase's parent indices and k-vectors, phase shares, the cumulative \(\chi^2\)

App request 17. formats::fullprof_outputs holds the two readers.

  • read_prf reads both layouts of FullProf's .prf:
  • the one with reflections on lines of their own and 0 w : p;
  • the older one with a Bragg column, where the phase is the tick's row. It returns title, \(\chi^2\), phases, wavelengths, Zero, SyCos, SySin, propagation vectors, excluded regions, the profile, and the background with FullProf's offset added back (Backg + least Backg/3, L082). h k l are read as FullProf's 3I3 fields, so "( 0 0-10)" is 0 0 −10.
  • read_hkl reads the blocks of a .hkl by the column names each prints. Hkl = \(\pm\)3, 2 and 1, and the magnetic lists, name their columns. The blocks profile matching writes (Irf = 2) do not: their columns are the manual's h, k, l, Mult, Intensity, then 2\(\theta\) and FWHM, checked against the same run's Hkl = 2 file.
  • Test fullprof_outputs, over every file in manuals/:
  • 188 .prf read and 3 of integrated intensities refused;
  • each phase's reflections match the count of line 3, and 65 files' excluded regions match their .out;
  • 168 .hkl read, each block no longer than its heading announces;
  • in 28 blocks, every 2T is a tick of the same pass's .prf.
  • fullprof_profiles now reads its 63 files with this reader and compares its background with the core's.
  • Command: drs fullprof <file.prf|file.hkl>.

W7–W8, in diagnostics, reflections and modes:

  • diagnostics::reflections: each reflection's d, 2\(\theta\), FWHM, η, |F|², I calc, I obs, \(\sigma\) and (I obs − I calc)/\(\sigma\). I obs is partitioned by the model as Rietveld's method shares the counts above the background, and is documented as such. Its \(\sigma\) is the points' \(\sigma\) propagated through the same partition.
  • Against the reflection tables of five FullProf runs' .out, the FWHM agrees to \(10^{-5}\).
  • For reflections of Icalc \(\ge 20\), I obs/I calc agrees within FullProf's print (0.005) on Y2O3, A and B. FullProf's \(\sigma\) differs by 2–6 %; its formula is not in the manual.
  • Frozen: rutile-anatase (TCH with axial divergence), 9 strong reflections lower by up to 0.13, not resolved; E's (0 1 2), whose window reaches into its out-of-order excluded region (L098).
  • reflections::forbidden: the systematically absent reflections in a range, each with the condition it breaks, written as the tables write it ("0k0: k = 2n", "h0l: l = 2n", "hkl: h + k + l = 2n", "00l: l = 4n"). The condition comes from the operator: the class of reflections it leaves fixed, and h·t integral on it.
  • modes::ModeParent::reflection: a mode phase's reflection in the parent's cell (h·P\(^{-1}\)), and its k-vector against the parent's reciprocal lattice, which the centring allows. The parent (group and P) comes from an AMPLIMODES analysis (Analysis::mode_parent) or the control file (mode_parent_group, mode_parent_basis, mode_parent_origin). Test symmetry_modes::parent_reflections:
  • case A by analysis 0_1: 96 reflections at \(\Gamma\), exactly those of the parent's lattice, and 62 at X, the k of its X irreps;
  • case E, R-3 on hexagonal axes: all at \(\Gamma\), with the obverse transformation a_h = (b − a)/2, b_h = (c − b)/2, c_h = a + b + c. The signs used in normalisation give the reverse setting, whose R centring is not F.
  • diagnostics::shares: the background's and each phase's share of Ycal at a point.
  • diagnostics::cumulative: Σw(y − yc)² from the first point, and √w(y − yc), with fraction() and GSAS-II's scaling (GSASIIpwdplot.py: the end at the observed maximum).
  • lsq::weights is public.
  • Test reflection_diagnostics; commands drs reflections [--forbidden] and drs calc --cumulative --shares.

L0982026-10-11finding

The mode cases B, D and E agree with FullProf: the 1–6 % of L093 came from the reflection limits, D's λ/2 widths and FullProf's rule for the background below its first point; what is left are single points at the edges of excluded regions, explained and frozen

L093 found Σw(y − yc)² 1–6 % away from FullProf on cases B, D and E. Before anything else was built (L037), the cause was found. There were three causes.

  • The reflection limits. The test took the .new files without FullProf's S(1/d)max (L035). It now takes each run's CODE.pcr (FullProf's refined values) with the limits of its CODE_calc.out. B and E then agree.
  • D's λ/2 widths. D gives Ratio < 0, and FullProf then reads U, V, W for the second wavelength on line 32 (manual p. 75: "If Ratio < 0 the parameters U,V,W … for the second wavelength are read separately"). The core had ignored them and kept the negative ratio, which made negative λ/2 peaks. Now Phase::uvw2 holds them: the import reads them with |Ratio|, the control file writes lambda2_uvw, the .pcr export writes them back, and the Jacobian holds them fixed. D's pattern now agrees with FullProf's to 1.3·\(10^{-5}\).
  • The linear background below its first point. FullProf reads a data point less than one step below the first background point on the line through the first two points. Every other point beyond the ends takes the end point's value. The step is FullProf's own, the "Step" of its .out. Evidence, from the .prf's Backg:
  • B and E: their points within the step are on the line;
  • A's point and LaMnO3_cub's (4.40° below 4.45°), exactly one step below: constant;
  • D's, exactly one data step below, but on the line, since FullProf's step there is a little longer than the data's. This is Convention::background_edge, with Model::fullprof_step set from the .out in the FullProf tests. On import it is kept rather than translated, since a translation would add background points and renumber the refined ones. The .prf's Backg offset (a quarter of the least background, L082) follows, since the extrapolated value can be the least (D).
  • Σ counts the points that get peaks. The core's statistics now leave out the points that its calculation leaves without peaks (Model::excluded_points, the rule of L033). Before, they used the plain regions. E's .out Σ leaves out a point that its .prf marks as counted. B's .out Σ is matched only when its two edge points are counted the way its calculation treats them.

What is left, frozen.

  • FullProf turns an excluded region into points with its own step, which for files of x, y, \(\sigma\) (Ins = 10) is not the data's: in B, D and E it is longer by 4·\(10^{-4}\) to 8·\(10^{-4}\) of a step. It also differs between two runs of the same data (\(\mathrm{Yb_{2}ZrWO_{8}}\): 0.040013 and 0.040016), so the core cannot derive it.
  • In B, the point at the low region's end has peaks and counts in FullProf, and the point before the high region has neither. The core treats both the other way.
  • In E, which lists its regions out of order (the high one first), FullProf calculates peaks inside the second region while leaving its points out of Σ, and counts them in N − P + C. symmetry_modes::EDGE_POINTS names these points. With FullProf's own Ycal over the core's point set, these points changed to FullProf's choice, Σ matches every .out within its print (four digits). The core's own Σ is within 0.03 % on all eight runs. Case A's window-edge points are the kind L035 froze.
  • The .prf's reflection ticks of D are at the Zero after FullProf's one cycle at NCY = 0. Its d_calc.pcr was not rewritten, so the test takes the .out's last Zero_pat1, as for the window experiments.
  • B's ticks past the data, near 180°, agree to 1.1·\(10^{-3}\)°, against 2.9·\(10^{-4}\)° within the data. Believed, not checked: FullProf's 2\(\theta\) in single precision near 180°. The tick tolerance applies within the data and 5° past it.
  • The magnetic run NdNi-HTO: its .hkl 2T and its .prf ticks differ from 0.004° to 0.164°. Not resolved, and left out of that cross-check.
  • Sent0 (D's \(\sigma\) factor below a given angle) is not implemented. D's Σ agrees without it.

Indexed. The sixteen .prf files of runs/modes/ are in fullprof_profiles: the eight CODE_calc.prf are compared (63 files now) and the eight pass-1 files are left out with their reason. Frozen there:

  • the window-edge points of A by modes and by coordinates (405 and 402, as srsclrrt's 404);
  • B's point at the high region's edge (Ycal max 0.065–0.145 there);
  • E's second region (Ycal max 0.358 inside it). All counts and sizes fail if they grow.

L0972026-10-11finding

How a refinement is steered in FullProf and GSAS-II, measured: FullProf's codeword multiplier halves the esd with the shift; a design for full control (W12, for Josu)

Josu asked for complete control of the refinement's path: the shifts, and the parameters linked to each other by symmetry or by choice.

FullProf.

  • Each codeword C = sign(a)(10p + |a|) carries a multiplier a, "the factor by which the computed shift … will be multiplied before use" (manual, codewords). Fields sharing p are one parameter, moved in the ratio of their multipliers.
  • Four relaxation factors by class (R_at atomic, R_an anisotropic, R_pr profile and cell, R_gl global; LINE 9) multiply the shifts.
  • Hard limits per parameter (Nre lines, LINE 47: number, low, high, step, IBOUND for hard or periodic bounds, the step for simulated annealing).
  • VARY/FIX commands with an optional multiplier "to limit the shift" (FP_COMMANDS).
  • The symmetry constraints, in automatic mode (Aut = 1).
  • Its advice: refine separately parameters correlated above 50 %.

Measured on the Mac (manuals/fullprof/runs/shift_control/, FullProf 8.20). One cycle of Y2O3 from a = 10.600 Å:

  • multiplier 1: shift +0.001567, esd 9.05·\(10^{-5}\);
  • multiplier 0.5: shift +0.000783 and esd 4.52·\(10^{-5}\);
  • R_pr = 0.5: shift +0.000783 and esd 9.05·\(10^{-5}\). So the multiplier damps the shift and scales the printed esd by the same factor, as the manual's \(\sigma\)(α_k) = a_k √((A\(^{-1}\))_kk \(\chi^2\)ν) implies when the derivatives do not carry a_k. The relaxation factor damps without touching the esd. A value's uncertainty cannot depend on how cautiously it is approached: an esd printed under a multiplier other than 1 is wrong by that factor.

GSAS-II (its code, commit fe944cd):

  • DefaultControls: 'max cyc', 'min dM/M' (convergence on the relative change of \(\chi^2\)), 'shift factor', 'SVDtol'; 'Marquardt' (the initial λ = 10^value, −3 by default) in GSASIIstrMain.py;
  • per-parameter minimum and maximum (parmMinDict, parmMaxDict), applied by setting a value back to its limit;
  • constraints of four kinds in GSASIImapvars.py: hold ('h'), equivalence with multipliers ('e'), a linear sum equal to a constant ('c'), and new variables ('f'), linear combinations refined in their parameters' place;
  • symmetry holds ("Fixed by symmetry", GSASIIstrIO.py) and equivalences generated automatically.

The core:

  • codewords' multipliers taken as the links' coefficients (how a lone multiplier is treated is to be checked against these runs);
  • the symmetry's ties;
  • limits that the engine only moves towards;
  • distance, angle and linear restraints;
  • Levenberg–Marquardt with SVD, cycles and convergence.

Proposed (APP_WORKFLOWS.md W12, for Josu): every control separate, visible and in the control file:

  • damping per parameter and per class, never touching an esd;
  • a maximum shift per cycle in each parameter's unit;
  • the Levenberg–Marquardt λ shown and settable;
  • the step shortened when \(\chi^2\) rises;
  • bounds with their behaviour (stop, reflect, periodic);
  • links: the symmetry's (shown, never editable); linear ones with an offset (shared sites, equal B's, one cell for two patterns); new variables such as mode amplitudes;
  • a .pcr's lone multiplier imported as damping, shared ones as coefficients, FullProf's esd scaling noted;
  • soft restraints with their weight and contribution to \(\chi^2\);
  • a refinement plan of steps;
  • each parameter's value, esd, shift, damping, bounds and links in view.
manuals/fullprof/FullProf_Manual.pdf (codewords; eq. 3.7; LINE 4 Nre, Cry; LINE 9; LINE 47) · manuals/fullprof/FP_COMMANDS.pdf (VARY/FIX) · manuals/fullprof/runs/shift_control/ (FullProf 8.20) · GSAS-II GSASIIobj.py, GSASIIstrMain.py, GSASIImapvars.py, GSASIIstrIO.py (local install) · core/src/lsq.rs, refinable.rs · APP_WORKFLOWS.md W12

The cycles of a refinement seen, set, stepped, paused and resumed, with every stop explained (APP_WORKFLOWS.md W11)

Josu could not see the number of cycles anywhere, nor control it. What existed:

  • the control file's [refine] cycles and convergence (FullProf's NCY and Eps), editable only in the Editor;
  • Refine's Run (⌘R), One Cycle (⌥⌘R) and Stop (⌘.);

  • the cycles and the convergence threshold visible and editable in the toolbar and the inspector, synced with the control file;

  • every stop with its reason: converged, cycles exhausted, diverging (\(\chi^2\) growing, a parameter jumping, an impossible value), a singular matrix named as R24 names it, or stopped by the user, recorded in the refinement log;
  • parameters fixed or freed during a pause, with the damping's restart said;
  • only accepted results become versions. The core gives the resumable refinement, the divergence criteria (documented thresholds, tested) and the stop reasons as data, with a command-line path; the app gives the controls and the display. Set aside: FullProf's way (NCY cycles, Eps, "Conv. not yet reached", no pause, no reason).
