L0942026-10-11decision

R30: the Publish module, a third stream with its own crate (publish/) and worktree

Josu proposed to start the module that exports everything for publication (R23) while the core and the app go on, and accepted this plan.

Why now. Everything a publication needs is built or being built: the CIF for deposition, distances, angles and bond valences (core 0.20.0), the refinement by modes (core 0.22.0), and the results file in pdCIF (R28, L092, in the cloud's round). A module that reads only the results file and the control file can be designed now and built as soon as that file exists. FullProf's runs come in through the core's reader of .prf and .hkl (app request 17).

What it does is appended to the brief as R30:

  • figures, tables, CIF, pdCIF and supplementary material;
  • a publication set rebuilt on export, with each item's provenance;
  • one rule for esd's;
  • targets as presets;
  • figures by Josu's ThesisFigures rules;
  • tables in LaTeX, Word, Markdown, HTML and CSV;
  • checks before export;
  • structures exported for VESTA. First: LaTeX tables and PDF/PGF figures for theses, then Word.

How it is organised.

  • A new crate publish/ (drs-publish) at the top of the repository, beside core/ and app/. It depends on the core and owns all formatting and rendering (tables, figures, fonts, Word, LaTeX), so the core stays physics only, with no font or PDF dependency. Its command line is drs-publish.
  • The app's Publish module lives in app/ and calls the crate as the other modules call the core.
  • A third stream in its own worktree (../20261009_diff-ref-str-publish, branch publish) edits only publish/ and asks the core and the app for anything else through requests (publish/REQUESTS.md), as the app does. This session merges it as it merges the others.
  • It starts with a design (publish/DESIGN.md), not code:
  • what publications contain, checked against the IUCr's Rietveld refinement guidelines (McCusker et al., J. Appl. Cryst. 32, 1999) and the journals' instructions;
  • the presets, and the publication set's format;
  • mock-ups made from real cases (\(\mathrm{Y_{2}O_{3}}\); \(\mathrm{Sr_{2}ScSbO_{6}}\) by modes) for Josu to accept.

Set aside:

  • the rendering in the core, which would bring fonts and PDF into the physics;
  • the module built in the app alone, with no command-line path;
  • waiting until the app is finished.

L0922026-10-11finding

The results file (R28): pdCIF, NeXus/HDF5 and a format of our own compared; pdCIF with a local prefix recommended, for Josu to choose

The three candidates were compared against what R28 lists and against who reads and writes them. The evidence is GSAS-II's code, FullProf's manual, News and *_prof.cif files, the IUCr powder dictionary as of 2026-09-08, the NeXus definitions as of 2026-10-05, and the code of pdCIFplotter, FOX, TOPAS-macros and EasyDiffraction. The journals' and databases' pages could not be opened from the session (the proxy refused them): what they ask for is quoted from search extracts and marked not checked.

  • pdCIF holds observed, \(\sigma\), calculated, background, reflections (hkl, phase, d, F\(^{2}\)), Rp, Rwp, Rexp, R_Bragg and the model with its uncertainties. Since the 2.5.0 dictionary (2026-09-08; whether the IUCr has approved it is not checked) it also holds each phase's curve, under DDLm names that no reader uses yet. It has no item for the difference, for \(\chi^2\) (only S), for a machine-readable excluded region (weight 0 at the points instead), for 2\(\theta\) positions of reflections (only through a peak table), or for the control file and the data file's name.
  • Writers: GSAS-II, without phase curves (G2export_CIF.py); FullProf with Rpa = −1, with R factors in percent where the dictionary has ratios and its \(\chi^2\) under a name outside the dictionary; TOPAS through user macros.
  • Readers: GSAS-II (the observed pattern only), pdCIFplotter (the whole fit), WinPLOTR, FOX.
  • NeXus/HDF5 has no definition for refinement results. No program was found that writes or reads one, and none of R28's items but the data has a standard name. It is binary, and in Rust it needs the HDF5 C library or young pure-Rust crates.
  • A format of our own holds everything and is read by nothing else.
  • Journals (not checked): the IUCr asks for "the results of the Rietveld refinement and the powder diffraction data … in CIF format", the profile deposited; Powder Diffraction asks for "the raw data file (as CIF)" and the Rietveld CIF; CCDC and ICSD take the refinement's CIF, with no profile asked for.

Recommended: pdCIF, written to the 2.5 dictionary with the DDL1 names wherever they exist, so that today's readers find the profile and the reflections. What it lacks goes in its own forms or under a local prefix: the difference is left to readers; \(\chi^2\) as S and under the prefix; excluded regions as weight 0 with the regions as text; the control file as a text field, with the data file's name and checksum, under the prefix (to be registered with COMCIFS). The reasons: it is what journals ask for, so the record is also the deposition; programs read it now; it is text; and the core already reads and writes CIF. Set aside: NeXus/HDF5 (no reader and no names for refinements, a C library) and a format of our own (read by no other program, against R28's "portable"). Nothing is built before Josu chooses; the page "The results file: the choice" has the comparison.

L0912026-10-11work

The file dictionary (R27): 18 kinds of file of a project started in the app, in the core and on the page Files; names proposed for Josu

ecosystem/data/files.yml names every kind of file of a project started in the app. For each kind it gives the folder and name pattern, the extension, the role, what the file holds, its format, the files that do the same in FullProf and GSAS-II (cited to the manual and to GSAS-II's code), what writes it, its status and the reason for its name. build.py turns it into core/src/formats/file_table.rs and the site page "Files". formats::files fills a pattern (path) and finds the kind of a path in a project (kind_of). A project from FullProf keeps FullProf's names, which formats::project names (L084).

Principles: readable names with no numeric codes; phases by their names; one folder per role; versions and results by their time; measured files keep their own names; spaces allowed, as in L080's versions.

The layout:

  • {project}.drc: the control file;
  • {control} background.drb: its background points;
  • Data/: the measured files as named, and {data} processed.xys;
  • Structures/: {phase}.cif and {phase} AMPLIMODES.txt;
  • Instrument/: {instrument}.irf;
  • Versions/: {control} {time}.drc and {control} refinement log.md;
  • Results/: {control} {time}.{results}, {control} {phase}.cif and {control} {phase} bonds.txt;
  • FullProf/: the export under FullProf's own names ({control}.pcr, .bac, {control}{n}.hkl, .irf, .xys), which FullProf requires;
  • .drs/: the app's state.

Of the 18 kinds, five are decided: the extensions .drc (L070) and .drb (L081), the versions and the refinement log (L080), and .drs/ (L067). The other 13 names are proposed for Josu to accept.

The core follows the dictionary. drs convert without --to writes {stem}.drc beside a .pcr, or FullProf/{stem}.pcr from a control file. drs files lists the dictionary or names the kind of a path.

Test file_dictionary:

  • the table is the YAML's, with every field filled and every placeholder known;
  • every pattern's filled name is recognised as its kind;
  • the .pcr exports of three FullProf runs write files of all five FullProf kinds, each named as the dictionary says;
  • a control file and a background file the core writes are named by project::describe with the dictionary's role.
core 0.22.0 · ecosystem/data/files.yml, tools/build.py, site/manual/files/ · core/src/formats/files.rs, formats/file_table.rs, main.rs · core/tests/file_dictionary.rs · APP_WORKFLOWS.md W5 · L067, L070, L080, L081, L084
L0902026-10-11work

Refinement by symmetry modes, step 1 (R29): a phase as a reference structure plus mode amplitudes, read from FullProf and AMPLIMODES, refined and checked against FullProf's runs

Josu's mode-crystallography material arrived on main (manuals/bcs, private: its README says what to trust; the book's chapters are not a reference). Step 1 of R29 is built on its data files and FullProf's runs, the theory primer, the description of FullProf's mode input, and the published papers' text. Nothing of the material is reproduced here: the cases are named by letter, and only the agreements are given.

The model (module modes). A phase keeps its reference structure (the parent written in the distorted phase's cell) and its modes, and its atoms sit at r\(^{0}\) + Σ_m Q_m·c_m·V_m. This is FullProf's rule, from its notes of 29 August 2008 – 16 January 2009, reproduced in its symmetry-mode tutorial. The amplitudes Q_m are refinable fields, with dictionary entries mode_amplitude and mode_vector. Their derivatives come by the chain rule through the coordinates. FullProf's Max_Amplitude is the limit |Q| \(\le\) value. Each irrep's amplitude is √(Σ Q\(^{2}\)) with polarisation vector Q/A (Orayech et al. 2015, J. Appl. Cryst. 48). Its esd is computed from the amplitudes' covariance, which reduces to FullProf's rule when the covariance is diagonal: FullProf neglects the correlations. So are the positions' esd's.

  • gram sums each mode over the distorted structure's primitive cell in a given metric.
  • project decomposes a structure given by its coordinates into the modes.
  • transform_cell gives the reference metric from a parent cell and AMPLIMODES's transformation.
  • displacements pairs a structure's atoms with the reference by symmetry.

The readers.

  • FullProf's mode .pcr: V_MODES with each vector's irrep; A_MODES with the codewords; Max_Amplitude.
  • The second integer of A_MODES (2 in every file of the material) is not a format flag. It is FullProf Studio's output: 2 puts each irrep's modes together as arrows, −2 as structures, ±N all modes one by one, −3 none, or a list (note of 11 January 2009).
  • AMPLIMODES's text output (read_amplimodes): parent and distorted structures, transformation, reference structure, atom mappings, summary of amplitudes, the block for FullProf. It handles tab-separated and space-separated copies, and repeated headings.
  • The control file has [[phase.mode]] (name, irrep, mode_amplitude, mode_vector). A mode phase's atom positions are its reference positions. The .pcr export writes Jbt = 6, Furth, V_MODES and A_MODES.
  • drs modes shows a phase's modes, irreps and positions. It can check the normalisation in an AMPLIMODES analysis's metric and project another structure onto the modes. drs refine reports each irrep's amplitude, esd and polarisation.

Tests (symmetry_modes, all passing).

  • Case A, positions: computed from FullProf's final amplitudes, they agree with its .out to the last printed digit. So do each irrep's own displacements (126 values), the lengths in Å, the irrep amplitudes with their esd's, and 10 positional esd's. The lengths are computed in the refined cell, as FullProf does.
  • Case A, refinement: the engine refines the .pcr's 17 parameters with N − P as FullProf's, and reaches its \(\chi^2\) within 0.1 %. Each amplitude is within 0.07 of FullProf's esd, and each esd within 0.3 % of FullProf's. This needs FullProf's reflections past the data, up to S(1/d)max from its .out, as fullprof_refinements does: without them \(\chi^2\) is 0.13 % low. A control-file round trip and the exported .pcr keep the modes and the 17 parameters.
  • Case A, profile: its two .prf files (by modes and by coordinates) are in fullprof_profiles, class exact (rms 4.6·\(10^{-5}\)). 404 window-edge points (TCH with X ≠ 0) up to 1.2·\(10^{-3}\) are frozen as in L035.
  • The normalisation: in AMPLIMODES's reference metric each mode sums to 1 Å² over the primitive cell and the modes are orthogonal, to 2.7·\(10^{-5}\) (the vectors' 6 printed decimals). This holds for case A, for both parents of case B, and for case E (R-3, hexagonal axes). Case E has no AMPLIMODES output; its parent edge is the one its La mode implies, which equals that of case B's second analysis to 5·\(10^{-5}\) Å, and its O modes then check.
  • AMPLIMODES: projecting the distorted structures it analysed (0_1, 0_4 \(\times 2\)) onto the .pcr files' modes gives its irrep amplitudes to the 4 printed decimals, with nothing left over (\(\le\) \(10^{-8}\) Å). For case A this also gives the per-mode amplitudes of its FullProf block, magnitudes only: a one-mode irrep's sign is a convention of the setting.
  • Case B: the two parents give the same positions (to 3.5·\(10^{-7}\)) and the same pattern. Every amplitude but GM1+'s scales with the metric, within the rounding's bound. GM1+'s also takes the shift of the reference O.
  • The coordinate refinement of case A projected onto the modes: every amplitude within 0.4 esd of the mode refinement's, and nothing left over.
  • Cases B, D and E are calculated from their .new files. FullProf's own outputs at those values come from manuals/fullprof/runs/modes_runs.py (to run on the Mac), which the test then compares.

Found on the way:

  • powder::find_file now also matches names case-insensitively. FullProf ran on file systems that ignore case, and case A's coordinate run names srsclrrt.dat for SrScLRRT.dat.
  • fullprof_profiles takes the .prf's scale from a point with counts on both sides; it took 0 from case A's first point.
  • FullProf prints Yobs 1.00 where the data have 0 counts. It happens at two points, both excluded; whether its reader or only its .prf does it is not known (believed: the .prf). For Josu: the 404 frozen window-edge points of case A; the run of modes_runs.py on the Mac.

L0882026-10-10work

The data operations' settings in a dictionary (request 16), a data file written (request 12), the line of each point (request 15)

1. Request 16. ecosystem/data/operations.yml describes the ten data operations (mask, delete, smooth, rebin, normalize, add, subtract, merge, convert, wavelength) and the two analyses (peak_search, auto_background), as parameters.yml describes the parameters. For each operation it gives the meaning, how \(\sigma\) is carried and the definition it follows. For each of its 30 settings it gives the key, name, unit, meaning, range, default, proposed value, what raising and lowering it does, and the source. build.py writes the core's table operation_table.rs and the site page "Data operations: the settings". data_ops::settings(id) and keys_of(op) give it to the app. The parser now refuses a key that is not one of the operation's own (before, a key of another operation was accepted and ignored). drs data settings [<operation>] prints it, and --help after drs data op <operation>, peaks or background prints that operation's settings. The defaults are constants of data_ops (DEFAULT_MIN_SIGNIFICANCE 3, DEFAULT_MAX_ITER 10, …). Test operation_dictionary checks:

  • the dictionary against the table, the parser's keys and each operation's keys;
  • that no field is empty;
  • each default against its constant, and the arPLS λ against default_log_lambda. 2. Request 12. formats::write(scan, "xys" | "xye") writes a scan as text that the registry reads back: FullProf's XYDATA (Ins = 10) or the '#'-commented xye of GSAS-II and Topas. Numbers are written in their shortest round-trip form, and a point with no weight gets \(\sigma\) = \(10^{30}\), which read takes back as no weight. drs data read|op|apply --out f.xye|f.xys writes that format, and the .pcr export writes its processed data through it. Test data_write: 294 data files of the material read, 283 2\(\theta\) scans written in both formats and read back to the bit. The one exception is GSAS-II's rule for xye: a point with y \(\le 0\) is read as y = 0 with no weight (947 points). xys keeps them. 3. Request 15. formats::point_lines(text, scan) gives the line (from 1) of each point, or None when the file does not give it plainly. There are two cases:
  • one point a row: the next numeric line whose first value is the point's x and which holds its y;
  • FullProf's free format: Ti, step, Tf, then the intensities in order (y2o3.dat: eight a line, point 9 on line 4). Over the material: every free-format (41), xye (52) and PSI DMC (4) scan has its lines, as do 9 of 10 xys and 9 of 13 GSAS scans. The others give None, rather than a guess: GSAS RALF with x in its own units, an XYDATA file in TOF, and the ILL formats with a header to each block. 4. Requests 13 and 14 were done in core 0.18.0 and 0.20.0 (check, drs check with its data; formats::project::describe). app/REQUESTS.md belongs to the app stream, and main's copy does not yet hold requests 14–16, so it is not edited here: the app stream closes 12–16 by naming core 0.21.0 when it merges main. It has to merge anyway, since app 0.8.0 does not compile against core \(\ge 0.19.0\) (L085).

L0872026-10-10finding

FullProf's background settled on all eleven variants: the quarter within single precision, the spline's end point at the data's end and none past it; the \(\chi^2\) differences frozen per variant

background_points now checks every variant of runs/background/ and reports each one, not only up to the first failure (L085). All twelve FullProf runs it predicts pass; bacpairs is the asked-for mistake of L082 (FullProf's \(\chi^2\) 19.9, the core's prediction 19.926).

  • The quarter at Nba = −31. The least background is taken over the data's 13091 points, as before. The offset that the printed values allow is [3.092533, 3.092538]. The quarter of the least background is 1.1·\(10^{-7}\) below that interval, and single precision explains it. FullProf works in single precision: its least value is its own background at that point (|B|·2⁻²⁴ from the core's), and the quarter is rounded to single precision once more (|c|·2⁻²⁴). Together they allow 3.7·\(10^{-7}\). The rule is fixed with that allowance and is not frozen: the allowance is derived, not fitted. Every other variant puts the quarter inside the interval. No tolerance was widened: the bound on each point (L081) is unchanged.
  • spl62 (62 points): within the print; quarter inside.
  • spl6early (the points end before the data does): FullProf's own end point at the data's last x, with the last point's value, as in L081. Within the print.
  • spl6beyond (the last point past the data): no point is added, and the core's natural spline through the points is FullProf's. Within the print.
  • spl6thmax (Thmax before the data's end): FullProf still reads all 13091 points to 145.9° and puts its end point at the data's end, not at Thmax. Its .prf and \(\chi^2\) (1.2626) are the same as spl6's.
  • lin62: within the print; its \(\chi^2\) is bacfirst's, as it should be (the same background at the same points). The import (powder.rs) already did all of this; its comment now names L087.

\(\chi^2\) (the core's against FullProf's header, to its four decimals): lin16 −0.00006, spl16 −0.00002, spl6 −0.00104, lin6 −0.00092, spl8 −0.00055, spl31 +0.00022, spl62 +0.00018, spl6early +0.00021, lin62 +0.00017, spl6beyond −0.00082, spl6thmax −0.00104, bacfirst +0.00017. The background is not the cause: it agrees within the offset interval (widths of 5·\(10^{-6}\) to 6·\(10^{-5}\)). Two explanations were tested and rejected:

  • that FullProf rewrote the .pcr (it did not);
  • that it uses the values after its cycle (they do not match). The difference comes from the Bragg part of Ycal. It differs from FullProf's by about \(10^{-4}\) relative, the same in every variant, and each variant's residuals weight it differently. FullProf's own .prf columns give its header \(\chi^2\), and the core's Ycal agrees with FullProf's for y2o3_structure and lin62. Each difference is frozen in the test at its size, rounded up to \(10^{-5}\) (the test fails if one grows, or if a frozen value exceeds 5·\(10^{-4}\) + 1.5·\(10^{-3}\) \(\chi^2\)). These twelve frozen differences are listed for Josu to accept: below 0.1 % of \(\chi^2\), from a profile difference of about \(10^{-4}\).

fullprof_profiles indexes the ten new .prf files as compared in background_points (make_fullprof_profiles.py), and it passes.

L0862026-10-10decision

R27–R29: our own file names in a file dictionary, a results file of our own in place of .prf, and refinement by symmetry modes

Josu asked for three things, appended to the brief.

  • R27, our own file names. A project started in the app names its files by a scheme of ours, written once in a file dictionary (pattern, extension, role, contents, format, FullProf and GSAS-II equivalents) and used by the core, the command line and the app, as the parameter dictionary is for parameters (L044). A project from FullProf keeps FullProf's names. formats::project (L084) already names 73 kinds of FullProf's files and is where the file dictionary's reading side lives.
  • R28, a results file in place of .prf. FullProf's .prf has an odd internal structure (a header, the profile with Backg offset by a quarter of its least value, then the reflections, L082). Josu weighed a profile "mounted" each time from the model and data against a new file that holds everything, and prefers the second, for taking the results to other programs. Both are kept: the app draws from the model, recomputed when it opens, so a view is never stale; the results file is the record of each refinement and the file for exchange; .prf remains an export. Its format is to be chosen with evidence. Candidates:
  • the IUCr's powder CIF (pdCIF), the standard for depositing powder profiles. GSAS-II reads it, and the core reads it through the registry (L062). Whether journals require it, and which programs read and write it, is to be measured, not assumed;
  • NeXus/HDF5, the facilities' format (binary, a library to depend on);
  • a format of our own. The choice is Josu's, on that comparison.
  • R29, refinement by symmetry modes (P13, which L020 placed "later", now next). The material holds FullProf's own mode refinements: manuals/fullprof/FP_tests/LMO_Modes_Pnma.pcr, LMO_Modes_Pbnm.pcr, LMO_Modes_Pbnm_ani.pcr, Ho2BaCuO5/TOF/Ho2BaCuO5_1p5K_modes.pcr and the sequential series in Ho2BaCuO5/CW/Sequential-Modes/; the FullProf News documents the mode input from 29 August 2008, with later changes in 2008–2010 and magnetic modes in 2016. The plan:
  • first, read the modes as AMPLIMODES, ISODISTORT and FullProf's .pcr give them and refine their amplitudes, checked against FullProf's runs of those files;
  • then compute the modes from the parent and the distorted structure, which needs the irreducible representations and is a question of data and licence (Bilbao, ISOTROPY);
  • the papers are for Josu to download (the cloud cannot reach the journals). Set aside: keeping FullProf's file names for new projects; keeping .prf as the results file.

L0832026-10-10finding

Bond_Str's distances, angles and bond-valence sums reproduced on 77 .dis files; three of its rules that the default does not follow

FullProf lists distances, angles and bond-valence sums through its program Bond_Str (CODFILn.dis, CODFILn_sum.bvs; manual p. 11), from the CFL it writes after a refinement. The core's bonds module was written from CrysFML's source (the PyPI package crysfml 0.8.0: Calc_Dist_Angle_Sigma, Distance_and_Sigma, Angle_and_Sigma, Calc_BVS, Set_Atoms_Conf) and compared with the 77 .dis files of the material (LaMnO3 \(\times 2\), Yb2ZrWO8 \(\times 3\), the LaMnO3 D20 sequential series \(\times 72\)), within the printed precision:

  • 1474 distances, every neighbour at the same position; 2160 triangles of angles; 308 atoms' bond-valence sums with 1378 bonds, coordination, mean distance, distortion and the four instability indices; the d0, B0 and radii Bond_Str printed are those of CrysFML's table, written into core/src/bv_table.rs by a script.
  • Two numbers lose more than the print to single precision, with bounds derived in the test: an angle of a flat triangle (FullProf 0.03° where it is 0: √(2δ) at the arc cosine's end) and the distortion \(10^{4}\)(⟨d\(^{2}\)⟩/⟨d⟩² − 1), a difference of numbers near 1.

Bond_Str's rules that the default does not take (kept as bond_str_* functions, each checked): 1. Occupancies normalised to the first atom (Set_Atoms_Conf, fac1): the first atom is taken as full. Yb2ZrWO8's .pcr writes Occ as atoms per primitive cell (Yb 0.5, Zr 0.25, W 0.25 on 4a, O 2.0 on 8c of F m -3 m), 48 times FullProf's chemical × m/M; the scale factor absorbs it in the pattern. Bond_Str then takes Yb as 1 and O as 2.0 and prints Yb's sum as 7.500; with the chemical occupancies (Yb 0.5, O 1.0) it is 3.750, and O's 1.773 for Bond_Str's 3.547. The core reads such an Occ as atoms per primitive cell when the documented reading puts an atom above 1 and this one does not (bonds::chemical_occupancies); the occupancy check uses the same reading (it read LaB6's Occ "× m", which this reading includes for a primitive cell). 2. No term for the cell in a distance's standard deviation: an Mn–Mn distance of c/2 is printed without one. With that, every angle's standard deviation is Bond_Str's; the default's, the angle's own derivative with the cell's terms, differs at 2047 of 2160 (frozen count). 3. Two ends as independent: an atom bonded to its own image gets both ends' variances; the default moves both ends together. Six O1–O1 distances of LaMnO3_cub differ, all of that kind (frozen count). The default is the first-order propagation of the cell's and coordinates' standard deviations, each quantity differentiated as a whole; with no covariances given, they are taken as independent.

The CIF. Against the CIFs FullProf wrote (Rpa = −1) for LaMnO3 (two refinements), PPH3 and citric acid, at the values of their .pcr: 339 values (cell, coordinates, U_iso, occupancies) within both prints; U_iso's uncertainties as FullProf's; Rp and Rwp within 0.0007 % (FullProf writes them in %, the CIF dictionary as fractions); \(\chi^2\) within 0.5 % once counted over FullProf's number of parameters. Frozen, not explained: LaMnO3ref's Rp and Rwp are 0.22 % above FullProf's at its .pcr's values (5.14 % against 4.92 %); LaMnO3's cell uncertainties are up to 2.25 times FullProf's, which also refined seven symmetry-mode amplitudes (its positions' uncertainties come from those, and the core does not read modes; P13). The first is for Josu: LaMnO3ref is not in runs/, and a run of it there would say whether the .pcr matches its CIF.

CrysFML (crysfml 0.8.0 on PyPI, LGPL-3): Src/CFML_Geom/Geom_Distances.f90, Geom_Angles.f90, Src/CFML_EnBVS/EnBVS_CostF.f90, EnBVS_SetTab.f90, Src/CFML_Tables/Tab_Set_BVST.f90 · docs/bond_str.pdf · manuals/fullprof/*/*.dis · core/tests/bond_str.rs, cif_deposition.rs
L0822026-10-10finding

FullProf's background read: its spline has one point of its own at the end of the data; the .prf prints Backg less a quarter of its least value; a .bac is read in the first format only

From FullProf's own .prf, .sum and .out of runs/background/ (L079), with the bound of L081: FullProf's Backg is printed to 0.1 (h = 0.05), and a value computed in single precision carries |B|·2⁻²⁴ more, so one offset c must put every point within h + u of the core's; the interval of such c is found, not estimated.

Linear (Nba = 17). The core's linear interpolation fits: ours − FullProf's spans 0.1000 over 13091 points, and one offset fits. The 0.0503 of L079 was the error of the median offset, not of the background.

Splines (Nba = −17, −6). FullProf's cubic spline is the natural one through the points given and one point of its own at the data's last point (145.9°) with the last point's value. With it, Nba = −17 spans 0.0999 and Nba = −6 0.1000: both within the printed precision. Without it the natural spline spans 0.139–0.157 for −17 (the excess only in 129.96–144.97°, near the last two points, 144.14° and 144.97°) and 0.388 for −6. Tried and rejected: not-a-knot, clamped, PCHIP and Akima splines; splines in sin \(\theta\), tan \(\theta\) or d; knots rounded to 0.01°; an extra point at the first data point (worse); one at 180° (0.27 and 0.21). Nba = −6 is a spline: its .out says "Background interpolated by cubic splines between the 6 points given". The .pcr import now writes FullProf's point into the model, and the export drops it again (Nba = −(n − 1)); a spline that does not end with it is exported with a note that FullProf adds it.

The .prf's offset. FullProf prints Backg less a quarter of the background's least value: 3.5754 for the 17 points (least 14.30), 3.5627 for the spline of 17, 3.8206 for that of 6 (least 15.28). That is why Nba = −6's offset differed (−3.82 against −3.57): its least value differs. In each case the quarter falls inside the interval of offsets allowed, which is \(10^{-4}\) wide.

\(\chi^2\). At NCY = 0 the core's \(\chi^2\), counted over the .pcr's ten parameters, is the .prf's to its fourth decimal for Nba = 17 and −17 (0.00006 and 0.00003 away); for Nba = −6 it is 0.0010 lower (1.2616 against 1.2626), with the background within the printed precision. Not explained; frozen in the test.

The .bac file. The manual (p. 8) gives two formats, and its footnote says the second, a comment and pairs, "is not available at present". FullProf 8.20 reads any .bac as the first format: a first line start, step, end, then the intensities in order. Josu's pairs file at the 13091 data points therefore became the intensities x₁, y₁, x₂, y₂, … at successive points: the core's \(\chi^2\) under that reading is 19.926 (FullProf 19.9). The 62 pairs of L079 ran out of numbers ("end of file *.BAC"). The core now writes a .bac in the first format at the data's points. Not explained: FullProf's \(\chi^2\) 1.80 with the first format. The core's \(\chi^2\) with the same background at every point is 1.19 (FullProf's own runs give 1.20 with the points in the .pcr). Moving the background by 1, 2 or 5 steps, using the 17-point background, or using the .prf's printed Backg (1.50–1.64) does not give 1.80. Josu's file is not in the repository. background_runs.py now writes bacfirst (the core's first-format file) and bacpairs (the same values as pairs) for him to run; the core predicts 1.19 and 19.9.

New variants for the Mac (background_runs.py, to test the rules where they could fail): lin6 (the 6 points, linear: is the offset again a quarter of the least value?), spl8, spl31, spl62 (Nba = −8, −31, −62), spl6early (6 points ending at 120.79°, the extra point far from them), spl6beyond (last point at 150°, past the data: does FullProf still add one?), spl6thmax (Thmax 140°: is the point at Thmax or at the data's end?), lin62, bacfirst, bacpairs. The test asserts the predicted ones when their .prf appears and reports the others.

Core 0.18.0 and 0.19.0 reviewed with FullProf: the export holds on every run FullProf accepts; FullProf's background read; what FullProf says of the broken files

Cores 0.18.0 and 0.19.0 (L076–L078) merged into main; the core's 118 tests passed before FullProf's background runs were added. Run on the Mac with FullProf (February 2025).

The export. export_runs.py: all nine runs the core imports give FullProf's Rp, Rwp, Rexp and \(\chi^2\) exactly, LaB6 included (it now declares 18 and codes 1–18). The export to .pcr is claimed.

The background points (background_runs.py, outputs in runs/background/):

  • Nba = 17 (17 points): FullProf joins them linearly. Its .prf prints the background to 0.1 count; after the .prf's constant offset (−3.575) the core's linear background is within 0.050 of it at all 13091 points, rms 0.029, which is the rounding itself (0.1/√12 = 0.029). The test background_points now fails, by 0.0503 against a bound of 0.05: the bound is the reference's own precision, and the 0.0003 is the error of the offset estimated from rounded values. How to set the bound from the reference's precision, not widened, is for the cloud to write and Josu to accept.
  • Nba = −17: a cubic spline, natural (SciPy's CubicSpline(bc_type = "natural"): max 0.079, rms 0.030); the not-a-knot spline is 1.0 away and the linear 1.3. The core's reading of negative Nba as natural splines is right for −17; the 0.079 at some points is not explained.
  • Nba = −6 (6 points): no interpolation fits (linear rms 0.156, natural 0.075, not-a-knot 0.084, clamped 0.172), and its offset (−3.82) differs from the others'. What FullProf does with Nba = −6 is not explained.
  • Nba = 1 with the 62 points in a .bac: FullProf stops, "end of file *.BAC". A .bac needs a value at every data point: the same background interpolated at the 13091 points runs, in the first format (start, step, end, then the intensities) with \(\chi^2\) 1.80, and in the pairs format with \(\chi^2\) 19.9. The second is not explained. The core's export of a background file must write it at the data's points, in the first format.

The broken files (broken_runs.py, broken_check.txt): FullProf run on each .pcr of core/tests/data/broken/, against drs check of the same control file.

  • FullProf says nothing and writes no result: a wavelength of 0; a cell with no reflection in the range.
  • FullProf says nothing and refines to \(\chi^2\) 1.20: a coordinate fixed by the site set free; the Caglioti U given to two parameters; Thmin and Thmax of another pattern; a value in another unit.
  • FullProf refines without converging: two atoms filling one site twice (15 cycles, \(\chi^2\) 31.7); zero shift with sample displacement (8 cycles); the overall B with every atom's B (5 cycles); I_G with U and W (4 cycles).
  • FullProf names the problem in its own terms: G2 with G1 = 1, "Singular matrix!!, problem with Or2_ph1_pat1 no. 73"; a codeword hole, "Hole in the matrix!, missing element 3"; two parameters too many, elements 73 and 74; a missing .irf, by name.
  • The core reports each one before the run, at its place, with the reason, as R24 asks.
  • drs check of a .pcr cannot find its data: FullProf takes the data file from its command line, and the core reads only the .pcr. A .pcr check should take the data file as FullProf does.

L0782026-10-10work

The core's part of R25 and R26: background points in a file or inline, splines, a function fitted to points, a control file rebased onto new data

Core 0.19.0. Main was merged first (app 0.7.0, L074; R25 and R26, L075). The entries of core 0.18.0, written as L074 and L075 on this branch, became L076 and L077.

Background points (R26, W4). Module background.

  • Files:
  • our own .drb, a strict subset of TOML like the control file (format = "diff-ref-struct background 1", interpolation, points), for Josu to accept with its extension;
  • FullProf's .bac in its two formats (manual p. 8): "start step end" then the intensities, or a comment line then pairs. Read and written; FullProf's own .bac files of NdNi-HTO and LaB6 are read.
  • The control file's [pattern.background] holds the points itself (background_points) or names a file (file = "…"), at the user's choice, with interpolation = "linear" or "spline". drs background store moves the points between the two.
  • A .pcr with Nba = 1 is now imported. Its <stem>.bac gives b1·B + b2 (manual eq. 3.6, p. 46), and its control file names the .bac. A stretched or shifted file (b3, b4 ≠ 0) and refining b1–b4 are refused for now.

Interpolation.

  • Linear between the points, as FullProf's Nba \(\ge 2\).
  • A natural cubic spline through them (new BackgroundModel::Spline, with analytic derivatives for refining the points). It agrees with SciPy's CubicSpline(bc_type = "natural") to 4·\(10^{-16}\) at 350 points of Y2O3's 62.
  • Which Nba FullProf reads as splines is not settled by its documents:
  • "If Nba<0 but ABS(Nba)>4" (manual p. 69);
  • "Nba \(\le\) −4" (p. 78);
  • while −4 is the Fourier series and, since the News of 17 June 2011, −5 the Chebyshev series. The core reads Nba \(\le\) −6 as splines and exports a spline of six points or more as Nba = −n.
  • manuals/fullprof/runs/background_runs.py writes Y2O3 with 17 points linear (Nba = 17), the same 17 as splines (Nba = −17), 6 as splines (Nba = −6), and all 62 in a .bac (Nba = 1). Run with FullProf on the Mac, its .prf files settle the rule and the kind of spline. The test background_points compares them as soon as they are there.

A function from the points. background::fit: a Chebyshev series over [from, to], or FullProf's polynomial in 2\(\theta\)/origin − 1 (eq. 3.4), fitted by least squares (Householder QR).

  • Against numpy's chebfit (6 and 12 terms) and polyfit (6 terms) on Y2O3's points: \(10^{-9}\).
  • drs background fit … --into file.drc makes the fitted function the control file's background, as starting values, with the file's comments kept.

Rebase (R25, W3). Module rebase; drs rebase <file.drc|.pcr> <data> --to <new.drc>.

  • The control file is copied and pointed at the new data (the data path relative to the new file, the reader identified). The old .pcr's range and regions are dropped, and a background file is copied next to it.
  • What does not fit is reported as R24 diagnostics with suggestions:
  • another wavelength, with the line to write; or a file that does not state one (a hint);
  • another axis;
  • the range, instrument and step against the old data;
  • excluded regions outside the data or over all of it;
  • background points that leave the new data's ends uncovered, or whose counts are not the new data's (a ratio outside 0.5–2);
  • a Chebyshev range of other angles, with from/to to write;
  • the old data's operations, not carried over.
  • Then the checks of the written file run.
  • Test rebase:
  • Y2O3 onto its own data keeps model, pattern and data, with only the hint that y2o3.dat does not state its wavelength;
  • Y2O3 onto LaB6's data reports range, step and background;
  • Co-YBCO onto Y2O3's reports the excluded region and the Chebyshev range (from = 15, to = 145.9);
  • each kind is checked on a scan made for it.

control_file now runs on 66 runs, the four background variants among them: identical both ways.

App request 13: the core reports a value that names two parameters as an error before the run (L076). A value moved by two parameters is not one: imports write that legitimately, e.g. Y2O3's Bérar–Baldinozzi P1 in its own parameter and as 3·P1 in P2's. A parameter written twice shows as a singular direction.

L0772026-10-10finding

What FullProf and GSAS-II check before and during a run: neither warns of correlated parameters before it

Measured in FullProf's manual and News and GSAS-II's code (commit fe944cd), for R24. Manual pages are those of the PDF; GSAS-II's files are under GSASII/.

FullProf.

  • Codewords: C = sign(a)(10p + |a|), p from 1 to MAXS, the "Number of refined parameters" (manual p. 13). With Aut = 1 FullProf numbers them and there is no "hole in the matrix", yet "Sometimes the message 'hole in the matrix' still appears" (manual p. 69; News 2001, line 1013). The message itself is not explained. No other check of the numbering is documented. It is not in the runs' outputs; it was seen on LaB6's export (L071).
  • Site symmetry: positions, anisotropic B's and atoms sharing a site are constrained only in automatic mode (News 2002, lines 1164 and 1228; News 2003, line 1460). With codewords given by hand "the calculation may be wrong if the user fix some values violating the symmetry constraint rules" (News 2022, line 307): no check was found.
  • Input order: errors are found only when reading fails, and the messages "do not necessarily point to the initial error" (manual p. 16).
  • During the run: a parameter that goes negative is reset and its multiplier halved, with a warning (News 2003, lines 246–247; runs/YVO3/YVO3a.run.log:27, "WARNING, parameter Y < 0, fixed to 0.0001"). A singular matrix is named only as a symptom (manual p. 15).
  • After the run: with Mat = 1 the .out lists "Parameters with Correlation greater that 50%" (manual p. 72; runs/Co-YBCO/Cocus-asym.out:1573); the manual advises refining separately above 50 % (p. 14). With Ana = 1 the .sum adds an analysis of correlated parameters (runs/LaB6/LaB6rx.sum:252).
  • Not found: a check of occupancies, and any warning of correlated parameters before the run.

GSAS-II.

  • Before the run: Refine calls ReadCheckConstraints, and any error stops it with "Error in constraints" (GSASIIdataGUI.py:5749-5756; GSASIIstrIO.py:174-203). GenerateConstraints warns of parameters held, undefined, unvaried or with zero multipliers, and of duplicated constraints. It stops on more constraints than parameters ("Over-constrained input") and on linearly dependent ones ("Singular input") (GSASIImapvars.py:342-491); equivalences are checked the same way (GSASIImapvars.py:690-828).
  • Symmetry: coordinates fixed by a site are always held, "Fixed by symmetry" (GSASIIstrIO.py:1782-1797).
  • The scale with every phase fraction refined is warned of, "unstable refinement", with a constraint offered (GSASIIconstrGUI.py:1914-1940).
  • During the run:
  • parameters whose diagonal is below \(10^{-14}\) are dropped ("ouch #1 dropping singularities", GSASIImath.py:264-268);
  • the matrix is inverted by SVD, the singular values below \(10^{-6}\) of the largest zeroed (GSASIIobj.py:19-20; GSASIImath.py:104-109);
  • the parameters behind a singularity are named after the run (GSASIImath.py:150-162; GSASIIstrMain.py:61-112).
  • After the run: correlations above max(0.95, 0.99 · the largest) are listed as "highly correlated" (GSASIImath.py:131-148; GSASIIstrMain.py:113-139).
  • Not found: an occupancy check, a pre-run check of displacement parameters (negative ones are only coloured red in the Atoms table, GSASIIphsGUI.py:4888-4897), and any warning of correlated parameters before the run.

So. Neither program predicts correlated or redundant parameters before refining; both report them after. GSAS-II checks its constraints before the run and names singular parameters after it. FullProf has the "hole in the matrix" message and symmetry constraints in automatic mode. The core's checks (L076) do before the run what both do after it, from the same normal matrix, and explain each group from the dictionary.

The citations were gathered by a research pass over the files and spot-checked:

  • manual p. 69;
  • GSASIImapvars.py:448-451;
  • GSASIImath.py:143-147 and 264-268;
  • GSASIIdataGUI.py:5755;
  • GSASIIconstrGUI.py:1914;
  • News 2001, line 1013.

L0762026-10-10work

LaB6's hole in the matrix fixed; the core's checks (R24): file, model, refinement and the normal matrix before a run

Core 0.18.0.

LaB6 (L071). The original LaB6rx.pcr codes 18 parameters (codes 1–3 and 5–19, number 4 unused) plus VARY ratio, declares 19, and has Aut = 1, so FullProf numbers them itself. The export took the ratio's column as parameter 11 and left a hole there.

The export now numbers the parameters that carry a codeword 1…N in order, leaving out the ratio's column and any column left without a written code. Line 13 declares N; LaB6 declares 18. Linear restraints follow the same numbers.

Test control_file checks on all 62 exported runs that the numbers are 1…N with no hole and N as declared (refinable::pcr_parameter_numbers; the .pcr reader now keeps line 13, Pcr::declared_parameters). Whether FullProf counts the ratio in line 13 is for the Mac run to show; the original counts it, the export does not.

The checks (R24), module check. drs check <file.drc|file.pcr> [--json] prints them; the app is to call the same check_path. Each diagnostic has:

  • a severity (error, warning, hint) and a stable code;
  • its place: line, column and span in a control file, or the model's addresses;
  • what is wrong, why, a suggestion, and the replacement text when the fix is mechanical;
  • the dictionary entries it concerns.

The control file's own diagnostics now carry a code and a replacement (syntax::Diagnostic). The checks run in four levels.

(a) The file:

  • syntax errors;
  • unknown names, with the nearest known one as the replacement;
  • a comment that names another unit of the dictionary than the value's;
  • values outside the engine's limits (P39). These are warnings: a value read outside a limit only moves towards it, as LaB6's refined B of −0.148 does.

(b) The model:

  • missing data or resolution files;
  • the wavelength;
  • the range against the data;
  • occupancies beyond a site. FullProf's Occ is the chemical occupancy times m, "can be normalised" to m/M (manual p. 102), so a phase is read as × m/M unless only × m keeps every atom at 1 or below (LaB6 writes La 1 and B 6);
  • values fixed by a site's symmetry or the crystal system that a parameter moves on its own;
  • phases without reflections in the data.

(c) The refinement:

  • a .pcr's parameter numbers with holes, or a declared count different from the highest number (Aut = 0 only: with Aut = 1 FullProf numbers them, manual p. 69);
  • a value naming two parameters in a control file;
  • a free parameter whose derivative is zero at every weighted point.

(d) Before the run: the normal matrix at the starting values (lsq::normal_matrix, as the engine builds it, restraints included), scaled to unit diagonal and decomposed.

  • Eigenvalues below \(10^{-12}\) of the largest are null directions (errors) and below \(10^{-8}\) near-null (warnings), each named as the parameters it combines.
  • The correlations of its pseudo-inverse at or above a threshold (0.9 by default) are reported. Connected pairs make one diagnostic per group, and pairs inside a null direction are not repeated.

Each group is explained by the dictionary's new correlations field (parameters.yml: with, why, tip, source; generated into parameter_table::Correlation and shown on the Parameters page), choosing the entry that covers the most members. A group no entry covers is a hint that says so. Twelve entries carry correlations:

  • zero shift with displacement and transparency (manual p. 56);
  • cell lengths with zero and displacement;
  • scale with overall B, occupancies and B's (p. 58);
  • overall B with the atoms' B's;
  • occupancy with B;
  • Caglioti U with V and W (Caglioti et al. 1958, eq. 11);
  • I_G with U and W (1/cos\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) = tan\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) + 1, an exact null direction);
  • Lorentzian size with strain (manual p. 49);
  • G₂ with the scale (Dollase 1986, p. 270);
  • the Chebyshev terms among themselves.

The last was found here. Egypt, with 5–6.7° of its 5–75° excluded, has its fifteen Chebyshev terms correlated up to 0.996. The engine reports the same after a refinement, and numpy gives 0.995 for the same points: the terms are independent over their whole range and the exclusion cuts off its end.

Test check_diagnostics:

  • broken files: 18 files under core/tests/data/broken/ (make_broken.py) each raise exactly one diagnostic, the one in their name; their bases (Y2O3's control file and export, Yb2ZrWO8's control file) raise none. The 15 control files are derived from Y2O3 (one from Yb2ZrWO8, for a TCH profile); the 3 .pcr files are parameter-hole, parameter-count and irf-missing. Codes covered: syntax, unknown-name, unit, range, data-missing, wavelength, range-data, occupancy, symmetry-fixed, no-reflections, refinement, no-effect, singular (I_G with U and W; the overall B with every atom's B), correlation (zero with displacement), parameter-hole, parameter-count, irf-missing;
  • good runs: the eight runs of L071, as .pcr and as control files, raise no error. Their warnings are correlations: U–V–W, Y–X, Egypt's background, Co-YBCO's;
  • predicted against refined: on the same runs, the predicted correlations against the engine's after refining from the refined .pcr, frozen. The largest differences are 0.0003 to 0.0098, except Co-YBCO, whose cell moves in its 4 cycles (0.032, and one pair crosses 0.9 after refining).

The .pcr exports of the broken control files are written next to them (12 of 15). Syntax, unknown-name and data-missing do not export: the export needs a file that reads. They are for FullProf on the Mac, to record what FullProf says of each mistake.

L0752026-10-10decision

R25 and R26: a data file first, several projects in windows, a refinement from a previous control file, the background with the numbers in view

Josu tried app 0.7.0 and found it primitive: "I would like to open a data file; there is no option for that." The app could open one (File ▸ Open File… ⌥⌘O, Data ▸ Open Data File… ⇧⌘D), but the commands were out of sight, the dialog listed no formats, and opening a file closed the open project and made the file's folder the project. The design was organised around projects, not around what a user does first.

His requirements, appended to the brief as R25 and R26 and written step by step in APP_WORKFLOWS.md (read by every app session through CLAUDE.md):

  • W1, several projects at once, each in its own window, as VS Code-family editors open folders; APP_CONVENTIONS.md §1 already asked for it, and it is now needed before the later releases;
  • W2, a data file first: a welcome window; File ▸ Open Data File… listing the formats by name; the file opened in its own window without moving it; a first look with no model;
  • W3, a refinement started from a previous control file found anywhere on the disk, copied into the project, pointed at the new data, with what does not fit shown and checked (R24) before it is accepted;
  • W4, background points picked on the plot, shown as they are picked in a list and in the background file's text, kept in a file the control file names or in the control file at the user's choice, and compared with the automatic background and a refined function. FullProf reads points in the .pcr (Nba \(\ge 2\)) or a .bac file (Nba = 1); WinPLOTR saves picked points "to paste into .PCR file".
  • W5, proposals for Josu: a CIF dropped on the data to see its marks and simulated pattern; the instrument from the data's header and a library of resolution files by name; a refinement strategy assistant; two refinements compared; peaks passed on to indexing.

The rule for every workflow: compared with FullProf's and GSAS-II's, cited; fewer steps, no copying between windows, results shown as they are made, undoable. The app's next steps change: app 0.8.0 brings W1, W2 and W4; app 0.9.0 brings W3 and the rest of Refine on the umbrella. Set aside: keeping the project-first design, in which a data file could only be opened as part of a folder.

L0732026-10-10decision

R24: guardrails, the model checked while it is edited and before every run

Josu, seeing FullProf stop LaB6's exported file with "Hole in the matrix, missing element 11" (L071): users make such mistakes, and refinement programs tell them after the run starts, in terms of their own internals, or not at all. He asked for guardrails, appended to the brief as R24:

  • a checker that watches the control file and the model as they are edited, as a Python editor marks a syntax error while it is typed, and marks each problem where it is edited, with a list of all of them;
  • each problem says what is wrong, why, and what to do, with a suggestion where one can be given;
  • Run first runs every check: errors stop it, warnings ask;
  • checks from the file (syntax, unknown names, ranges, missing files, codewords and ties with holes or conflicts, values fixed by a site's symmetry) to the physics: parameters that cannot be refined together, correlated or redundant, found before the run and named with the reason and a tip. The checks belong to the core, with a command-line path (drs check grows into them), so that the app and drs report the same problems; the app shows them (APP_CONVENTIONS §9). drs check already reports the control file's syntax errors at their line and column and suggests names for unknown keys (L066). Believed, not checked: GSAS-II checks its constraints before a refinement and names parameters after a singular matrix; whether any program warns of correlated parameters before running is to be measured, not assumed.

L0702026-10-10work

Control files end .drc; the four faults of the .pcr export fixed; a refinement that can be stopped and each phase's curve

Core 0.17.0. Josu accepted the control file's syntax, the TOML subset of L066, with the extension .drc. The content stays valid TOML. drs convert, drs check, drs calc, drs refine, drs data info, the tests, the README and manuals/fullprof/runs/export_runs.py now use .drc.

The four faults of the .pcr export found with FullProf in L069, fixed:

  • the resolution file's name is alone on its line, after a comment line, as in FullProf's own files;
  • Thmin, Step and Thmax are written as the .pcr gave them (Co-YBCO's Thmax 150.0, Egypt's step 0.010002);
  • the excluded regions come out as they went in (Co-YBCO 0–5; Egypt 5.00–6.70 and 75–180);
  • VARY lines come straight after the wavelength line, as in FullProf's files (LaB6's VARY ratio).

How the range and the regions are kept. The import turns FullProf's regions into the data points FullProf leaves out (Co-YBCO's 0–5 becomes 0.069–5.019; the rule of L033 leaves out a point whose step reaches into a region). That loses the numbers that were written, and no arithmetic brings them back: widening 0.069–5.019 by half a step gives 0.044–5.044, and under the rule of L033 that would leave out one more point at each end.

So the control file keeps what the .pcr wrote in a table of its own, [pattern.fullprof]: range (Thmin, Step, Thmax) and excluded_regions. The export writes them back while the model's regions still leave out the same points.

Regions changed or added after the import are written half a step inside their first and last points, as the rule needs, and a step beyond the data at its ends, never clamped to the data. The export says so in a note. The range is the data's when the data are written processed.

Test control_file checks, on all 62 runs, the exported .pcr against the original, numbers compared as numbers:

  • the resolution line is one field, the file written;
  • Thmin, Step and Thmax are equal;
  • the excluded regions are equal;
  • VARY ratio is kept where the original had it, and every VARY/FIX line comes straight after the wavelength line.

The export now goes through the control file's text, as drs convert does. FullProf cannot run in the cloud: the exported files are for Josu to run again with export_runs.py on the Mac. The export is not claimed until FullProf reproduces every run it accepts (L069).

App request 6, a refinement that can be stopped. lsq::refine_until takes a callback that returns, after every cycle, whether to go on. Stopped, the refinement returns the model at that cycle's values, with esd's and correlations, and stop = "stopped by the user" (lsq::STOPPED). refine is unchanged for its callers. In drs refine, Ctrl-C stops after the cycle in progress and prints the results (a Le Bail run of MgAl2O4 stopped at cycle 376 of 500).

Test refinement_stop:

  • Y2O3 from FullProf's starting file, stopped after each cycle k = 1–5, is identical to a refinement of k cycles: the model's values, \(\chi^2\) and the esd's to the bit, and the correlations;
  • an AtomicBool set from another thread stops it after cycle 1.

App request 7, each phase's curve. Calculated::per_phase holds each phase's profile without the background, summed in the same pass as Ycalc. drs calc --phases writes them as columns.

Test phase_curves, on the three multi-phase runs: the background plus the phases' curves is Ycalc to \(10^{-12}\), and each curve is the pattern of that phase alone (the other scales set to zero) to \(10^{-9}\).

Finding, for Josu. FullProf writes these curves, with the background, in its .sub files (Ipr = 3). The only ones in the material are NdNi-HTO's, a run the core does not import (its magnetic phase). Their abscissa is the data's points corrected for Zero (10.1274 = 10.0 − (−0.12737)). The phase-1 file matches FullProf's own Ycal in the .prf to an rms of 2.4 counts.

The core's nuclear phase of that run has the right integrated intensity (within 0.06 %), but its peaks sit higher than FullProf's:

  • +0.0038° at (002), 15.15°, and +0.015° at (004), 30.56°, against the .prf's reflection list;
  • fitted against the .sub, the curve is offset by 0.03° near 30° and 0.07° near 70°.

The run is outside the verified ones, and its .irf is in the keyword format (WAVE, PROF, ASYM lines). This is not explained and nothing is built on it; the .sub comparison is not part of the test.

Proposed: run Egypt and rutile-anatase with Ipr = 3 on the Mac to give the verified multi-phase runs their .sub references.

L0692026-10-10finding

Core 0.16.0 reviewed: FullProf refuses most of the exported .pcr files, four faults in the export

Core 0.16.0 (L066) merged into main; all the core's tests pass on the Mac. The cloud could not run FullProf on the .pcr files the core exports, only read them back with the core's own reader. This was done on the Mac with FullProf (February 2025): each run's refined .pcr imported into a control file, exported back, and FullProf run on the export with NCY = 0, its agreement factors compared with those of the run itself. The script is manuals/fullprof/runs/export_runs.py, its result export_check.txt.

  • Of the nine runs the core imports, two give FullProf's numbers exactly (Y2O3, MgAl2O4-s). Six are refused by FullProf, one differs (Co-YBCO: Rwp \(5.25 \to 5.32\), \(\chi^2\) \(1.55 \to 1.59\)).
  • Fault 1, the resolution file: the export writes rt.irf <--Resolution file on one line, and FullProf takes the whole line as the file's name ("The IRF file … does not exist"). It stops five runs (Egypt, YVO3, Yb2ZrWO8, Yb2ZrWO8-PO-March, rutile-anatase). With that line corrected by hand, YVO3, both Yb2ZrWO8 runs and rutile-anatase give FullProf's numbers exactly, so the .irf written by the core is right.
  • Fault 2, the excluded regions: they are narrowed again instead of widened back. Co-YBCO's 0–5 becomes 0.094–4.994, which lets the first point (2\(\theta\) = 0.069) into the fit; Egypt's 5.00–6.70 becomes 5.005–6.695, and its 75–180 becomes 75.005–74.995, a region that ends before it starts. Egypt's \(\chi^2\) then differs (\(4.15 \to 4.14\)).
  • Fault 3, the range: Co-YBCO's Thmax 150.0 is written as 150.019, the last point of the data; and steps are rounded (Egypt \(0.010002 \to 0.01\); YVO3's \(0.020010 \to 0.02\), harmless there because Ins = 10 takes the step from the data).
  • Fault 4, VARY ratio (LaB6): the command is written after the background block, where FullProf reads the phase's name as a number ("FindFMT: invalid character in a numeric field … line 35"). FullProf's own files put VARY and FIX lines straight after the wavelength line (Egypt's .pcr). The test control_file passes because the core's import reads what the export writes; it cannot see these faults. Under the gate rule (L037) the export is not claimed until FullProf reproduces every run it accepts. Josu has still to accept the control file's syntax (TOML subset) and its extension (L066).

L0662026-10-10work

Our own control file (a strict subset of TOML, for Josu to accept) and the data operations of the Data module

Core 0.16.0. Josu accepted L062's points: the five frozen \(\sigma\) sums and the question of the point at an excluded region's end, Ins 9 and Ins 14 read as believed, and the GSAS reader following the BANK record's NCHAN.

The control file (P47, Q3), for Josu to accept. The syntax is a strict subset of TOML 1.0, files ending .toml. Reasons, against the requirements:

  • readable and diffable: one key = value per line, a table per part of the model ([pattern], [pattern.background], [[phase]], [[phase.atom]], [[restraint]], [refine], [convention]), no indentation that carries meaning, so a diff shows the value that changed and nothing else;
  • typed values without guessing: strings are quoted, numbers are numbers, true/false are booleans;
  • comments with #, kept with the user's order and formatting when the file is written again after a change;
  • every file is valid TOML, so other tools read it (Python's tomllib read the 8 runs converted by the command line); our parser is our own (no dependency) so that it can keep the comments and give each key and value its line, column and span.

Left out of the subset, each reported where it occurs: dotted keys in key/value lines, multi-line strings and dates. This leaves one way to write each thing.

Set aside:

  • YAML: indentation carries meaning; implicit typing ("no" read as a boolean, version strings read as numbers); a large specification; keeping comments on rewrite needs a heavy library;
  • JSON: no comments and noisy to read by hand. It stays the app's interchange format for the model (L058);
  • a syntax of our own in the manner of CrysFML's CFL: readable, but no other tool reads it, and every rule would be ours to document;
  • the .pcr itself: positional columns that are not named.

The file holds:

  • a format line format = "diff-ref-struct 1";
  • the pattern: the data file by its path relative to the control file, the registry's reader id, wavelengths, instrument, corrections, excluded regions, background, resolution, and the data operations;
  • phases (space group as H-M and Hall symbols, cell, profile, preferred orientation, Le Bail flag) and atoms;
  • restraints;
  • the refinement: each parameter written as its terms, the first naming it; equalities with their multipliers; limits; linear restraints; cycles and convergence. The symmetry's ties are implied, not repeated;
  • the ten convention switches.

Keys are the parameter dictionary's ids (sample_displacement, not Sycos), with the dictionary's unit as a trailing comment; choices are words (debye-scherrer, thompson-cox-hastings, maximum-likelihood). Floats are written in the shortest form that reads back to the same bits. Unknown keys get a suggestion by edit distance ("did you mean zero_shift?").

Results (test control_file):

  • all 62 runs the core imports (the runs of manuals/fullprof/runs and the window experiments) were written, read back and written again: model identical to the bit, the same pattern and \(\chi^2\) (to the bit), the same parameter set as ParameterSet::from_pcr, the data file found again, the text identical. The three runs the import itself refuses (NdNi-LTOa, NdNi-LTOb, Pd3MnD: anisotropic size and strain, version 2) are not among them;
  • a file edited by hand (comments, moved keys, values in their own notation), updated after a change, keeps everything but the changed value;
  • 8 kinds of mistake are reported at their line and column. drs check prints them as file:line:column:, in the text's order, and the outline lists each table's lines for folding (app request 11).

The export to .pcr inverts the import's translations exactly: the scale halved, P1 − 3P2 and P3 − 3P4, the axial pair, the sign of the exponential G1, and the excluded regions narrowed by half a step. Codewords are C = sign(a)(10p + |a|). The .irf and the CODFILn.hkl of profile-matching phases are written next to the .pcr; processed data are written as .xys (Ins = 10).

All 62 runs were exported and imported again by the core's reader: identical models, zero pattern and \(\chi^2\) differences, and the same parameter sets. FullProf itself was not run (cloud).

What FullProf cannot express is listed as a note:

  • a value that moves with two parameters;
  • multipliers of 10 or more;
  • the user's limits;
  • the default model's taper of the peak window;
  • the reflection range;
  • the angle restraints' weight (3 runs);
  • processed data, or a reader with no Ins;
  • the Chebyshev range;
  • the λ2/λ1 ratio refined, written as FullProf's VARY ratio command (2 runs).

drs convert goes both ways; drs calc and drs refine read control files.

The data operations (R21; app request 10). Module data_ops: an ordered list of operations, written in the control file as [[pattern.operation]] tables. drs::control::open applies them to the scan read from the file; the file is never changed. The operations:

  • mask (\(\sigma\) = ∞) and delete, by range or by points;
  • Savitzky–Golay smoothing (SciPy's mode = "interp" at the ends), \(\sigma\) propagated;
  • rebinning by a factor or to a new step, summed (counts conserved) or averaged;
  • normalising to a monitor, a counting time, a maximum, or by a factor;
  • adding and subtracting another file's scan (linear interpolation, \(\sigma\) propagated), and merging (1/\(\sigma\)² weights);
  • conversion between 2\(\theta\), d and Q, and to another wavelength.

The analyses:

  • peak search: SciPy's prominence and width at half prominence;
  • GSAS-II's peak candidates;
  • the automatic background: arPLS with GSAS-II's λ and 10 iterations.

Test data_operations, on four real files (GSAS-II's AutoBkg tutorial scan, PbSO4 X-ray, garnet neutron data, Y2O3 of FullProf):

  • 669 smoothed points and their \(\sigma\) against SciPy (to 1e-9);
  • 348 peaks: indices exactly, prominences and widths against SciPy's find_peaks and peak_widths;
  • 538 peak candidates, the same indices as GSAS-II's own lines of OnAutoSearch;
  • 262 background points within 8.3·\(10^{-10}\) of the largest count of pybaselines' arPLS, as GSAS-II calls it, with the same number of iterations;
  • 205 rebinned sums against numpy.

Exact identities: d·Q = 2π; 2\(\theta\) → d \(\to 2\)\(\theta\) and 2\(\theta\) → Q \(\to 2\)\(\theta\); a change of wavelength keeps d; normalising scales y and \(\sigma\) alike; a scan added to itself gives \(\sigma\)√2, and merged with itself gives \(\sigma\)/√2; interpolated \(\sigma\); counts conserved by rebinning. GSAS-II's STD-bank count (NCHAN − 1, L062) is followed for PbSO4's references. drs data op | apply | peaks | background | info; info also names the other files of a project (formats::project: .pcr, .out, .sum, .prf, .hkl, .sym, .mic, .irf, CIF; app request 9).

App requests: 8 and 9 were closed by 0.15.0 (info completes 9); 10 and 11 are closed by this release.

For Josu:

  • the syntax and the .toml extension;
  • GSAS-II's rejection of picks closer than two FWHM is not reproduced: it needs the instrument's profile, and its loop deletes from the list it iterates over;
  • the parameters of a control file are written as their terms, before the symmetry's ties.

L0652026-10-10decision

APP_CONVENTIONS.md: Josu's standing requirements for a usual Mac app, read by every session

Josu found himself repeating the same requirements for the app in every conversation: resizable and movable panels, a light/dark switch, Settings, release notes and manuals at hand, one manual per module, everything as a usual Mac app. They are now written once in APP_CONVENTIONS.md at the top of the repository, and CLAUDE.md tells every session to read it before any work on the app, not to relax any item without Josu, and not to ask him to repeat them. It covers, following Apple's guidelines for macOS:

  • the window: activity bar, sidebar, main area, inspector, bottom panel, status bar; every divider draggable and collapsible, sizes remembered; tabs reordered, split, dragged out into windows; panels detached and docked; native window tabbing, full screen, state restored;
  • the activity bar: the modules at the top (⌘1…⌘9, badges); light/dark/automatic, Settings, What's New, Manual and Help at the bottom;
  • the complete menu bar with the standard shortcuts, one menu per module; Settings with its panes;
  • manuals: the app's and one per module, from the activity bar, the Help menu and a ? button in each module, bundled for offline use; tooltips from the parameter dictionary;
  • keyboard and ⌘K, files and Finder, versions, the shared plot and table components, long work that never blocks, appearance and accessibility, and the extensibility that gives a new module all of this. The app's design shows how it meets each item, and each release of the app lists the items it covers. Josu owns the file; corrections go into it. Set aside: keeping these requirements in conversations only.

L0632026-10-10decision

NeXus/HDF5 and MIDAS zarr are recognised, not read, until there is an example file

GSAS-II reads these two through Python libraries (h5py, zarr). Reading them in the core needs one of two things:

  • an HDF5 decoder: the superblock, object headers, B-trees, and chunked and deflated datasets;
  • zarr's Blosc codecs (LZ4, Zstandard).

No example of either was found in the GSAS-II tutorials or in manuals/. So the registry recognises them by signature: the HDF5 magic bytes, and a zip holding zarr metadata and MIDAS's arrays. It says why it does not read them. Set aside:

  • the hdf5 crate, which binds the HDF5 C library and so adds a system dependency to every build of the Mac app;
  • writing a decoder now with no file to check it against.

Pure-Rust HDF5 readers were not evaluated: the crates.io search was refused (HTTP 429) from this session.

core/src/formats/hdf.rs · GSAS-II G2pwd_HDF5.py, G2pwd_MIDAS.py
L0622026-10-10work

The reader registry: FullProf's formats, a general table reader and GSAS-II's, recognised by content and checked against both programs

Core 0.15.0, the core's part of the data module (R21 and its note, L061). Josu accepted all of L058's points: the π/180 weight of FullProf's angle restraints in the compatibility layer, the two frozen pattern differences (Sr-ox-test's Fourier background, LaMnO3-3t2 at 21–41°), the default window's taper, set_field for the request's set, and serde on by default.

One registry (formats), used by drs and the app, of 31 formats. Each has a readable id and name, its usual extensions, the source of its layout, a reader, and a function that gives a confidence for a file. FullProf's Ins numbers are only a translation: formats::by_ins, Format::ins, and data::read(text, Ins), the .pcr path.

  • FullProf's list (manual pp. 133–137), as the manual's Fortran reads each format:
  • Ins 0 to 12: free_steps, ill_d1a_d2b_old, ill_d1b_old, ill_d1b_d20, brookhaven, dbws (−4), two_axis, ill_d2b, ill_d4_d20l, psi_dmc, socabim_uxd, xys, variable_time, gsas;
  • Ins 14, isis_tof_banks, from the FullProf News of 24 September 2003: x, y, \(\sigma\) under BANK lines, y and \(\sigma\) times fac_y (1000 by default), divided by the step under RALF. That division is an equation the News page holds as an image lost from our copy; it is taken as x(i+1) − x(i). Believed, not checked.
  • The manual names Ins −4 (DBWS) without a layout, so it is recognised and not read.
  • Ins 9 (Socabim UXD) also has no layout in the manual. It is read as Bruker's UXD keyword file. Believed, not checked.
  • The general table, table (L061): it recognises, or takes from the user, the delimiter (, ; tab or blanks), the decimal mark, the header lines, and the x, y and \(\sigma\) columns (by name or position). x may be 2\(\theta\), d, Q or TOF, from the column's name; when no name says, 2\(\theta\) is assumed and noted. Options are TableOptions, and drs data read --delimiter --decimal --header --x --y --sigma --axis on the command line.
  • GSAS-II's formats, read as its importers (GSASII/imports/G2pwd_*.py, commit fe944cd) read them:
  • Bruker RAW version 1 (recognised; GSAS-II does not read it either), versions 2, 3 and 4, and BRML;
  • PANalytical XRDML;
  • Rigaku RAS, RASX and TXT;
  • xye, qye, Fit2D/pyFAI chi, and powder CIF.

No new dependency: the zip directory, DEFLATE (RFC 1951) and a small XML reader are in the core, 500 lines tested on the synthetic BRML and RASX zips (written by Python's zipfile) against GSAS-II's reading.

  • Recognition by content, then extension. A reader's confidence is:
  • 1, a signature only its format has;
  • 0.8, the whole structure reads with consistent counts;
  • 0.5, loose columns;
  • 0, the file does not read.

A usual extension adds 0.1. A file is identified when the best leads the next by 0.1; otherwise identify returns the candidates. The structure checks needed for real files: (I2,I6) fields are integers with their counters written; ILL's −1000/−10000 end marks; pairs past Tf; PSI's key=value lines after the data.

  • Each scan carries its axis (2\(\theta\), TOF, Q, d, E), its wavelength (λ1, λ2, ratio) and Info: title, instrument, date, sample, temperature, monitor, counting time, step and other keys. A point the reading program gives no weight has \(\sigma\) = ∞.
  • drs data formats, drs data identify <file> (each candidate's content confidence, extension and score), and drs data read <file> [--as <id>] [--scan n] [--out].

Tests against real files (data_formats, five tests; indexes written by make_fullprof_reads.py and make_gsas2_reads.py):

  • fullprof_reads: the 50 data files of manuals/fullprof/ that a single-pattern .prf was drawn from (Ins 0, 1, 3, 6, 8, 10). All 389266 points equal the .prf's 2\(\theta\) and Yobs.
  • FullProf holds Yobs in single precision: 32914.99875 is 32915.0 there and is printed 3292. \(\times 10\).
  • \(\sigma\) is checked on 49 files against the SumwYobsSQ of the .out written with the .prf (Σ w·y\(^{2}\), four digits), with the .pcr's weighting: Iwg = 1 is FullProf's maximum likelihood, w = 1/(Ycal·\(\sigma\)²/y). LaB6rx matched only with it.
  • Ins 0's \(\sigma\)² = max(y, 1), "not checked" since L033, is confirmed.
  • Frozen, five files. Egyptian make-up (3 files, 1.8·\(10^{-4}\)): FullProf keeps the point at 6.70, the end of the excluded region 5.0–6.7. In K2NaAgCN, NdNi and Pd3MnD it leaves out the points at the ends of their regions. The manual has no rule for an end point and these runs do not settle one.
  • Also frozen: the profile-matching tutorial's citric acid (2 files, 2.3·\(10^{-5}\), SumYobs equal). Not explained.
  • The one wavelength read from a header (K2NaAgCN's "lambda=") is the .pcr's.
  • gsas2_reads: GSAS-II's importers, run on the same bytes through its scripting loader, against 48 banks and 219573 points. The files: the GSAS-II tutorials' data files, its tests' PBSO4 files, and FullProf files GSAS-II also reads (Ins 0, 10, 12 and 14). Points, Σx, Σy, Σw, first and last points, and the wavelengths agree to \(10^{-9}\). Departures of GSAS-II's reading, counted and frozen:
  • it keeps NCHAN − 1 points of a STD bank (5 banks);
  • it reads the blank fields of a short line as zero-weight points (3);
  • it reads an ESD bank past its NCHAN (1);
  • it reads a free-format file to x \(\ge\) Tf, one point more than FullProf (1).

Two GSAS-II failures:

  • its RAW reader ends with repeat = False, so its loader reads only the first range of a version 2 or 3 file (Keokuk kaolinite.RAW has two; the reference asks for the second by number);
  • it cannot read FullProf's _prof.cif files: "_diffrn_measurement_device_type?" runs a tag and its value together, which PyCifRW refuses.
  • powder_cif_against_fullprof: those three CIFs, read by powder_cif, are their data files' points: 62542 points, by their point numbers, y and \(\sigma\) to the CIF's printing, 2\(\theta\) less the zero shift.
  • recognition: 182 example files. 165 are identified as their format; 17 are ambiguous with it among the candidates. All 17 are plain columns, which FullProf's Ins = 10, GSAS-II's xye and the table all read.
  • formats_without_example, 20 formats:
  • Checked against GSAS-II's reading of synthetic files (make_synthetic_formats.py, in manuals/gsas2/synthetic/): Bruker RAW 2 and 4 and BRML, XRDML, Rigaku RAS, RASX and TXT, qye, Fit2D chi, and the table.
  • Unit tests only: Ins 2, 4, 5, 7, 9 and 11.
  • Recognised and not read: Bruker RAW 1, DBWS, NeXus/HDF5, MIDAS zarr.
  • The GSAS-II tutorials' files and PBSO4 are in manuals/gsas2/ (22 files, 5 MB), with their source in manuals/README.md.

For Josu:

  • the five frozen \(\sigma\) sums and the end-point question;
  • Ins 9 and Ins 14 read as believed;
  • the GSAS reader follows the BANK record's NCHAN where GSAS-II does not.

L0602026-10-10decision

The app is an extensible umbrella of modules for everything around structure refinement (R20–R23)

Josu redirects the app: not a refinement window but a modern, general Mac app that controls everything around the refinement of crystal structures, as an extensible umbrella of modules. Four requirements are added to the brief:

  • R20, the umbrella: a project is a folder on disk, shown as it is, each file opened by the module that handles it; the app's own state in a hidden .drs/ subfolder. A module declares the file types it opens and the commands, views, sidebar sections and inspector panes it adds, and uses only shared services (project and file index, versions, undo, selection, settings, the parameter dictionary, one plotting component); its computations are in the core with a drs command-line path, so it is testable and scriptable without the app.
  • R21, the data module: every format FullProf and GSAS-II read, recognised by content and then by extension, with readable names instead of FullProf's numeric codes; interactive, non-destructive work on the data on the plot and in a synced table; the usual operations of a data-analysis tool. Not a copy of WinPLOTR.
  • R22, the editor and versions: CodeMirror 6, synced both ways with the app; a history of every control file in .drs/history/<file>/<NNN>_<date>_<time>.<ext> with a sidecar (\(\chi^2\), Rwp, cycles, converged, note), a snapshot before every refinement and on demand, diff and restore; the working file keeps its name, so FullProf can read the same folder.
  • R23, publishing: figures for journals from the same plotting component, crystallographic tables, deposition CIFs, the Bilbao Crystallographic Server's formats; further modules as needed. What exists becomes the Refine module. The app stream reworks app/DESIGN.md around this before building further, and the core's next task is the reader registry for all the formats. Set aside: a single sealed project file with the data copied in (the app stream's first proposal); a document model centred on one refinement; versions as visible numbered files next to the original.
