L0582026-10-10work

FullProf's angle restraints pull at π/180 of their stated weight; a smooth peak window in the default model; the app's requests 1–5

Core 0.14.0. Josu accepted L055's frozen differences and its four decisions (this entry's request).

Restrained refinements against FullProf's runs (L056). FullProf's four restrained refinements each end at their starting point in one cycle: the tutorial files were already at FullProf's minimum. There O2–C1–O5 stands at −4.0\(\sigma\) of its restraint and Ag–C–N at +3.4\(\sigma\). The engine, with the stated weight, moves them to 1.65\(\sigma\) and 1.8\(\sigma\). So FullProf's angle restraints pull less than they say.

  • FullProf's listing states the weight in degrees: its "Sum{W(Ang_obs − Ang_cal)²}/4" is \(\chi^2\) times the mean of (\(\Delta\)/\(\sigma\))², with \(\Delta\) and \(\sigma\) in degrees (sr-ox-test: 11.3 printed; the distances' 1.33 the same way).
  • Scaling the angle rows' weight by π/180 makes FullProf's starting points the engine's minimum. Every restraint of the four runs then agrees with FullProf's within 0.73\(\sigma\). Every refined parameter of the two oxalate runs and of K2Na[Ag(CN)2]3 lies within FullProf's esd (at most 0.83 esd; FullProf's _conv.pcr imported for the comparison).
  • (π/180)² fits as well (0.72\(\sigma\)): both leave the angles nearly unrestrained. A radian derivative against degree residuals would give π/180; this is inferred from the results, not read. FullProf's least-squares code is not public, and CrysFML's restraint cost (cost_functions.f90) is in degrees throughout and has no derivatives.
  • It is FullProf's rule, so it lives in the compatibility layer: Convention::angle_restraints. The import keeps the default, the weight as stated: a .pcr's \(\sigma\) is the user's statement.
  • fullprof_restraint_runs now runs by default and passes with FullProf's rule. Two of these runs' calculated patterns differ from FullProf's at its own values; neither difference is caused by the restraints. Both are frozen:
  • Sr-ox-test: \(\chi^2\) 1.9605 against 1.931. Its Fourier background (18 coefficients) differs from FullProf's by a smooth term of up to 1.85 counts below 60°, constant beyond; Sr-ox-rest by 0.3 counts. Truncating the series to the manual's 12 terms makes it worse.
  • LaMnO3-3t2: 2.2416 against 2.137 (+4.9 %), all of it between 21° and 41°, around the peaks below AsyLim = 35°, with the background exact. The other F_b, H_G in z, and P2 moved or dropped were each tried and each makes it worse; there 7 parameters differ from FullProf's by up to 2.71 esd. make_fullprof_profiles.py now gives the CODE_conv.prf files their own reason: converged refinements, with no _calc pass, compared through \(\chi^2\) at FullProf's values in the tests it names.

A peak window that brings each peak smoothly to zero (L055's open point). The default model calculates each peak to ±Wdt·H as before, then multiplies it by a cosine taper that falls from 1 to 0, with zero slope, by \(\pm\)1.25·Wdt·H (profile::taper; the derivative path applies it as a function of the position and the FWHM). S is now continuous as widths and positions move. FullProf's cut at the edge is Convention::window_edge, and the import keeps it (no exact translation).

  • engine_derivatives: two more passes take the default model at the runs' own Wdt (rutile + anatase, Y2O3), 705 derivatives in all, the worst 5·\(10^{-7}\).
  • esd_profile, at each run's own Wdt with the taper: the two sides agree to 0.010, where FullProf's cut gave Y2O3's W 1.38 and 0.32. The ratios are those of Wdt = 400 to 0.004: Y2O3 0.888, Yb2ZrWO8 0.948, MgAl2O4 0.999, Egypt 1.011, rutile + anatase 0.969.
  • default_convention's table of how far the fully default model is from FullProf's patterns was re-measured: the added tails move most runs, e.g. Yb2ZrWO8 from 3·\(10^{-6}\) to 3.4·\(10^{-3}\).

The app's requests (branch app, app/REQUESTS.md). 1–5 are closed by core 0.14.0; 6–8 stay open.

  • 2: ParameterSet::from_addresses(free, equal, limits, model), with the symmetry applied as in from_pcr, and refinable::field_of, the inverse of address. Errors name the address. Equalities add up, so a field may move with several parameters (the import's P1 + 3·P2). User limits replace the engine's.
  • 3: Model::fields() -> Vec<FieldInfo> (address, dictionary id, value, limits, fixed_by: "crystal system" or "site symmetry"), Model::all_fields, and Model::set_field(field, value) -> Result. The tied values follow: a cubic cell's b and c; the coordinates a site expresses through the free one. The engine's Model::set stays unchecked, so set_field is the request's set.
  • 3, structural edits (module edit): add_phase (setting and cell), add_phase_from_cif, remove_phase (with its restraints), add_atom, remove_atom (refused while a restraint names it), set_background, set_background_terms, add_excluded, remove_excluded, set_shape, set_asymmetry (none, Bérar–Baldinozzi, axial divergence with TCH only), set_preferred_orientation_function, set_preferred_orientation.
  • 4: parameter_table.rs carries every field of every entry, and GROUPS; parameters::dictionary() and groups(). parameter_dictionary checks names, groups and kinds against the YAML.
  • 5: a name for each of the 47 entries ("Caglioti U", "Pseudo-Voigt η₀", "Preferred orientation G₁", …), shown on the Parameters page.
  • 1: Model::to_text and Model::from_text (JSON of the model, space groups as their Hall symbols and operators), behind the serde feature: the crate's first dependency, on by default so that cargo test covers it; --no-default-features builds with none. Tests:
  • model_editing: 1085 values of 11 runs, each address unique and field_of its inverse; from_addresses refines to from_pcr's \(\chi^2\) and values exactly on five runs; a user's limit held; the edits and the ones refused.
  • model_text: 62 imported runs written, read and written again, with identical text, patterns (to the bit) and values.

For Josu:

  • the inferred weight of FullProf's angle restraints (Convention::angle_restraints);
  • the two frozen pattern differences (Sr-ox-test's Fourier background, LaMnO3-3t2 at 21–41°);
  • the default window's taper;
  • set_field in place of the request's Model::set;
  • serde on by default.

L0552026-10-10work

Restraints, Le Bail fits and quantitative phase analysis in the engine, and the esd's confirmed by the \(\chi^2\) profile

Core 0.13.0, phase 2 step 5 with restraints (P40, P35, P33). Josu accepted L053's frozen differences, YVO3's report and its two decisions (this entry's request).

Restraints (P40). The .pcr reader now reads a phase's soft distance and angle constraints (Dis, Ang of line 19; lines 45 and 45-1, manual pp. 123–124; FullProf may write the angle lines' numbers without spaces) and the general linear restraints (NLI on line 7).

  • Module restraints: an atom of a restraint is taken through {R|T}. R comes from FullProf's table of rotations, numbered ITnum: 1–24 in non-hexagonal frames, 25–36 in hexagonal ones, negative for the product with the inversion; T is the whole translation. The table is FullProf's own (p. 123), not its list of the group's operators, so a restraint names an operator exactly.
  • Distances and angles are computed in the cell's metric over the dual numbers, so their derivatives with respect to the cell and the coordinates are exact.
  • Linear restraints Σ c·p \(\approx\) v \(\pm\) \(\sigma\) act on free parameters by name (refinable::LinearRestraint; FullProf's parameter numbers are its codewords').
  • In the engine each restraint is an observation with weight \(\chi^2\)_ν/\(\sigma\)², \(\chi^2\)_ν of the data at the start of the cycle, as FullProf's (restraints tutorial, p. 2). P is not reduced, and Rp, Rwp and \(\chi^2\) remain the data's.
  • drs refine prints each restraint's target, value and \(\Delta\)/\(\sigma\). Test fullprof_restraints:
  • Geometry against FullProf. Each run writes CFML_Restraints_1.tpcr, the structure's distances and angles as restraint lines with the values FullProf calculates. Read as restraints:
  • Yb2ZrWO8 (our run): 4 distances within the print (3·\(10^{-5}\) Å), among them O–O through ITnum −4;
  • LaMnO3 of the symmetry-modes tutorial (positions from 7 mode amplitudes; the core's agree with FullProf's "Final Complete Structure" to its 5 decimals): 23 distances up to 1.6·\(10^{-4}\) Å from FullProf's;
  • LaMnO3 on D20 (la0_190.pcr): 6 distances up to 1.5·\(10^{-4}\) Å and 31 angles up to 0.0135°. The LaMnO3 differences are not explained and are frozen. A wrong convention (ITnum, the central atom, which atom an operator moves) would be off by tenths of an ångström or by degrees.
  • Derivatives against Richardson differences: \(10^{-6}\) relative, or \(10^{-5}\) for k2na1p5's Ag–C–N angle at 0.18°, where acos is steep.
  • Restrained refinements of the three runs that carry restraints:
  • SrHC3O6·H2O (tutorial; two files): 8 restraints, \(\chi^2\) \(1.928 \to 1.9277\); O2–C1–O5 goes from +4.05\(\sigma\) to +1.65\(\sigma\);
  • LaMnO3 on 3T2: 3 restraints, all within 0.03\(\sigma\);
  • K2Na[Ag(CN)2]3: 7 restraints; the Ag–C–N angle goes from +3.4\(\sigma\) to +1.8\(\sigma\). All converge in 3 cycles, and every restraint ends within 3\(\sigma\) or nearer its target.
  • Linear restraint: U + W of Y2O3, added as NLI lines, is held to 0.02\(\sigma\) while both move.
  • FullProf's output of these refinements is not in the material. manuals/fullprof/runs/restraint_lebail_runs.py makes it on the Mac (with the two Le Bail files below), and the ignored test fullprof_restraint_runs compares with it. The restraints tutorial prints a FullProf run of sr-ox-test, but from a state other than the material's file, so it cannot be compared.

Le Bail fits (P35). Module lebail.

  • Every Jbt = 2 phase has its intensities replaced at every cycle by the Rietveld formula (manual eq. 3.15, p. 44): I_obs,h = I_calc,h Σ_i v_hi r_i / Σ_i v_hi, with r_i = (y_i − b_i)/(y_c,i − b_i).
  • With Irf = 0 and no .hkl file, the reflections the group allows are generated with equal intensities.
  • The engine then runs all its cycles, as FullProf does with Dum = 1 (p. 68), which all its Le Bail tutorials set.
  • The intensities are not counted in P: LBF_lmopr's N − P + C is 2461 with P = 8, in FullProf and in the core. A first version summed I·\(\Omega\) over the points without the step and came out 1/step too strong (Rp 750 %). Test fullprof_le_bail:
  • LBF_lmopr (LaMnO3, FullProf's tutorial .out). The engine starts from the values the .out lists before its first cycle and runs its 15 cycles. Every parameter ends within FullProf's esd (at most 0.80); the cell esd's are again twice FullProf's under TCH. \(\chi^2\) after 15 cycles is 1.3630 against 1.420 (frozen −4.0 %): the engine starts from the intensities of LBF_lmopr1.hkl, which FullProf wrote at the end, and FullProf's \(\chi^2\) is still falling by 0.006 per cycle. At FullProf's final values and .hkl intensities the core gives 1.4139 against 1.420 (frozen −0.43 %), and one more extraction gives 1.4080. The .hkl is believed to hold intensities one extraction later than FullProf's last \(\chi^2\); not checked.
  • MgAl2O4s_LeBail: ten extractions from equal intensities, nothing else refined. The header's "global user-weighted \(\chi^2\) (Bragg contrib.)" turns out to be S over all points divided by N − P + C over the points with Bragg contributions (Yb2ZrWO8_conv.out: 5357/3114 = 1.720, printed 1.72; Egypt: 28130/6723 = 4.184, printed 4.18). The core gives 1.8516 against 1.848 (frozen +0.20 %; FullProf's starting intensities and Bragg points are not known).
  • Cocuc_LeBail: its header (26.69) comes from an earlier run than its values (U = 0.086, a = 3.869). The engine's nine cycles (\(\chi^2\) \(21.6 \to 3.15\)) are reported, not compared.

Quantitative phase analysis (P33). Module qpa: W_φ = S·ATZ·V/t over the sum (Hill & Howard, as the manual writes it, p. 31; ATZ, p. 91). The phase carries ATZ and Brindley's t, now in the dictionary (qpa_atz, brindley_coefficient). Brindley's t is 1 in all 154 phases of the material that give it, so its use is unchecked. drs refine prints the fractions. Against FullProf's "Fract(%)", in fullprof_refinements:

  • Egypt's five phases: 39.911, 16.407, 22.020, 4.938, 16.725 % against 39.91, 16.41, 22.01, 4.94, 16.73;
  • rutile + anatase: 77.906 and 22.094 against 77.91 and 22.09;
  • each within 0.07 of FullProf's esd. The esd's differ. FullProf's are reproduced by treating the numerator and the sum as independent, \(\sigma\)²(W) = \(\mathrm{W}^{2}\)[(\(\sigma\)_φ/S_φ)² + Σ_ψ W_ψ²(\(\sigma\)_ψ/S_ψ)²]: from the engine's scale esd's, rutile 0.515 against the printed 0.52 and Egypt's first phase 0.255 against 0.25, all within 2 % and the print. It gives a single phase an esd (Y2O3: 100 \(\pm\) 0.23 %). The core propagates the covariance of scales and cells: 0 for one phase, and rutile \(\pm\)0.190 % for both phases.

Test esd_profile (L054). The test fixes one parameter at its value \(\pm\) its esd, refines the rest from the minimum, and measures ΔS/\(\chi^2\)_ν, which is 1 if the esd is the one the surface supports. There is one parameter of a different kind in each of the five runs that agree with FullProf.

  • At the runs' own Wdt the two sides disagree (Y2O3's W 1.38 and 0.32): S jumps when a peak's window edge crosses a point. With Wdt = 400 the surface is smooth and both sides agree to 0.011.
  • Ratios: Y2O3's W 0.88–0.89, Yb2ZrWO8's cell a 0.95 (the parameter of L054, now symmetric), MgAl2O4's O x 1.00, Egypt's scale 1.01, rutile + anatase's O z 0.97.
  • At \(\pm\)\(\sigma\)/2 each ratio is a quarter of these (Y2O3 0.22), so the surface is quadratic. What is short of 1 is the model's own curvature Σ r w ∂²y, which the Gauss–Newton matrix leaves out: the profile esd is 6 % larger for Y2O3's W and 3 % for Yb2ZrWO8's cell. Frozen per run. FullProf's cell esd's under TCH would give ratios near 4.

Decisions.

  • Weight-fraction esd's propagate the covariance. Set aside: FullProf's formula, which ignores that a phase's scale is also in the sum, and gives a lone phase an uncertainty.
  • Restraints are weighted by \(\chi^2\)_ν, as FullProf's. Set aside: weight 1 (restraints as observations of fixed quality), which pulls harder when the fit is poor.
  • With Le Bail phases the engine runs all its cycles. Set aside: stopping on the other parameters' shifts while the intensities still move.
  • With Irf = 0 and no .hkl, the reflections are generated with equal intensities. FullProf's starting intensities are not documented.

For Josu:

  • run manuals/fullprof/runs/restraint_lebail_runs.py on the Mac (four restraint and two Le Bail references);
  • the frozen differences: the LaMnO3 .tpcr distances (1.6·\(10^{-4}\) Å) and D20 angles (0.0135°); LBF_lmopr (−0.43 % at FullProf's values, −4.0 % after 15 cycles); MgAl2O4s_LeBail (+0.20 %); the esd-profile ratios;
  • the four decisions;
  • an open point for the default model: the peak window makes S discontinuous (the esd profiles), so a window that brings each peak to zero smoothly would help the engine.

0.12.0 reviewed and merged: the engine's esd's are right where FullProf's are half, by the fit's own \(\chi^2\) surface

The engine of L053 was reviewed on the Mac before it was merged into main: all 64 core tests and the app's 3 pass, clippy reports no warnings, build.py runs.

The open question of L053, why the engine's esd's of the cell (and of I_G) are twice FullProf's in every TCH run, was put to a test that uses neither program's covariance: the profile of the fit. On Yb2ZrWO8, the cell a was fixed at its best value plus offsets and everything else refined again with drs refine; the rise of S = Σw(y − y_c)² over its minimum (5353.916, N − P = 3227, \(\chi^2\)_ν = 1.6591) is what an esd predicts, ΔS = \(\chi^2\)_ν (δ/\(\sigma\))²:

  • δ = \(\pm\)0.000082 Å (the engine's esd): ΔS = 1.336 on both sides, where the engine's esd predicts 1.659 and FullProf's (0.000041 Å) 6.636;
  • δ = +0.000164 Å: ΔS = 6.178, against 6.636 and 26.546. The \(\chi^2\) surface, calculated by a model that reproduces FullProf's patterns to \(10^{-5}\), agrees with the engine's esd within 10–20 % (the curvature is not exactly quadratic) and not with FullProf's: in these TCH refinements FullProf reports cell esd's about half of what its own fit supports.

It now counts the files on disk; the index is regenerated (nine "left out" lines, with their reason) and the test passes. L0532026-10-09work

The least-squares engine: named parameters, constraints, limits, Levenberg–Marquardt with analytic derivatives, and FullProf's refinements reproduced

Core 0.12.0, phase 2 step 4 (P37–P39, P42–P44). Restraints (P40), Le Bail and QPA are not part of it.

Derivatives. The peak calculation is generic over a number type (dual::Real): with forward-mode dual numbers it gives Ycal and its exact derivatives in one pass. Per phase, 21 variables are carried: zero, Sycos, Sysin, Ratio, the six cell parameters, U V W X Y I_G η0, G1 G2, B_ov and the scale. The structure factor's derivatives with respect to s and to each atom's x y z, B, Occ and betas are analytic. Model::derivatives reuses the peak selection and windows of calculate. Test engine_derivatives: on ten FullProf runs, every kind of field (592 derivatives, those below \(10^{-7}\) of Ycal counted as zero) is checked against Richardson-extrapolated central differences, to 2·\(10^{-5}\) of the largest derivative; the worst is 6.3·\(10^{-7}\). The dual path reproduces calculate to \(10^{-10}\). The cell needs a step of \(10^{-6}\)·a: a step of \(10^{-4}\) Å moves a peak far enough to change the number of terms of the FCJ quadrature (CrysFML's NTERMS), a jump in the pattern.

Parameters (P37–P39). refinable::ParameterSet::from_pcr turns a .pcr's codewords (C = sign(a)(10p + |a|), manual p. 13; with Aut = 1 a bare multiplier is a parameter of its own) into free parameters:

  • each is named by the dictionary, after the field its codeword was first written on: pattern1.zero_shift, phase1.cell_a, phase2.O.atom_position.z, phase1.berar_baldinozzi.1;
  • a codeword shared by several values is an equality constraint with its multipliers (P38);
  • the cell is constrained by the crystal system (monoclinic: the unique axis from the 2-fold or the mirror);
  • the import's translations are applied to the multipliers: X-ray scale \(\times 2\), P1 + 3P2, the axial pair, −G1. Refused, with the reason: a coded wavelength, Str1–3, LorSiz, Shape1 of a non-pV phase, and VARY keywords other than ratio. The limits (P39) are in refinable::limits and in the dictionary's new limits field (on the "Parameters" page):
  • \(\ge 0\): scale, B, Occ, I_G, Y, S/L, D/L and the wavelength ratio, and U and X of a TCH phase over a resolution file (there they are the sample's alone);
  • G2 within 0–1; March's G1 \(\ge\) \(10^{-3}\); cell lengths \(\ge\) \(10^{-3}\) Å. The test parameter_dictionary now also checks that every field the engine refines carries a refinable id of the dictionary, and that the dictionary's limits match the engine's.

Engine (P42). lsq::refine does Levenberg–Marquardt on the normal matrix scaled to unit diagonal, solved by a Jacobi eigen-decomposition (the SVD of a symmetric matrix), with eigenvalues below \(10^{-12}\)·λ_max left out and counted. Each cycle tries Gauss–Newton (μ = 0) first; if \(\chi^2\) rises, μ = \(10^{-3}\) and \(\times 10\) up to \(10^{8}\), then ÷10 after each accepted step. A step that would take a field past its limit is cut back to the bound for the parameter that alone moves it, and that parameter is held (left out of the solve) while the gradient pushes it out. It stops when every |shift| is below Eps·esd, the .pcr's Eps, as FullProf does (p. 76). Weights follow Iwg, the maximum-likelihood ones recomputed each cycle. Every cycle is an event (lsq::Cycle, Q5): values, shifts and esd's, Ycal, the statistics, μ, and the singular directions.

Statistics (P43, P44). Rp, Rwp, Rexp, \(\chi^2\) over N − P (+ C, which is 0: constraints reduce P), the Durbin–Watson d of the weighted residuals, and R_Bragg and R_F per phase with Rietveld's apportioning of the observed counts. Then the covariance, esd's and correlations, and the esd of any field through its multipliers (√(mᵀCm)). The esd is \(\sigma\)_k = √(C_kk \(\chi^2\)) with \(\chi^2\) over all the points used. FullProf takes \(\chi^2\) over the points with Bragg contributions (manual eqs. 3.7–3.8), so its esd's are larger by √(\(\chi^2\)_Bragg/\(\chi^2\)_all). Measured:

  • LaB6: 0.912 against √(12.86/15.47) = 0.9118;
  • rutile + anatase: 0.982 against 0.9825.

drs refine <file.pcr> [--cycles N] [--data <file>] imports the .pcr, refines its coded parameters and prints:

  • R-factors, \(\chi^2\), DW, μ and the largest shift/esd per cycle;
  • each parameter with its esd, and whether it is at its limit or outside its limits;
  • R_Bragg and R_F per phase;
  • the correlations above 0.8.

Test fullprof_refinements. The nine runs of L052 are refined from their starting files (CODE.pcr.in, imported), with FullProf's reflection limits and data. Each is compared with CODE_conv. Agreement in five runs:

  • Y2O3 (72 parameters): \(\chi^2\) 1.1985 against FullProf's 1.1990;
  • Yb2ZrWO8 (24): 1.6599 against 1.6600;
  • MgAl2O4 (45): 1.3823 against 1.3810;
  • the Egyptian make-up (44): 4.1445 against 4.1450;
  • rutile + anatase (20): 1.3387 against 1.3390. In all five every refined parameter is within FullProf's esd (the largest: Y2O3's zero, 0.997 esd), and N − P is FullProf's N − P + C. Rp agrees within 0.01, Rwp within FullProf's printed digits, DW within 0.001. R_Bragg agrees within 0.06, except MgAl2O4's MgO (10.84 against 10.5), not investigated. The engine converges in 2–4 cycles; FullProf took up to 11. \(\chi^2\) is compared within the tolerance of fullprof_profiles (5·\(10^{-4}\) + 0.15 %).

Differences found, explained where possible, frozen in the test:

  • FullProf's peak window changes with the number of cycles. In rutile + anatase's 11-cycle run, FullProf's .prf at the converged values matches windows of Wdt·H, H with the strain term X tan \(\theta\) (Σw(y − yc)² 10552.2; the core with Wdt·H gives 10551.5, and with the 1-cycle rule Wdt·H_noX of L041, 10810.3). The test refines that run with the default model's window, Wdt·H. In LaB6, FullProf's own .prf files of the 1-cycle and the 8-cycle run, from identical parameter values, give 107888 and 108157: the window differs, and neither rule reproduces the second. LaB6's \(\chi^2\) at FullProf's values is 12.942 against 12.86 (frozen 0.64 %). Its refined \(\chi^2\) is 12.891, and its cell is 1.29 FullProf esd's away (0.71 of the engine's). Rule not found.
  • Co-YBCO: FullProf's last point is not a minimum. The function agrees there (\(\chi^2\) 1.5239 against 1.524). The engine lowers it to 1.4833 from the start and to 1.4834 from FullProf's values. 17 parameters then differ by more than FullProf's esd (cell a by 25.6; zero, U V W, S/L). FullProf's own cycles show \(\chi^2\) climbing from 1.56 to 1.74 and still falling by 0.01 per cycle when it stopped (L052).
  • The esd's of the cell parameters, and of I_G (Yb2ZrWO8), are twice FullProf's (after the scaling above: q = 1.96–2.01) in every TCH run (Npr = 7). In Y2O3, the one pseudo-Voigt run, they agree (q = 1.001). The engine's derivative is checked against finite differences of a pattern that reproduces FullProf's (at Yb2ZrWO8's values, Σw(∂y/∂a)² agrees to \(10^{-6}\)). Why FullProf prints half is not known.
  • Seven other esd's are frozen by name, each with its measured q:
  • the end points of linear-interpolation backgrounds: Y2O3 0 and 61; Yb2ZrWO8 0 and 1; MgAl2O4 0 and 31; q from 0.67 to 1.25;
  • LaB6's wavelength ratio and scale (VARY ratio): q 0.56 and 0.48. Cause not found.
  • The Egyptian make-up: FullProf's N − P + C is one more than the points it fits (7001 points, 172 excluded, 44 parameters: 6785; FullProf 6786). Believed to be the last point (75.00°), excluded but counted; not checked.
  • NdNi-HTO: its magnetic phase is not calculated (FullProf's 42 parameters, the engine's 41), so it is not comparable (\(\chi^2\) at FullProf's values 26.0); frozen.
  • YVO3, as asked, reported without forcing agreement. FullProf does not converge in 100 cycles (L052). The engine converges in 6 cycles:
  • U is held at its limit 0, where FullProf takes it to −0.091 with an esd of 3·\(10^{-10}\);
  • Y goes to 0.0065 \(\pm\) 0.0037 (FullProf 9·\(10^{-5}\)), and X to 0.654 (FullProf 0.857);
  • \(\chi^2\) 2.29 against FullProf's 3.68 at its last values; FullProf's last values give 3.77 in the core (the window of L039).

Decisions.

  • esd's are scaled by \(\chi^2\) over all the points used, not FullProf's Bragg points. It is the standard \(\chi^2\)_ν of the fit; FullProf's choice is documented, and the ratio is predictable. Set aside: copying FullProf's scaling.
  • A value read outside its limits (LaB6's B Biso −0.148 in the tutorial) is not moved onto the limit; it may only move towards it, and drs refine says it is outside. Set aside: clamping on import, which would change the user's model silently.
  • The rutile + anatase comparison uses the default window, which is what FullProf's last cycles use there. The other runs keep the import's window, which FullProf's 1-cycle runs use. No run-history rule is built into the core until it is understood.

For Josu to accept:

  • the frozen differences above (LaB6's window, Co-YBCO, the cell and I_G esd's with TCH, seven background, ratio and scale esd's, Egypt's N count, NdNi's magnetic phase);
  • YVO3's report;
  • the two decisions on esd scaling and out-of-limit values.

L0502026-10-09work

The papers decide the defaults, and a .pcr is translated into the default model on import

Core 0.11.0, with the six papers of manuals/papers/ read.

The papers against each rule (page and equation):

  • Bérar & Baldinozzi (1993), pp. 128–129. The paper writes g = g₀[1 + A(\(\theta\))F_a(dθ/w) + B(\(\theta\))F_b(dθ/w)] (eq. 14), with F_a = 2z exp(−z\(^{2}\)) (eq. 12), F_b = (8z\(^{3}\) − 12z) exp(−z\(^{2}\)) (eq. 13) and A(\(\theta\)) = A₀/tan \(\theta\) + A₁/tan 2\(\theta\) (eq. 15). Its dθ is positive on the low-angle side: the transfer functions of eqs. 1–3 (p. 128) are nonzero for dθ > 0, and axial divergence sends intensity only below the Bragg angle (Finger, Cox & Jephcoat, p. 895). So:
  • the sign of z is FullProf's; the manual's eq. 3.29 prints the opposite, and L046's provisional default (the manual's) was wrong there;
  • the paper's F_b is the manual's 2(2z\(^{2}\) − 3)F_a. FullProf's (4z\(^{2}\) − 3)F_a is not the paper's: it is the paper's + 3F_a. Default now: the paper's sign and F_b. FullProf's F_b stays behind Convention::berar_baldinozzi.
  • Finger, Cox & Jephcoat (1994), pp. 893–895. Their S and H are the sample and detector half-heights at distance L. 2φ_min (eq. 4), 2φ_infl (eq. 5) and the Gauss–Legendre sum (eq. 9) are what the core's port of CrysFML's Prof_Val computes with S/L and H/L as given. CrysFML (lines 1289–1294) also reads a pair with D_L < 0.0002 as (H/L + S/L, H/L − S/L), so S_L alone means S/L = H/L = S_L/2. The paper has no such rule. Default: the pair as given; CrysFML's reading stays behind Convention::axial_divergence.
  • Thompson, Cox & Hastings (1987), pp. 80–82. The pseudo-Voigt (eq. 1), η (eq. 2) and H (eq. 3) are the core's, coefficient for coefficient. The paper's widths are F_G = V tan \(\theta\) (with the general Caglioti form, eq. 4) and F_L = X/cos \(\theta\) (eqs. 8a–8b): its X is FullProf's Y. FullProf's X tan \(\theta\) comes from the microstructure paper (eq. 19). No change.
  • Caglioti, Paoletti & Ricci (1958), pp. 224–225. Eq. 11 gives the squared Gaussian FWHM as a quadratic in a = tan \(\theta\)/tan \(\theta\)_M (eq. 5), the U tan\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) + V tan \(\theta\) + W form. No change.
  • Dollase (1986), p. 268. Eq. 4 sums P over the m symmetry-equivalent planes, which is the average L042 found in FullProf. Table 1, no. 6 is March's (r\(^{2}\) cos\(^{2}\)α + sin\(^{2}\)α/r)^(−3/2), FullProf's eq. 3.32. Table 1, no. 5 is the exponential b + (1 − b) exp(−Gα²); FullProf's eq. 3.31 writes exp(+G1 α²). Default: Dollase's sign; FullProf's stays behind Convention::preferred_orientation.
  • March (1932), p. 291. The pole density of an affine deformation is the cube of the deformation ellipsoid's radius (ρ_max/ρ_min = (a/c)³); its axial case is Dollase's no. 6. No change. The X-ray Lorentz–polarisation is set by none of these papers: the default stays the manual's eq. 3.26.

Convention now goes rule by rule: eight switches (Lorentz–polarisation, Bérar–Baldinozzi, axial divergence, exponential preferred orientation, excluded points, peak window, reflections past the data, microstructure output), with DEFAULT and FULLPROF.

Importing a .pcr (L047). Model::from_pcr (used by drs calc and the app) translates the values, refined under FullProf's rules, into the default model. Each translation is exact:

  • X-ray scale \(\times 2\) (Lp, Ilo = 0, 1, 4; profile-matching intensities carry no Lp);
  • (P1, P3) + 3(P2, P4) (Bérar–Baldinozzi);
  • the axial pair through CrysFML's reading;
  • G1 → −G1 for the exponential preferred orientation;
  • the excluded regions snapped to the points FullProf leaves out, on the data's grid. Two rules have no exact translation, so FullProf's are kept and listed: the peak window (a width without X tan \(\theta\) and an extra low point) and the reflections generated past the data (FullProf's undocumented limit). The microstructure output convention has no effect on the pattern and is set to the default. Model::from_pcr_fullprof keeps the values as written under all of FullProf's rules; the FullProf tests and drs calc --fullprof use it. The app now opens a .pcr through the translated import and says which FullProf rules it keeps. Its code changed, but it could not be built here (no GTK/WebKit in the cloud container); its test expects Y2O3's \(\chi^2\) between 1.0 and 1.5, and the import gives 1.192154.

Test default_convention (pcr_import). Every FullProf run of manuals/fullprof/runs/ was imported: the ten runs, the preferred-orientation run and the 35 window experiments, 47 in all. Each gives the same \(\chi^2\) as the .pcr's values under FullProf's rules, over the same points and weights: |\(\Delta\)\(\chi^2\)| = 0 in all 47. NdNi-LTOa, NdNi-LTOb and Pd3MnD are refused at import as version 2 (anisotropic size and strain). What the two kept rules explain, as |\(\Delta\)\(\chi^2\)|/\(\chi^2\) when they are also switched to the default (frozen per run):

  • YVO3: 5.5·\(10^{-2}\);
  • LaB6: 3.0·\(10^{-2}\);
  • rutile + anatase: 2.4·\(10^{-2}\);
  • NdNi-HTO: 3.4·\(10^{-3}\);
  • Co-YBCO: 4.0·\(10^{-4}\);
  • MgAl2O4: 2.3·\(10^{-4}\);
  • Yb2ZrWO8-PO-March: 1.1·\(10^{-4}\);
  • Y2O3: 1.0·\(10^{-4}\);
  • Yb2ZrWO8: 3.4·\(10^{-6}\);
  • Egypt: 3.4·\(10^{-7}\);
  • the window experiments: 0 to 0.93. The window experiments are modified copies, not fits; at Wdt = 4 FullProf's window cuts the Lorentzian tails, and there the default fits better (yvo3_base_w4: \(\chi^2\) \(34.8 \to 3.2\)). The part of default_convention that checks rules, default_rules, checks the paper's B&B formula and the identity FullProf(P1, P2) = paper(P1 + 3P2, P2) to \(10^{-14}\).

The parameter dictionary was updated accordingly. Each paper's checked now cites its PDF in manuals/papers/ with page and equation, and the references carry their DOIs. The asymmetry and preferred-orientation entries carry the papers' equations, and every rule's note says how a .pcr's value is translated.

For Josu: the two rules kept from FullProf on import (peak window, reflections past the data) are the open compatibility points; L048 accepted their frozen differences. The least-squares engine is not started.

L0492026-10-09work

The app's walking skeleton: a Tauri window that opens a .pcr and draws the core's pattern

The first build of the app (app 0.1.0), as L044 planned, on the Mac: a Tauri 2 window with the core linked as a library (no server, no Python). Its one command, open_pcr, reads a .pcr and its data through the core as drs calc does and returns the pattern, the reflections of each phase, the excluded regions and Rp, Rwp, \(\chi^2\). The window, plain HTML and JavaScript on a canvas with no framework and no network, draws Yobs, Ycal, the difference, a row of reflection markers per phase (λ2 fainter) and the excluded regions shaded, with zoom, pan and a readout under the cursor; light and dark follow the system. The bundle diff-ref-struct.app is 9.6 MB. Checked: three tests (a reference run opened through the command; a missing file gives a message), clippy clean; the drawing rendered in Chrome on Y2O3, rutile + anatase and the five-phase Egyptian make-up from what the command returns. Not checked by hand: the app's own window and its file dialog. The first drawing showed the calculated curve falling in Egypt's excluded region at 5–6.7°, where the profile is the background alone by design; the regions are now shaded and the difference is left out there.

The parameter dictionary and the default/FullProf split of L046 were reviewed on the Mac before they were merged: all 58 core tests pass, clippy reports no warnings, build.py writes the dictionary page and the generated table. Found in the review:

  • A .pcr read under the default convention keeps FullProf's refined values, but the default X-ray Lorentz–polarisation is half of FullProf's, so the fit is lost: Y2O3's \(\chi^2\) goes from 1.19 to 14.4 (the app's test caught it). L0462026-10-09work

The parameter dictionary, and the default model split from FullProf's rules; the papers themselves still unread

Two items of phase 2 (L043, L044), core 0.10.0.

What could not be done. The original papers could not be read. The session's network policy refused journals.iucr.org, doi.org, api.crossref.org and arxiv.org, and the repository holds none of the papers (only FullProf's manuals, tutorials and the microstructure paper). So:

  • each paper's full reference was checked against bibliographic records found by web search, linked in the dictionary; their equations were not read;
  • two page ranges disagree between sources and are left marked: Thompson, Cox & Hastings (1987), 79–83 in FullProf's manual and GSAS-II, 84–89 in an IUCr issue listing; Dollase (1986), 267–272 in most bibliographies, 273–274 in the listing;
  • March (1932) was not found, and is cited only through Dollase. The comparison of L036–L042's rules "with the original paper" is therefore made with FullProf's manual and with the microstructure paper, which are in the repository. Where FullProf departs from them, the core's default now follows the manual. Each such default is marked as not yet checked against its paper.

The default model and the FullProf convention (powder::Convention). A model read from a .pcr follows the manual. Convention::FullProf keeps FullProf's rules: the FullProf tests set it, and so do drs calc, drs simulate and drs mic with --fullprof. Rule by rule:

  • X-ray Lorentz–polarisation. Manual eq. 3.26 (p. 53) with K = ½ gives ½(1 + Cthm cos\(^{2}\)2\(\theta\))/(2 sin\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) cos \(\theta\)); FullProf computes twice that (L033). Default: eq. 3.26. A factor the scale absorbs: a default model's scale is twice FullProf's.
  • Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry. Manual eq. 3.29 (p. 54) prints z = (2\(\theta\)_i − 2\(\theta\)_h)/H and F_b = 2(2z\(^{2}\) − 3)F_a; FullProf computes −z and (4z\(^{2}\) − 3)F_a (L035). Default: eq. 3.29 as printed. The paper (Bérar & Baldinozzi 1993, J. Appl. Cryst. 26, 128) decides, under L043, and was not read: this default is provisional.
  • The peak window. The manual (p. 75) sets the profile to zero beyond Wdt × FWHM. FullProf counts the width without X tan \(\theta\) for TCH (L035) and takes one more low point when X ≠ 0 (L042). Default: ±Wdt·H, the points within it.
  • Reflections past the data. FullProf generates none below the first point and stops at a limit it does not document (L035). Default: every reflection whose window reaches the data, on either side.
  • Excluded regions. FullProf also drops a point whose step reaches into a region (L033). Default: the points within the regions.
  • Microstructure. The microstructure paper's eq. 14 (manuals/fullprof/docs/Microstructural_effects.pdf, p. 3) takes β* = β cos \(\theta\)/λ with the reflection's own λ; FullProf uses λ1 for the λ2 reflections (L039). Default: the reflection's λ. FullProf's √|U| for a negative U (L039) becomes "no strain" in the default.

Not changed, with the reason:

  • The March–Dollase average over equivalent reflections (L042) is not a FullProf departure: GSAS-II computes it the same way (GSASIIstrMath.py GetPrefOri, lines 3066–3078, commit 305a159), and the manual is silent.
  • Widths at the Bragg angle without Zero (L039): the manual's \(\theta\) is the reflection's.
  • Bérar–Baldinozzi on top of FCJ (L042): the manual (pp. 109, 112) does not make them exclusive.
  • The parametric resolution file's WAVE, CTHM and ASYM (L042): rules of reading FullProf's input; the 2001 manual does not describe those lines.
  • The .prf background offset and the background-only last point: they belong to reading FullProf's output and stay in the tests.

How far the default lies from FullProf's convention on the runs, as the peak part's rms and maximum over its largest value, with the Lp factor 2 taken out (test default_convention, frozen):

  • Y2O3: 1.3·\(10^{-3}\) and 4.5·\(10^{-2}\), at 29°;
  • Yb2ZrWO8: 1.7·\(10^{-3}\) and 2.9·\(10^{-2}\), at 29.6°;
  • every other run: at most 8.7·\(10^{-4}\) and 3.2·\(10^{-3}\). The largest differences sit below AsyLim: the Bérar–Baldinozzi sign. All FullProf tests pass unchanged under Convention::FullProf.

The parameter dictionary (ecosystem/data/parameters.yml), 45 entries, 39 of them refinable. Each entry gives:

  • a readable id (sample_displacement), the symbol, the unit and the meaning;
  • the equation in LaTeX, cited to the page where it is printed, with its paper;
  • FullProf's name and .pcr line;
  • GSAS-II's name, cited to its variable table (GSASIIobj.py lines 613–676, commit 305a159);
  • the range, measured on the 240 .pcr files of manuals/fullprof/ that the core reads, the window experiments left out;
  • the default, and when to refine, after the manual's sequence (p. 14). tools/build.py renders it as the manual page "Parameters: the theory" and writes the core's parameter_table.rs. The new test parameter_dictionary fails when a field of Model, Phase or Atom is neither mapped to an id nor named as no parameter (checked by removing Phase.y), and when the table is out of date. Found while writing it: GSAS-II's Lorentzian width is Z + X/cos \(\theta\) + Y tan \(\theta\) (GSASIIpwd.py line 929), so its X and Y are FullProf's Y and X; and its U, V, W give a Gaussian variance in centidegrees\(^{2}\), not H\(^{2}\).

For Josu:

  • To finish the comparison with the papers, the PDFs of the papers below could be added to the repository (for example manuals/papers/), or journals.iucr.org and doi.org allowed in the environment's network settings: Bérar & Baldinozzi 1993, Dollase 1986, March 1932, Thompson, Cox & Hastings 1987, Finger, Cox & Jephcoat 1994, Caglioti, Paoletti & Ricci 1958.
  • The Bérar–Baldinozzi default is the one that matters (up to 4.5% of a peak). It stays provisional until that paper is read. The least-squares engine is not started.

L0452026-10-09decision

The licence opened as design question Q7: the parts ported from CrysFML are LGPL-3

Several parts of the core are ported from CrysFML, which is under the LGPL-3: the Finger–Cox–Jephcoat profile (Prof_Val), the choice of unique reflections (Hkl_Uni, Asu_Hkl), the scattering tables and the profile range (Locate). A translation into Rust is a derivative work. While the repository is private nothing follows; a distributed app brings the LGPL's obligations for those parts. Opened as Q7 on the Design page, to be decided before any public release. The options: an LGPL-compatible licence for the project; keeping it private and undistributed; or rewriting those parts from the published papers instead of from CrysFML's code. Until then, every port from CrysFML keeps naming its source file and commit, so that the parts concerned can be found.

conversation of 9 October 2026 · Design, Q7 · core/README.md
L0442026-10-09decision

A parameter dictionary as the single source of names and theory; an early walking skeleton of the app

Two items added to the roadmap with Josu:

  • The parameter dictionary (phase 2, before our own control file): one entry per parameter with a readable name (sample_displacement, not Sycos), symbol and unit, its meaning in a sentence, the equation in LaTeX with the paper it comes from, the FullProf and GSAS-II equivalents, the usual range, the default and when to refine it. The core's names, the app's tooltips, the editor's hover and completion, and the theory part of the manual are all generated from it, so they cannot drift apart. FullProf's cryptic names and the missing theory behind its parameters are what this is meant to fix.
  • A walking skeleton of the app (phase 3, started early, alongside phase 2): a Tauri window that opens a .pcr, calls the core as a library, with no server, and draws Yobs, Ycal, the difference and the reflection markers. It tests the join between the Rust core and Tauri while that is still cheap to change, and is built on the Mac, since a cloud session cannot build the app. Set aside: names and help texts written separately in each place; waiting for phase 3 to join core and app.

L0432026-10-09decision

FullProf and GSAS-II are references for results, not templates; GSAS-II installed on the Mac

Josu's concern, made a rule (CLAUDE.md; design question Q8): the aim is to obtain the results FullProf and GSAS-II obtain, with our own core, not to copy either program. So:

  • the core's own model is the default: parameters with physical names, equations taken from the original papers, algorithms chosen on their merits (a peak window set by an accuracy target, for example);
  • what FullProf does differently from its manual or from the papers (the sign and F_b of the Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry, the peak window and its extra low point, the .prf background offset, the March–Dollase average) lives in a FullProf-compatibility layer, documented and tested, used to validate against FullProf and to import .pcr files;
  • where FullProf departs from its manual, the original paper decides the default. Each rule accepted in L036–L042 is to be checked against its paper (roadmap, phase 2).

GSAS-II is the second reference, for refinement results above all. It was installed on the Mac on the same day from the GSAS-II team's installer for Intel Macs (gsas2main-Latest-MacOSX-x86_64.sh, GSAS-II-buildtools v1.0.1, GSAS-II version 5843) into ~/Applications/GSAS-II, apart from the repository; its scripting interface (GSASIIscriptable) was checked to run. conda-forge has no build for Intel Macs. Set aside: matching FullProf's numbers for their own sake; FullProf as the only reference.

L0422026-10-09finding

The peak window: CrysFML's low edge, the parametric resolution file and March–Dollase found; YVO3 at Wdt = 40 still open

The 35 FullProf runs of manuals/fullprof/runs/window_experiments/ (L041) and Yb2ZrWO8-PO-March are now in fullprof_profiles as exact comparisons: 53 files compared (17 before), all passing; 23 of the experiments with no point set apart, 12 with 1–3 window-edge points counted and frozen. Core 0.9.0.

The window. CrysFML sets a profile range with Locate, which returns j with V(j) \(\le\) x < V(j+1) (deps-pycrysfml 2258e77, CFML/Testing/nFP/Src/nFP_Reflections.f90 lines 205–208, pos_left = pt - fwhm*wdt, ptr(1) = locate(xp, pos_left); CFML/Src/CFML_Maths/Math_Locate.f90 lines 64–68). So the low side starts one point below 2\(\theta\) − Wdt·H and the high side ends at the last point at or below 2\(\theta\) + Wdt·H. FullProf's windows show this extra low point for TCH phases with X ≠ 0 and not for X = 0. Without it, every Yb2ZrWO8 experiment with X ≠ 0 misses exactly one low point per peak (16 per file), and the YVO3 Wdt = 4 experiments miss 230–469. With it they all agree. The X = 0 experiments, PPH3, citric acid, Y2O3 and Yb2ZrWO8 agree to \(10^{-5}\) without it and worse with it. Why X decides it is not known. LaB6's window-edge points fall from 50 to 2.

L041 revised. The "shorter high side" with a parametric resolution file came from two inputs the measurement did not know FullProf takes from that file:

  • its WAVE and CTHM lines replace the .pcr's wavelengths, Ratio and Cthm. In yb_irf1_x0 the .pcr gives 1.5406, 1.5406, 1, 0.7899, and the .out prints 1.54056, 1.54440, 0.4374, 0.8000;
  • its ASYM S_L, D_L is added to the phase's: in yb_all_x0, 0.0637 + 0.0637. With either alone the profile is up to 53% off; with the sum it agrees to \(10^{-5}\). FullProf also applies the phase's Bérar–Baldinozzi factor on top of the FCJ profile when a phase has both: in yb_irf1_x0 (Asy1 = 0.00901 and the file's S_L) FCJ alone gives 2.9·\(10^{-2}\), the product \(10^{-5}\). With these, the window is the symmetric Wdt × H_noX, and all ten parametric-file experiments agree to rms \(\le 2.3\)·\(10^{-5}\).

The test also had to change in two ways:

  • the tick marks of the experiments use the Zero after the cycle, read from the .out, because their .pcr was not rewritten;
  • FullProf marks the λ2 positions even with Ratio = 0 (yvo3_noK2: 479 marks). find_file now also searches the grandparent and its folders, because the experiments name the data of runs/.

Preferred orientation. FullProf's P is the March–Dollase factor (eq. 3.32) averaged over the reflections equivalent to h, not taken at h alone. In Yb2ZrWO8-PO-March, G1 = 0.85 about [1 1 0]: P(2 2 0) = CORR/CORR without PO = 1.010306; the average over the 12 equivalents is 1.010305, h alone gives 1.628. All 16 reflections agree to \(10^{-6}\). The run is added to fullprof_corrections (1707 reflections of 11 runs) and its profile to fullprof_profiles (rms 3.6·\(10^{-5}\), \(\chi^2\) 1.7040 against FullProf's 1.704). The same average is assumed for the exponential form, which has no reference.

Not found, frozen in fullprof_profiles (count and size; the test fails if either grows), for Josu:

  • YVO3 itself at Wdt = 40 (YVO3a_calc.prf): still rms 2.34·\(10^{-2}\), max 0.193, 4005 window-edge points. The same YVO3 at Wdt = 4 agrees to rms 5·\(10^{-6}\) but for one point, so the difference comes with the wide window. A low side of Wdt·H with X and a high side of Wdt·H_noX gives rms 1.9·\(10^{-2}\) and leaves a smooth offset that grows with angle.
  • Rutile + anatase (106 points, up to 0.127; before 106 up to 0.082) and MgAl2O4 (42 up to 0.025; before 47 up to 0.014): FullProf's low edge lies between the bare edge and the extra point. In rutile 110 at 27.46°, the point at 26.93°, 0.59 step beyond, is left out, where the experiments take points up to 0.97 step beyond.
  • 15 points in 12 experiments: the high side of the lowest-angle reflection, always inside the asymmetry range (Yb2ZrWO8 111 below AsyLim with Bérar–Baldinozzi, YVO3 101 and LaB6 100 with FCJ; LaB6's 2 points are of this kind). Two more are at 139–141° from peaks past the data's end (w8_x0.645). The least-squares engine is not started: YVO3 is still frozen.

L0412026-10-09finding

Where FullProf cuts a peak: symmetric Wdt × the width without X·tanθ, except with a parametric resolution file

FullProf was run on modified copies of the refined Yb2ZrWO8 run, one parameter at a time, and each isolated window measured in its .prf (scripts, results and method in manuals/fullprof/runs/window_experiments/):

  • With a tabulated resolution file, the half-window is Wdt × the TCH width computed without the phase's X·tanθ. It stays the same from X = 0.1 to 1.5 while the peak width triples, and holds from Wdt = 4 to 40 (with X = 0.645: 0.0750·Wdt against 0.0753 at 72.9°, 0.1504 against 0.1510 at 139.9°). With X ≠ 0 the low-angle edge reaches one step further.
  • The Kα2 line and the Finger–Cox–Jephcoat asymmetry leave the rule as it is; with the asymmetry FullProf's marker moves, not the window.
  • With YVO3's parametric resolution file (Res = 1, Xins = 0.0402) the window is no longer symmetric. Its low-angle side is Wdt × that width (1.008 to 1.05 of it from 140° to 30°), its high-angle side shorter, from 0.92 of it at 29.8° to 0.79 at 139.9°. The core's symmetric window cannot reproduce that: the likely cause of YVO3's differences. The rule for the high-angle side is still to be fitted.

Also made: a preferred-orientation reference, Yb2ZrWO8-PO-March (March–Dollase about [1 1 0], G1 = 0.85), since no tutorial run uses preferred orientation. The exponential function gave none: this FullProf build reads its G1 (0.30, −0.30), lists 0.0000 and calculates the pattern without preferred orientation.

L0392026-10-09work

Phase 2, step 3: isotropic size and strain, the corrections checked on the runs, four runs of tutorial 6 in the profile test

Size and strain (P24). New module microstructure and drs mic: per reflection, what FullProf writes in its .mic file. The sources are:

  • the sample's widths of eq. 19 of the microstructure paper (manuals/fullprof/docs/Microstructural_effects.pdf, p. 4), added to the resolution file's;
  • the split into size (√I_G/cos \(\theta\), Y/cos \(\theta\)) and strain (√U tan \(\theta\), X tan \(\theta\)) that the .mic header states;
  • the TCH (H, η) (eqs. 9–10, p. 2) and the integral breadth of the pseudo-Voigt (eq. 7);
  • apparent size D = 1/β* (Scherrer, eq. 14) and maximum strain e = ½β*d (eq. 17).

CrysFML08's microstructure routines (deps-pycrysfml commit 2258e77, Pow_MicroStructure.f90) cover only the anisotropic models. Found on the runs:

  • β* is taken with λ1 for the λ2 reflections too (strains larger by λ2/λ1, 61.0327 against 60.8810 in YVO3).
  • A negative U enters as √|U|.
  • Where the resolution file's H_G\(^{2}\) is negative (NdNi-HTO above 150°), FullProf prints HGi and betaG as NaN and Beta_TCH as 0.
  • FullProf prints the apparent sizes and strains of the values after its cycle, and the widths and breadths of those it read. YVO3's cycle moves U from 0 to −0.0914 and X from 0.645 to 0.821; its printed strain 60.8810 is that of the shifted values, where the values read give 44.2415.

LorSiz and the size and strain models are anisotropic: version 2 (P25), refused with a message.

Widths at the Bragg angle. FullProf evaluates the widths at the Bragg angle, without Zero and the displacement terms.

  • With this, its .out widths (Hw, HwG, HwL, ETA) are reproduced to 2·\(10^{-5}\), against up to 2.6·\(10^{-3}\) at the shifted angle (YVO3, Zero 0.0555°).
  • It also brought Co-YBCO from rms 4.8·\(10^{-4}\) to 1.5·\(10^{-4}\).
  • It cut the window-edge points kept apart in L035 from 181 to 106 (rutile + anatase) and from 49 to 47 (MgAl2O4); their freeze is tightened to these numbers.

Corrections (P27–P29). Found on the runs: the CORR column of FullProf's reflection lists is m · Lp · A · P, times Ratio for λ2.

  • New test fullprof_corrections compares it for 1691 reflections of 10 runs, all within the print of d and CORR.
  • It covers X-ray Lp with and without a monochromator, neutron Lp, λ2, and cylinder absorption with μR = 0.386 (the NdNi runs; without absorption the product would be off by 5–6%).
  • No run uses preferred orientation (Pref1 is neutral in every phase), so it is not checked.
  • The surface micro-absorption (line 15) is used by no run and is now refused with a message, where it was silently ignored.
  • Y2O3's pseudo-Voigt list prints CORR = 1 and is left out.

Weights. Iwg is now read: maximum likelihood (Iwg = 1, manual p. 43) weights by 1/\(\sigma\)²_calc. LaB6's \(\chi^2\) becomes 12.9486 against FullProf's 12.94 (with 1/\(\sigma\)²: 15.35).

Tests:

  • fullprof_microstructure:
  • the .out widths of 9 isotropic runs, 2710 reflections, at the Bragg angle of FullProf's printed d;
  • every row of the .mic files of YVO3 (468), Yb2ZrWO8 (16) and NdNi-HTO (204, nuclear phase): widths and breadths against CODE.pcr, size and strain against CODE_calc.pcr, within the print of its parameters.
  • fullprof_profiles:
  • Yb2ZrWO8 is exact (rms 3.4·\(10^{-5}\), \(\chi^2\) 1.6600 against 1.66).
  • LaB6 is exact (rms 7.7·\(10^{-5}\), \(\chi^2\) 12.9486 against 12.94), with 50 window-edge points frozen.
  • YVO3 is frozen (below).
  • The tick marks of a run are its Bragg angles plus the Zero after the cycle (YVO3: 20.4825 + 0.0619 = 20.5444; the refined cell would put them 0.1° off).
  • NdNi-HTO is left out for its magnetic phase; NdNi-LTOa, NdNi-LTOb and Pd3MnD as version 2.
  • fullprof_spacegroups now covers 49 blocks in 37 files (B m a b, P b n m, P c c n, P m -3 m new):
  • it compares Hall symbols as groups ("B m a b": the table's '-B 2ab 2' and FullProf's '-B 2bc 2' differ by a centring translation);
  • it skips the magnetic phase's block, which has no symbol.

Listed for Josu (the gate): differences not resolved, explained and frozen in their tests. 1. The peak window. Wdt × (the TCH width without X·tan \(\theta\)) reproduces FullProf's windows only to 0.5% rms (a fit gives 1.013 × that width). Neither a share of X·tan \(\theta\), nor a fraction of a step, nor Wdt·H fits rutile, MgAl2O4, LaB6 and YVO3 together. The points just outside the core's window are frozen:

  • rutile + anatase: 106 up to 0.082;
  • MgAl2O4: 47 up to 0.0136;
  • LaB6: 50 up to 0.407;
  • YVO3: 4006 up to 0.202. 2. YVO3. X = 0.645 makes FullProf's windows several times wider than that rule and narrower than Wdt·H. Frozen at rms 2.35·\(10^{-2}\), max 0.194, |\(\Delta\)\(\chi^2\)| 0.0105 (2.3001 against 2.29). 3. Y2O3's pseudo-Voigt η. It follows η0 + X·2\(\theta\) with a slope 7·\(10^{-5}\) below the X read, up to 4·\(10^{-5}\) at 145°; the profile is not affected. Frozen at 1.89 of its tolerance in fullprof_microstructure. 4. The Egyptian make-up's CORR. Off by 6% to a factor 17 from m·Lp·A, while its profile agrees to 1.6·\(10^{-5}\), so it is not the factor applied; its meaning there is not known. 5. Not implemented: magnetic structure factors (NdNi-HTO's profile is not compared) and the de Keijser "beta" column of the .mic.

L0372026-10-09decision

0.7.0 reviewed and merged; the gate rule in CLAUDE.md; the window-edge points frozen in their test

The work of L035 and L036 was reviewed on the Mac before it was merged into main:

  • All 52 tests pass and clippy reports no warnings. The profile index was regenerated by its script: the same. The round did not change manuals/.
  • L035's finding about the FullProf runs (L034) is right and corrects them: asked for NCY = 0, FullProf still ran one cycle in every run ("Number of cycles: 1") and rewrote CODE_calc.pcr after it (anatase's Ti Biso \(0.538 \to 0.486\)). The outputs of CODE_calc belong to CODE.pcr. manuals/fullprof/runs/README.md and run_fullprof.py said otherwise and are corrected.
  • The window-edge points that L035 keeps apart (49 for MgAl2O4 up to 1.35·\(10^{-2}\), 181 for rutile + anatase up to 8.15·\(10^{-2}\)) were only printed by fullprof_profiles, so a change that made them more or worse would have passed. They are now frozen: the test fails if their count or their largest deviation grows. Checked by lowering the rutile count to 180, which fails.

Decided with Josu on the same day, and written into CLAUDE.md: nothing is built on an unexplained difference with FullProf. A step starts only when the tests below it pass; a difference that cannot be resolved is explained with evidence, counted and frozen in its test, and listed for Josu to accept; a tolerance is never widened to make a test pass. The least-squares engine (phase 2, step 4) starts only when fullprof_profiles passes on the five runs. Also in CLAUDE.md: the generated tables in core/src are not run through cargo fmt, so that they stay byte for byte as their scripts write them.

L036, the cloud session's decision to follow FullProf's output where it departs from its manual and to keep the two unresolved rules apart, is presented to Josu for acceptance.

L0362026-10-09decision

FullProf rules found only in its output are taken as found, and the unresolved ones are kept apart

Three things the core now does as FullProf does, not as its manual says: the sign and the F_b of the Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry, the width the peak window is counted in, and the reflections generated past the data. Each is measured on FullProf's runs and stated in the code with its evidence.

Two rules were not found:

  • The reflection limit past Thmax. The tests take FullProf's printed S(1/d)max.
  • The last 0.06–2.7% of the window when X ≠ 0. The test reports those points apart and keeps the exact tolerance on the rest.

Reason: the aim is FullProf's numbers, and these rules can only be read from them. Keeping the unresolved points apart, counted and bounded, shows them instead of hiding them in a looser tolerance.

Set aside:

  • keeping the manual's formulas and widening the tolerances (Y2O3 would pass at 0.5, which tests nothing);
  • asking FullProf's author: possible later, but no answer is needed to go on.

L0352026-10-09work

Phase 1 closed against FullProf's own structure factors; the pattern differences of L034 found, one by one

Which .pcr the runs belong to. Asked for NCY = 0, FullProf still runs one cycle: CODE_calc.out says "Number of cycles: 1", and the shifted values are written back into CODE_calc.pcr. The .hkl, .prf and the R-factors of CODE_calc belong to the values FullProf read, those of CODE.pcr.

  • Evidence: with CODE_calc.pcr, anatase's F differ by up to 1.8% at high angle. The .out shows Ti's Biso moving from 0.538 to 0.486 in that cycle. With CODE.pcr, every F agrees to the print.
  • The same may be behind LaMnO3ref.prf (L033): a .pcr written after the cycle its .prf was drawn before. Not checked: the earlier state is not in the material.
  • Both tests read CODE.pcr, and the generator says why.

Structure factors (phase 1). The new test fullprof_reference_sf compares F, real and imaginary, with FullProf's CODE_calc.hkl (Hkl = −3, conventional cell). It covers all 11 phases of the five runs, 1992 reflections.

  • All agree within a bound derived from FullProf's print (half a unit of the fourth decimal) and its single precision. The phase 2πh·x carries the rounding of x times |h|+|k|+|l|, and f, Occ, T a few units of 2⁻²⁴.
  • The worst reflection is at 0.50 of the bound: Co-YBCO, where |ΔF| = 5·\(10^{-5}\) is the print.
  • The largest differences, 8·\(10^{-4}\) on |F| \(\approx 55\), are in the Pb phases of the Egyptian make-up, with |h|+|k|+|l| up to 15.
  • Phase 1 is done: the reflections (L028) and the structure factors of the four reference structures agree with FullProf's own output from the command line.

The pattern differences of L034. Against CODE_calc.prf, rebuilt from CODE.pcr, each cause was found from the files and checked on every run.

  • Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry (Y2O3: up to 0.48 near 20.7°, rms 1.7·\(10^{-2}\)).
  • FullProf does not compute eq. 3.29 (manual p. 54) as printed. The core's peaks leaned the wrong way.
  • With z of the opposite sign and F_b = (4z\(^{2}\) − 3)·F_a, in place of 2(2z\(^{2}\) − 3)·F_a, Y2O3 agrees to its print (rms 7·\(10^{-5}\)).
  • The same two changes were inferred from FullProf's output by the EDI project (enhantica/edi, commit 855a426, pd-neut-cwl_PbSO4_beba-asymmetry.py). CrysFML has no such routine.
  • P3 and P4 are not checked: all runs have them at zero.
  • Reflections past the data. Wdt = 40 lets Y2O3's reflections beyond 145.9° reach into the pattern, by up to 1.6% at the end.
  • FullProf generates, per phase, up to the S(1/d)max its .out prints, and nothing below Thmin (S(1/d)min is Thmin exactly, in every run).
  • The limit grows with the phase's own sample broadening. The two Egyptian phases with none stop exactly at Thmax.
  • Its rule was not found: no single multiple of any width fits the 11 phases.
  • The core takes the limit as Phase::reflection_limit, which the tests read from the .out. Unset, the core goes 5° past the data.
  • The peak window (rutile + anatase up to 0.18, MgAl2O4 up to 0.033). The manual (p. 75) says the profile is zero beyond Wdt × FWHM. The window edges show something else:
  • Each peak's window was fitted against the core's untruncated peaks.
  • For TCH, FullProf counts the window in a width that leaves out the phase's Lorentzian strain term X·tan \(\theta\). For 11 rutile reflections, windows of 0.525° to 1.244° agree with that width to 0.003°.
  • MgAl2O4, whose only Lorentzian term is X, gets Wdt·H_G. The Egyptian make-up and Co-YBCO (X = 0) get the whole FWHM.
  • The window is symmetric about 2\(\theta\)_h also for the Finger–Cox–Jephcoat profile. The core had extended it to CrysFML's emin.
  • Not resolved: where X ≠ 0, FullProf's window is still wider than this by 0.06% (rutile 110) to 2.7% (anatase 101). A share of X·tan \(\theta\), or a fixed part of a step, does not fit all the edges.
  • The test reports those points apart: 181 for rutile + anatase and 49 for MgAl2O4, all within 3% of the window plus one step beyond its edge. The exact tolerance holds on all the others.
  • Excluded regions. A point is excluded when a step from it, to the next point or from the previous one, reaches into the region. Co-YBCO gives the background alone at 5.019°, after a region ending at 5.00°.
  • Checked and not a cause: S_L and D_L of Co-YBCO (D_L = 0) are read as CrysFML reads them. The other readings give \(\chi^2\) of 95 and 467.

Result, Ycal relative to FullProf beyond its print (rms, max):

| Run | rms | max | |---|---|---| | Y2O3 | 7·\(10^{-5}\) | 1.4·\(10^{-3}\) | | Co-YBCO | 4.8·\(10^{-4}\) | 3.9·\(10^{-3}\) | | MgAl2O4 | 2·\(10^{-5}\) | 1.9·\(10^{-4}\) | | rutile + anatase | 9·\(10^{-5}\) | 1.1·\(10^{-3}\) | | Egyptian make-up | 1.6·\(10^{-5}\) | 4.8·\(10^{-4}\) |

In all five Backg agrees to 5·\(10^{-7}\) and every reflection position to 2.4·\(10^{-4}\)°. The five runs are in fullprof_profiles as exact, and their pass-1 .prf files are left out with the reason. The test now measures deviations beyond FullProf's print, which Y2O3's one decimal on counts near 18 requires. The earlier files still pass.

\(\chi^2\) of L033, against the runs. The core's \(\chi^2\) over FullProf's N − P + C, against FullProf's own in CODE_calc.out, is now checked in fullprof_profiles (tolerance print + 0.15%):

| Run | core | FullProf | |---|---|---| | Y2O3 | 1.1986 | 1.199 | | Co-YBCO | 1.5484 | 1.548 | | MgAl2O4 | 1.3813 | 1.381 | | rutile + anatase | 1.3737 | 1.372 | | Egyptian make-up | 4.1442 | 4.145 |

The rutile + anatase gap is the window edges.

  • Of the gaps in L033's table, only Y2O3's (1.31 against 1.20) was the core's: the asymmetry.
  • Cocus-asym, MgAl2O4s and rutana differed because their headers belong to earlier states.
  • Egypt in L033 was another file (Egypt_qa_database.pcr). Its Rietveld run agrees.
  • Cocus-firstpeak now gives 12.2 (16.6 before the asymmetry fix) against its header's 3.63. It has no run, so it stays unexplained.

L0342026-10-09work

FullProf run on the Mac for the reference refinements; 0.6.0 reviewed and merged; what the runs show

FullProf (macOS distribution of February 2025, fp2k, x86_64) was installed on the Mac with Josu's authorisation and run on the final .pcr of the reference tutorials (L020): Y2O3, Co-doped YBCO, MgAl2O4 with MgO, rutile with anatase, and the five-phase Egyptian make-up. manuals/fullprof/runs/run_fullprof.py redoes it. Each run has two passes: the refinement as the tutorial runs it, then a calculation only (NCY = 0) from the refined file, whose outputs (CODE_calc.prf, .hkl with Freal and Fimag, .out) belong exactly to the parameters in CODE_calc.pcr. Changes to the tutorials' files are output-only: Hkl = −3, Prf = 3, Rpa = 0 (this FullProf build stops with "syntax error in format" while writing a CIF); a resolution file named by a path on the author's Windows machine is pointed at the local copy. MgAl2O4b_Rietveld.pcr is left out: on its data it gives Rwp 98 % and \(\chi^2\) 590. The distribution also held FullProf-Restraints-RigidBodies.pdf and bond_str.pdf, now in manuals/fullprof/docs/.

0.6.0 (L033) was reviewed before it was merged:

  • All tests pass (51 with the fix below), clippy reports no warnings; fcj_table.rs and the profile index were regenerated from their sources: the same.
  • The new .out files of the runs broke fullprof_spacegroups, which expected exactly 13 files. Relaxed to "at least 13", it found that it looked FullProf's symbols up in the International Tables' order: for "F d -3 m" and "I 41/a m d" that is origin choice 1, where FullProf means choice 2. The test now reads the symbols with SpaceGroup::from_fullprof_symbol (L030) and passes on all 23 files, 35 blocks, 14 groups (F d -3 m, F m -3 m, I a -3, I 41/a m d, P 4/m m m, P 42/m n m, P 4/m b m, I 2/c, P c m n, P m c n and the original four). fullprof_metric passes on all 23 files as it was.

Run against the calculation-only references, the 0.6.0 pattern agrees for Co-YBCO (Ycal rms 1.4·\(10^{-3}\)) and the Egyptian make-up (8.9·\(10^{-4}\)), and differs for Y2O3 (rms 1.7·\(10^{-2}\), up to 0.48 near 20.7°, the 211 region), rutile with anatase (rms 2.2·\(10^{-2}\), up to 0.18 near 26.9°) and MgAl2O4 (rms 3.5·\(10^{-3}\), up to 0.033 near 53°). These are the \(\chi^2\) gaps of L033, now located point by point. They are not yet in the test, which would fail; finding their cause is the next step.

L0332026-10-09work

Phase 2, steps 1 and 2: the .pcr and data readers, and the calculated pattern as FullProf computes it

The core reads FullProf's inputs and calculates the powder pattern from them (core 0.6.0). New modules:

  • pcr: single-pattern .pcr files (COMM format). It reads the job, the flags by the names of their comment lines, wavelengths, background, excluded regions, the refined globals, and phases with atoms (Jbt 0, 2, 4, 6, with betas and symmetry modes). It also reads profile, asymmetry, preferred orientation and absorption, and resolution files. Codewords are kept as written. All 151 tutorial .pcr files are read, and 214 of the 282 files of manuals/fullprof/ in all. The others are multi-pattern (12), time-of-flight (8), Nsc ≠ 0 (5), and magnetic or superspace formats.
  • data: Ins 0, 1, 3, 5, 6, 8 and 10, ported from the Fortran the manual prints (pp. 133–137), with Fortran's blank-ignoring fixed fields kept. Ins 12 (GSAS) follows GSAS-II's G2pwd_fxye.py (commit c13e730). \(\sigma\) and variable steps are kept.
  • profile: Gaussian, Lorentzian, pseudo-Voigt and TCH (manual eqs. 3.8–3.17, pp. 47–48), and Bérar–Baldinozzi (eq. 3.29, p. 54). Finger–Cox–Jephcoat is ported from CrysFML's Prof_Val (CFML_PowderProfiles_Finger.f90, commit 8ed8670) with its Gauss–Legendre tables (fcj_table, generated by make_fcj_table.py).
  • powder: the model built from a .pcr (eqs. 3.1–3.2, p. 41), with:
  • positions with Zero, Sycos and Sysin (pp. 56–57);
  • Caglioti widths with the resolution file's terms added;
  • the overall B (p. 58), preferred orientation (eqs. 3.31–3.32) and absorption (eqs. 3.33–3.34);
  • backgrounds: points, polynomial (eq. 3.4), Chebyshev (Nba = −5) and Fourier (eq. 3.4′);
  • several phases with their scales, and excluded regions;
  • agreement factors, and Poisson simulation (no external crate: a SplitMix64 generator, Knuth's method below a mean of 30 and Hörmann's PTRS above).
  • drs calc <file.pcr> [--data f] [--out f] writes 2\(\theta\), Ycal, Backg (and Yobs, \(\sigma\)). drs simulate writes an XYDATA file with Poisson noise.

Refused with a message: size and strain broadening (the next step of phase 2), TLS, Cry ≠ 0, and magnetic phases.

Test fullprof_profiles: of the 85 .prf files of manuals/fullprof/, 9 are rebuilt from their .pcr. Ycal and Backg are compared point by point, as relative deviation |\(\Delta\)|/max(|Y_FP|, 1):

| File | Class | Ycal rms | Ycal max | |---|---|---|---| | PPH3-sls (Rietveld) | exact | 9.7·\(10^{-5}\) | 4.8·\(10^{-3}\) | | PPH3-sls_1 (profile matching, two settings) | exact | 1.2·\(10^{-4}\) and 1.9·\(10^{-4}\) | 5.8·\(10^{-3}\) | | citric-acid-sls_1 (Rietveld) | exact | 1.4·\(10^{-4}\) | 3.9·\(10^{-3}\) | | citric-acid-sls_1 (profile matching, two settings) | exact | 1.2·\(10^{-4}\) and 2.1·\(10^{-4}\) | 7.0·\(10^{-3}\) | | LaMnO3_cub | exact | 3.7·\(10^{-4}\) | 7.0·\(10^{-3}\) | | LBF_lmopr (Le Bail) | lebail | 2.7·\(10^{-3}\) | 6.1·\(10^{-2}\) | | LaMnO3ref | inconsistent | 1.6·\(10^{-2}\) | 9.7·\(10^{-2}\) |

In every file Backg agrees to 5·\(10^{-4}\) or better, and the reflection positions to 5·\(10^{-4}\)°.

  • LBF_lmopr: the weak reflections differ because the .hkl holds the intensities after the last cycle, while the .prf was drawn with those of the cycle before.
  • LaMnO3ref: its profile behaves as if Zero were about 0.0207, where its .pcr, .out and .prf header give 0.01671, and it differs at high angle. The .out reflection list agrees with the core: Icalc within the test's tolerance and FWHM within 3.4·\(10^{-4}\).
  • LaMnO3_cub: positions are off by up to 4.4·\(10^{-4}\)°, growing with k. FullProf's b is about 1.5·\(10^{-5}\) Å below its .pcr value, as in L028.

The other 76 files are left out:

  • 71 of the LaMnO3 D20 sequence (la0.prf, la0120.prf … la0190.prf), which carry a magnetic phase (Jbt = 1). The 70 per-temperature files still serve as data: their Yobs are read as FullProf read them.
  • 5 from simulated annealing: four list integrated intensities with no profile, and one (Lmo_san_prof.prf) holds the annealing cluster profile.

The observed data of 80 files, 301429 points, agree with the readers (Ins 3, 6 and 10). The index and its reasons are written by make_fullprof_profiles.py.

Found in the files, not in the manual:

  • The .prf Backg column is the background less a quarter of its minimum (LaMnO3_cub prints 11.789 for 15.719).
  • FullProf divides the whole .prf by a power of ten for large counts (citric acid: Yobs 7390 for 73902.4).
  • FullProf's last point carries the background only (PPH3: 4890 where the peaks add 110.25).
  • In excluded regions Ycal is the background. A point is excluded when x \(\le\) b and x + step > a.
  • λ2 adds peaks only when Ratio ≠ 0 and λ2 ≠ λ1 (Cocus-asym, a neutron file with Ratio = 1).
  • X-ray Lp is (1 + Cthm·cos\(^{2}\)2\(\theta\))/(2 sin\(^{2}\)\(\theta\) cos \(\theta\)), twice eq. 3.26 with K = ½. PPH3 (Cthm = 0) agrees exactly. Y2O3 (Cthm = 0.78) gives \(\chi^2\) 1.31 with this formula and 14.4 with the manual's, against FullProf's 1.20.
  • Profile matching takes the CODFILn.hkl intensities without the scale factor.
  • A phase with S_L = D_L = 0 takes the resolution file's ASYM values (rutana: \(\chi^2\) 1.50, against 1.68 before).
  • FCJ applies at all angles below AsyLim, above 90° too.
  • The .out Icalc is the intensity within the ±Wdt·H window, I·(1 − η(1 − (2/π)·atan(2·Wdt))).

Checked only through \(\chi^2\), against the \(\chi^2\) in each tutorial .pcr header (FullProf / core):

| File | FullProf | core | |---|---|---| | Y2O3 | 1.20 | 1.31 | | Cocus-asym | 1.48 | 1.56 | | Cocus-excl | 3.05 | 3.07 | | MgAl2O4b | 1.54 | 1.86 | | MgAl2O4s | 1.52 | 1.45 | | rutana | 1.68 | 1.50 | | YVO3 | 2.33 | 2.50 | | ybfluo | 1.72 | 1.77 | | CaTiO3 | 1.256 | 1.256 | | LaMnO3_cub | 2.63 | 2.66 | | PPH3 | 44.0 | 44.1 |

These cover the Bérar–Baldinozzi asymmetry, Sycos/Sysin, the polynomial background, the Ins 0 and 1 readers and the resolution file. The header \(\chi^2\) is not a firm reference: citric acid reproduces its .prf to \(10^{-4}\), yet its \(\chi^2\) is 57.1 against the header's 68.6. Not explained:

  • Egypt (7.80 against 4.42) and Sr-oxalate (3.20 against 2.09–2.15).
  • Cocus-firstpeak (16.6 against 3.63), whose data and model are those of Cocus-excl (which agrees) apart from asymmetry, Sycos, Zero and widths. Its \(\chi^2\) stays at about 14 above AsyLim, so the asymmetry is not the cause. Sycos variants did not explain it.

Not covered:

  • The material has no .xrdml, .raw or .brml file, so those readers are not written.
  • The GSAS files are time-of-flight data in FP_tests, so the Ins 12 reader is untested against FullProf. No tutorial uses Ins 5 or 8: those readers are checked only by their port from the manual's Fortran.
  • drs simulate is checked by the mean and variance of its Poisson generator (unit test), not against FullProf.
core/src/{pcr,data,profile,powder,fcj_table}.rs · core/tests/fullprof_profiles.rs · core/tests/data/make_fullprof_profiles.py, make_fcj_table.py · FullProf manual pp. 41–58, 65–137 · CrysFML@8ed8670 CFML_PowderProfiles_Finger.f90 · GSAS-II@c13e730 G2pwd_fxye.py
L0322026-10-09decision

Phase 2 written out: the calculated pattern first, then the engine, each step against FullProf

Phase 2 is detailed on the roadmap from the version-1 features of the proposal (L019, L020). It takes everything that computes and refines, in the core and on the command line, in six steps:

  • the inputs: FullProf .pcr import and the data formats of P02;
  • the calculated pattern as FullProf computes it (positions, profiles, asymmetry, resolution file, Lorentz–polarisation, background, several phases, simulation);
  • the sample terms (isotropic size and strain, preferred orientation, absorption);
  • the least-squares engine (named parameters, constraints, limits, restraints, Levenberg–Marquardt with SVD, cycle events, statistics, covariance);
  • Le Bail, quantitative analysis, automatic background points;
  • our own control file and the outputs (CIF, listings, distances, angles, bond valences).

The calculated pattern comes before the engine because it can be checked exactly: the 85 .prf files in manuals/fullprof/ hold FullProf's calculated profile and background point by point. Phase 2 is done when the four reference refinements are reproduced from the command line within FullProf's esd's. Josu authorised installing FullProf on the Mac to run them, which also closes phase 1 (L031). Set aside: starting with the engine on simulated data only (no outside check of the model it fits); the app's features, which wait for phase 3.

L0302026-10-09work

Phase 1: structure factors with FullProf's conventions, checked against 12026 F\(^{2}\) of FullProf and against gemmi

The last item of phase 1, in core 0.5.0.

What the core computes:

  • F(h) = Σ_j Occ_j f_j(s) exp(−B_j \(\mathrm{s}^{2}\)) Σ_g T_jg exp(2πi h·(R_g x_j + t_g)), with s = sinθ/λ. The inner sum runs over all the operators of the group, centring and inversion included. T_jg is the anisotropic factor of the image, with betas R β Rᵀ.
  • Occ is FullProf's. The manual (p. 102) defines it as "chemical occupancy × site multiplicity (can be normalised to the multiplicity of the general position)", so a full site has Occ = m/M, and the sum over all M operators then counts each position once. Conversions to and from chemical occupancy use the site module.
  • X-ray form factors, with f′ and f″, come from CrysFML's tables (FullProf's library, LGPL). The anomalous terms are taken at the anode among Cr, Fe, Cu, Mo and Ag whose Kα1 is nearest to λ; that is CrysFML's rule (Create_Table_fabc_Xray), and FullProf prints the Mo values at λ = 1.001 Å.
  • Neutron scattering lengths are in \(10^{-12}\) cm.
  • The tables are generated by make_scattering_tables.py from CFML_Scattering_Chemical_Tables.f90 (commit 8ed8670): 214 form factors, ions included; f′ and f″ for 98 elements; 108 lengths.
  • A small CIF reader and drs sf (CIF, or symbol + cell + --atom; --xray λ|anode, --neutron) list h k l, mult, d, |F| and F\(^{2}\).
  • SpaceGroup::from_fullprof_symbol reads a symbol as FullProf does. "F d -3 m" and "I 41/a m d" are then origin choice 2, where the International Tables' order, followed so far, put choice 1 first.

Found about FullProf:

  • The F\(^{2}\) it prints (the .hkl column F2 written with Hkl=2, and StrFactor^2 in the .out) is |F|² of the full sum. It includes f′ and f″ and leaves out the overall B.
  • The manual's eq. 3.44 (p. 57) sums over the reduced set and applies NLAT = (LAT·ICEN)² (p. 58). The full sum contains both.
  • The reduced-set |F|² alone does not reproduce the printed values: for PPH3 (0 1 1) it gives 200.6 against 302.597.

Tests (all pass; the core has no external dependency):

  • fullprof_structure_factors: every F\(^{2}\) FullProf printed in the material, 12026 reflections. These are PPH3 (P 21/c, 34 atoms, X-rays, 7538) and citric acid (P 21/c, 21 atoms, 4074), from their .out and .hkl files, and LaMnO3 (P n m a, neutrons, atoms on 4c, 4b, 8d, 4c with Occ 0.5, 0.5, 1, 0.5; 414), from Lmo_san.out.
  • The reference file is written by make_fullprof_structure_factors.py with file and line. The atoms come from rigid bodies and symmetry modes and are printed to only 4–5 decimals. F\(^{2}\) is therefore compared within the uncertainty that this printing carries through F, plus the print of F\(^{2}\) and single precision. The largest differences reach 0.05, 0.25 and 0.37 of that bound.
  • Controls show the test can tell the conventions apart. Without f′ and f″, 4589 of 7538 (PPH3) and 943 of 4074 (citric acid) reflections fall outside the bound. With chemical occupancies in place of m/M, 413 of 414 do (LaMnO3).
  • The same test checks that the core's coefficients equal FullProf's printed ones to the fourth decimal, and that f′ and f″ equal them exactly.
  • gemmi_structure_factors: the four reference structures of L020 as their tutorial .pcr files give them: Y2O3, MgAl2O4, Co-doped YBCO, and rutile, anatase, laurionite, phosgenite, cerussite, galena and gypsum from the two QPA tutorials. That is 10 phases and 1435 reflections with d \(\ge 0.9\) Å, X-rays and neutrons.
  • gemmi 0.7.5 (pip, test-data step only, make_gemmi_reference.py) builds the groups from FullProf's Hall symbols and handles special positions itself. With gemmi's own coefficients the core agrees to 6·\(10^{-8}\) (X-rays; gemmi stores single precision) and 4·\(10^{-10}\) (neutrons) of Σ Occ·M·|f|. The unique reflections and the operators are the same.
  • Tables compared entry by entry: of 15 X-ray species and 14 neutron lengths, the only difference is O\(^{2-}\). CrysFML's entry is a two-Gaussian fit (a = 4.758, 3.637; b = 7.831, 30.05; c = 1.594), gemmi's a four-Gaussian one. It changes |F| of rutile and anatase by up to 7·\(10^{-4}\) of that scale. Where each entry comes from was not checked.
  • structure_factor_properties, in all 530 settings (182320 reflections), with an atom on the general position and one on the most special position, anisotropic:
  • F = 0 for the 56205 absent reflections;
  • F(−h) = F(h)* without anomalous terms;
  • Bijvoet differences in each of the 277 non-centrosymmetric settings and in none of the centrosymmetric ones;
  • real F with −1 at the origin;
  • equal |F| for equivalent reflections;
  • the same F as the atoms written out in P 1, which checks the Occ = m/M convention.
  • cif_structures: FullProf's CIFs for PPH3 and LaMnO3 give the structures of its .out files, and an ICSD CIF gives F m -3 m from its operators.

Not in the material: FullProf's own structure factors for the four reference structures. No .hkl or .out file of their tutorials prints F. Josu can produce them on the Mac by setting Hkl = −3 in each tutorial .pcr (manual p. 11: real and imaginary F of the conventional cell) and running FullProf. Until then the phase's "Done when" is met for reflections, while the structure factors of the four rest on gemmi and on FullProf's F\(^{2}\) for three other structures.
